Then she, having given birth to a fish, abandoned the fish form and attained a divine form. The celestial nymph went to the path of the Siddhas and Charanas. That girl, the daughter of the fish, the fish-scented one, may you become the servant of the king Say. Endowed with the essence of beauty, she was adorned with all qualities. But she, named Satyavatī, due to the fish slaughter, was for some time a fish-scented one, smiling with purity. She was engaged in service, and she was in the water. Extremely endowed with beauty, even desired by the Siddhas, the wise one saw her, and the wise one, named Charudāsa, was captivated. The learned sage, the great sage, spoke to her, "O Bhagavān, how can there be a meeting with the sages who are situated in the sky?" Thus addressed by them, the Lord created a mist, by which the land became like a darkened place. The girl, astonished by the mist, was shy and full of spirit. Know me, O Bhagavān, as a girl always following the will of my father.

Page 234

Due to your connection, my sinless maidenhood is tainted. If my maidenhood is also blemished, how can I protect it, O best of the twice-born? I wish to go home, but I am eager to stay, O wise one. Considering this, O Lord, please arrange what follows. Thus addressed, the most revered sage spoke to her, filled with affection: "You will become a maiden." Choose a boon, O timid one, that you desire, O beautiful one. My favor is indeed futile if it is not granted to the pure-smiling one. Thus addressed, she chose the most fragrant body. The Lord, pleased, granted her the boon of a beautiful form. Then, filled with joy and adorned with qualities of womanhood, she went along with the sage. Hence, her name became known as Gandhavati. Due to her fragrance, people on earth would sniff her out. Then, her name became known as Yojanagandha. Even the Lord himself went to his abode after announcing her. Thus, Satyavatī, delighted, received the excellent boon. Joined with Parāśara, she bore a son endowed with virtues. He was born on the banks of the Yamunā, the mighty Parāśara. She approached her mother, as if engaged in penance, and focused her mind. The descent of the ancient lineage has begun.

224 By that curse, Dharma was born in a Shudra womb. The wise one, in the form of a Brahmin, was indeed the sinless one. Saṅjaya, born of the sage, was the charioteer of Gavalgaṇa. Gautama gave birth to a pair, from the arrow of autumn. A mother, Kṛpa, and a great warrior were born. From Drona, the one adorned with blood, Aśvatthāmā was born. Thus, Dhṛtarāṣṭra of great wisdom had a hundred sons; among them, Duryodhana and others were eager for battle. Abhimanyu was born to Subhadrā, the daughter of Vasudeva, and the great-souled Pāṇḍu. From the Pāṇḍavas, five sons were born to Kṛṣṇa, all endowed with beauty and skilled in all arts. From Yudhiṣṭhira, the son of Prativindhya was born; from Bhīma, Vṛkodara; from Arjuna, Śrutakīrti; and from Nakula, Śatānīka. Similarly, from Sahadeva, the valiant Śrutasen was born; and in the forest, Ghaṭotkaca was born to Bhīma and Hidimbā. Śikhaṇḍin was born as a daughter to Drupada, who later became a son; the Yakṣa desired her with great affection. In the battle of the Kurus, countless warriors assembled, ready to fight in the conflict of a hundred thousand kings. Their innumerable names cannot be counted, even in a hundred years. These are the main ones mentioned, to whom this narrative pertains. The descent of the original lineage has begun.

Janamejaya said: Those who are mentioned, O Brahman, and others not mentioned, I wish to hear them properly, as well as the words of other kings. As the great charioteers, born here, O great one, you are worthy to explain that to me. Vaśaṁpāyana said: This is indeed a secret, O king, we have heard about the gods. I shall tell you that, bowing to the Self-born. In ancient times, having created the earth with three-sevenths, Jamadagni performed austerities for the great Indra, the best of the winds. When the earth was created without kṣatriyas, The Brahmanas and kṣatriyas, O king, began to compete. With them, the resolute ones, who had taken vows, In the season of righteousness, not desiring anything false. From them, the kṣatriyas received great strength, Then, O king, kṣatriyas were born, endowed with valor. And also sons and daughters, again for the increase of kṣatriyas. Thus, from the resolute ones, kṣatriyas were born, Endowed with long life, established in righteousness.

228 Ādivaṃśāvataraṇapava At that time, the four Vana became the best of the Bharata. The truthful woman approached, not desiring falsehood; likewise, other beings also, even those born in lower forms. The righteous ones, along with their wives, went forth, O best of the Bharata. Then, due to the heat, the hundreds of thousands of living beings were exhausted; those people, devoted to dharma, were on the earth. Freed from ailments and afflictions, the men were liberated. Then, GPR, the king of elephants, stood firm again in the Kṣatra, with the names of the great warriors. He governed the Kṣatra again, ruling this earth with dharma; the Brahmins and others then rejoiced greatly in the Vana. The kings, dispelling the faults of desire and anger, did not punish with the rod, but rather, with dharma, they guided the people. Thus, in the Kṣatra devoted to dharma, the thousand-eyed and hundred-sacrificer caused the people to flourish in their land and time. At that time, no child was dear to the king, nor did any youth attain the pleasures of life. Thus, with the long-lived ones, O best of the Bharata, this land, extending to the ocean, was filled.

Page 241

In the great sacrifices, the Kṣatriyas are very generous; then the Brāhmaṇas are honored with offerings. At that time, Brāhmaṇas do not sell the Vedas, nor do the Śūdras chant them. They engage in agriculture with cows and other animals; they do not abandon the work of the land. Humans do not milk calves; they do not sell goods that are not properly weighed. The human tigers perform their duties; they follow righteousness and are devoted to their tasks. Those who are engaged in their own work are the rulers among men; thus, at that time, righteousness does not waver. In due time, cows give birth, and women also bear children; trees bear fruits and flowers in the seasons. Thus, in the Kṛta age, when everything was proper, the earth was filled with living beings. Then, in the world that arose, demons were born among the Kṣatriyas, the best of men. At that time, the Devas were defeated in battle by the Asuras, and they also became established on the earth. Here, desiring divinity, the strong among men...

Page 242

230 The beings were born, O Lord, among those asuras. Among the cows and horses, O King, Among the flesh-eating beings, elephants, and animals. Here, the kings of the earth are born, Unable to bear the burden of their own selves. Then the kings, some accompanied by their retinues, The sons of Diti and Danu, thus fell from their positions. Those who were valiant and fierce roamed the earth, This ocean of milk was disturbed by the enemies. The brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas, and vaiśyas, And other beings were tormented by their might. Terrifying and striking, they roamed the earth, Hundreds of thousands of beings. From the hermitage, the great sages, With their strength, were agitated. The non-brāhmaṇas, intoxicated with pride and strength, Thus, the earth was tormented by the might of those asuras. Tormented, the kings approached the brāhmaṇas. Indeed, the wind could not hold the earth, O King, Then, the earth was unable to bear the weight of the asuras. Then the earth, tormented by fear, Sought refuge in the Lord of all beings, the Father of all. The descent of the original lineage began.

Page 243

The great one, surrounded by divine sages, addressed the eternal Brahmā, the sustainer of the worlds, along with Gandharvas and Apsarases, who were firmly established. Then, the earth, adorned with joy, was requested for the purpose of creation by the guardians of the worlds, all of them in the presence of Bhārata. Thus, the self-born one, knowing the duty of that earth, spoke of it as known to the Supreme Being. Why would the creator of the universe not be aware, O Bhārata, of the desires of the gods and demons in their entirety? The great king spoke to the earth, the lord of the earth, the omnipotent one, the lord of all beings, Śambhu, the progenitor. As you have reached me, O earth, for that purpose, I shall appoint all the celestial beings. Having spoken thus, the deity Brahmā, O king, released the earth and then commanded all the celestial beings to create. To relieve the burden of this earth, let it be borne separately by the portions of the earth, and thus it was said here. Similarly, gathering the groups of Gandharvas and Apsarases, the Lord spoke this excellent word.

Page 245

The kings of the gods were born as they desired. The demons and the rakṣasas, as well as the gandharvas and serpents, and many other beings were born. The demons and the rakṣasas, as well as the gandharvas and serpents, were not defeated even in the presence of the best of the Bharatas. Janamejaya said: I wish to hear about the origin of the assemblies of the gods and demons, the gandharvas, and the apsarases, as well as all the humans and all the yakṣas and rakṣasas. I wish to hear the truth about the origin of all living beings. Vairāmpāyana said: Indeed, I will tell you, bowing down to Svayambhu, about the creation and dissolution of the worlds. The sons of Brahmā, known as the six great sages, are Marīci, Aṅgiras, Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu, and Vasiṣṭha. From Marīci, the son of Kaśyapa, these great beings were born, the three daughters of Dakṣa. Aditi, Diti, Danu, Kālī, Anayā, Siṁhikā, the sage, Krodha, Prāva, Ariṣṭā, Vinatā, and Kapilā were also born. Kaduśa, the mother of the tiger, was indeed the daughter of Dakṣa, O Bhārata.

Thus, these divine Gandharvas have been described. Therefore, I will describe other beings, O Bhārata, who are flawless and of the lineage of the beloved Aruṇa. The beautiful Anūpā was thus revealed. The Siddha, Gola, and the great renowned Pṛṇāśa, the celibate, the one with the quality of love, and also the seventh, Suparṇa. Viśvāvasu and Bhānu, and also Suchandra, are the tenth. Thus, these divine Gandharvas have been described. This lineage of the Apsarases, known for its auspicious qualities, was formerly revealed by the great goddess. Alambusā, with mixed hair, and the unblemished Tula, Aruṇā, and also the protected Rambhā, are of that kind, O Manoramā. Asita and Subāhu, and also Suvrata and Subhujā, and Supriyā, and the well-known Atibāhu, and Hahā and Huhū. Tumburu and these four are remembered as the best of the Gandharvas. The nectar, the cows of the Brāhmaṇas, and also the Gandharvas and Apsarases. The offspring of Kapilā has been described in the Purāṇa. Thus, the origin of all beings has been told by me, as it is properly counted, of the Gandharvas and Apsarases.

Page 251

There are many sons of Atri, known as the lord of men. All are knowledgeable in the Vedas, accomplished, and of peaceful disposition, great sages. The demons, the sons of Pulastya, the monkeys, and the Kinnaras, as well as the animals of the Pūtahasa, lions, tigers, and Kimpuruṣas. The sons of the sacrifice are equal to the sacrifice, companions of the insects, renowned in the worlds, devoted to truth. Dakṣa was born from the right thumb of the lord sage, a Brahmin, the protector of the earth, a boon for those with sons. From the left thumb of that great soul, the sage gave birth to five daughters. Those daughters, with lotus-like eyes, were established by the lord of creatures, who had lost his son. By divine arrangement, O king, the thirteenth was born. The names of the righteous are to be known: Kīrtimān, Kīrtitakṣmī, Medhā, Kīrtitakṣmī, Dhṛti, Pūṣṭi, Śraddhā, and Kriyā. Buddhi, Lajjā, and Matī are also the daughters of Dharma; these are the doors of Dharma established by the self-born. The twenty-seven of the moon are well-known in the world; they are aligned with the eyes of time, the moon's daughters.

Page 252

240 Adi-Vamsha-Avatara The yogins of the stars are situated in the journey of the womb. The grandfather is the sage, the god, and the father of Prajāpati. I will describe his eight sons, the Vasus, in detail. Dharu, Dhruva, Soma, Aha, Anila, Anala, Pratyuṣa, and Prabhāsa are remembered as the eight Vasus. The son of Ghoomrāya is Dhara, the knower of Brahman, and Dhruva as well. The moon is from the mind of the powerful, and the breath of the breathers. The son of Rata is Aha, and the son of Śāṇḍilya is Hutaśana. Pratyuṣa and Prabhāsa are remembered as the sons of Prabhātā. The son of Dhara is Draviṇa, and Huta-Havyavaha as well. The son of Dhruva is the Lord Time, the creator of the worlds. The son of Soma is Vaca, the speaker, by whom one is born. The son of Manoharā is Prāṇa, and also Ramana. The son of Aha is remembered as Jyoti, and also as Śrama, the peaceful sage. The foremost son is Kumāra, the illustrious one of the Mandāra forest. His branch is Vidākh, and he is known as Kārtikeya due to the connection with the Kṛttikā stars. The son of Anila is Śivā, the swift one, and the unknown one is also a son of Anila. The son of Pratyuṣa is a sage named Nātha Devala. Devala has two daughters, Kṣamā and Vanta, who are wise.

Page 253

Bṛhaspati's sister is a virtuous woman and a celibate; she, accomplished in yoga, wanders unattached throughout the world. The sister of Prabhāsa is known as Vasūtanāma. The great Vishvakarman was born as the Prajāpati of the Relpa. Among all ornaments, he is the best craftsman who created the divine aerial cars for the deities. Men sustain themselves by the craft of that great soul, whom they worship daily as the imperishable Vishvakarman. After breaking the right breast, the human form of Brahman emerged, bringing forth the Lord Dharma, who bestows happiness. He has three sons, all charming to the minds of all beings: Śama, Kāma, and Harṣa, who uphold the worlds with their brilliance. In the realm of desire, the attainment of Śama is the beloved; Nandī is the abode of Harṣa, where the worlds are established. The son of Marīci, the great one, was born as the king of the gods and demons; he is the source of the worlds. The Tvaṣṭṛ, the bearer of the sun, is the one who creates forms. The great one, who is free from envy, resides in the sky. The sons of Dādada and Vādite are led by Indra, the king of men.

Page 254

242 Where the sorrowful are established, there is the lower aspect of Viṣṇu. These are the three hundred deities; I will explain their lineage and groups. The lower aspect of Rudra, the Maruts, and the Vasus should be known as such. Vinatā's son Garuḍa is powerful, and so is the red one; Bhagavān Bṛhaspati is counted among the Ādityas. O King, know the hidden ones, the medicinal plants, and the animals; this group of deities sings your praises. One who sings is freed from all sins. The Lord Bhṛgu, having pierced the heart of Brahman, emerged; he is the son of Bhṛgu, the poet, the wise one, the son of the poet, the planet. For the purpose of the life journey of the three worlds, the self-born one is appointed and roams around the universe. The great teacher of yoga, endowed with great intelligence, was Bhṛgu; he was also wise among the gods, a celibate, and a vow-keeper. When he was appointed by the mighty one for the welfare of yoga, he produced another son, the unblemished Bhṛgu. Chyavana, the one of radiant austerity, the self-controlled thinker, who, being angry, fell from the mother for liberation, O Bhārata. The descent of the original lineage.

Page 255

Āruṣī, the daughter of Manas, was the wife of the wise one; she gave birth to Mahāyaśa after breaking his thigh. Mahātapa, Mahāteja, a young one endowed with virtues. Ṛcīka's son was Jamadabha, and from Jamadagni, there were four sons of the great soul. Rāma was the most illustrious among them, endowed with incomparable qualities. There were a thousand sons of Jamadagni. Another daughter of a Brahmin, who stands with the characteristics of those who are established with Manu, is the one who is the creator and the ordainer in the world. The goddess Lakṣmī, the auspicious one residing in the lotus, is indeed their sister. From her, the sons are the horses that traverse the sky. The elder sister of Varuṇa, the goddess Śukrā, was born. Know her son as Bala, and also the divine Surā, the delight of the gods. Due to the mutual consumption of the desires of beings, he is known as the destroyer of all beings. From her, the sons of the dreadful ones, who are always engaged in sinful deeds, are born. There is great fear and also the fear of death, the ender of life.

Page 256

Kākī, Śyenī, Bhāsī, Gṛtarāṣṭrī, and Śukī gave birth; Tāmra, the goddess, bore these five, renowned in the world. Kākī gave birth to Ulūkā, Śyenī to Śyena, and Bhāsī produced Bhāsā and Radhā, the lord of people. Gṛtarāṣṭrī gave birth to Hamsa and Kaṭa-hamsa, and all kinds of Chakravāka; she, the illustrious one, bore them. Śukī, the wise in dharma, gave birth to a son like a parrot, endowed with auspicious qualities and pure attributes. Women, even when born of anger, gave birth to self-born beings; also, the deer and the deer-like ones, and the auspiciously named Hari. Mātā Jīmutha gave birth to Śādūlī, a white one, fragrant, endowed with auspicious attributes, and also to Surasā, the illustrious one. All the offspring were deer, the deer-like sons of the best of men; bears and the deer-like ones, and also the deer. Then, the elephant-like Nāga, Bhadramana, was born; the son of that one was the great elephant. The offspring of the Haras and the swift Vanaras were born; indeed, the sons of the horses are called Bhadra. Then, the illustrious one gave birth to Śādūṭī, lions, and tigers, O Bhārata; there is no doubt about the great fortune of all. Then, the Mātā gave birth to the Mātanga offspring, O lord of men.

Page 257

The white elephant named Dīrghāyus was born. Thus, the daughters of the king were born, including Rohiṇī and others. In Rohiṇī, cows were born, and in Gandhavatī, sons of the charioteers were born. From the son of Surasa, the daughter of Anala was born. The powerful son of Aruṇa, named Śyenī, generated great wealth and longevity. O son of Vinatā, the renowned Garuḍa and Aruṇa. Thus, the great Manu, the origin of all beings, is praised as the best among the wise. Hearing this, a person becomes pure and free from sin, attains omniscience, and finds the highest path. Janamejaya said.

Page 258

246 Ādivaṃśāvataraṇapava Of the gods, demons, yakṣas, and also of the rākṣasas, I am the Lord of all beings. I wish to hear about the great souls among humans, The birth and lineage of these beings. Vērāmpāyana said: Among humans, those who are the best, First, I will speak of the demons. Known as Vipracitti, he was a demon, Jarāsandha is known as a human. The son of Diti, who is remembered as Hiraṇyakaripu, He was born in the human realm. Known as Bhaga, he was the brother of Prahlāda, He is known as Śalya, born of the Bāhīka lineage. Anuhāda, the illustrious one, was known as Jaghanyaja, Dhrṣṭaketu is known as a lord among humans. He who is known as a demon among kings, Darva is the one who is known on earth. Bāṣkala, the best of the demons, Bhagadatta is known as a lord among humans. Ayodhiśirā, Aśvarirā, and Ayodhiśaṅku, the mighty, And also the fifth, known as Vegavān.

Page 260

248 Ādivaṃśāvataraṇapava Isṛpā was the most powerful among those demons. Pāpajinna was a king known for his renowned valor on earth. 021 Ekacakra was a great demon known for his fame. Prativindhya was also known and became renowned on earth. 022 Virūpākṣa was a Daitya and a great warrior. Citravarma was known and resided on earth as a king. 023 Hara, the best of the demons, was a valiant one. Suvāstu was his name, and he was born as a human. 024 Ahar was a great luminary, the destroyer of the hundred wings. Bāhīka was a king who became renowned on earth. 025 Nicandra and Candravakra were the best of the demons. Muñjakerā was known and was a prosperous king. 026 Nikumbha, undefeated in battle, was born with great wisdom. Bhūma was remembered as the best of the earth's rulers, the lord of the gods. 027 Jhārabha was a great demon among those deities. Porava was a royal sage who became prominent among men. 028 The second was Śalabha among those demons. Prahāda was a Bāhīka who became a king among men. 029 Candra, the best of the Diti, was like the lord of stars. Ṛṣiko was a royal sage who became the best of kings. 030 Mṛtapā was known as the best of the demons.

Page 261

Know the western king, the best of kings. The great and renowned Mahāsura, known as AeA, became a king on earth. The illustrious Mayūra, a great Mahāsura, became the king known as Viśca. The renowned Supaṇa, thus the lesser one, became known as Kālakiirti. The one known as Candrahante, the foremost of those, became the king named Śunaka. The destroyer of the moon, known as Vināśana, became the king named Jānakin. The long-tongued one, known as ALA, was mentioned as a demon. The renowned Kādirāja became the king of the earth. The one who gave birth to the planet, that one was the lion, Candrākamadana. The one known as Krātha became the king of men. The one who was short-lived among sons, the foremost of demons, became the king named Vikṣara. The second Vikṣara, the great king of men, became known as the king of the Pāṃsura kingdom. The renowned Balavīra, who was the best of demons, became the king known as Pauṇḍramatsya.

Page 262

250 Vṛtra, known as a great demon, was a king named Maṇimān, a royal sage. The one who killed in anger was a demon named Daṇḍa, a king on earth. Another, known as Krodhavadhana, was famous as Daṇḍadhāra, a lord of men. Among the sons of Kālaka, eight kings were born, powerful like lions. In Magadha, there was a glorious king named Jayatsena, a great demon among the eight. The second among them was a glorious king, known as Aparājita. The third was a great king, Mahābāhu, a great demon, known for his strength. The one known as the fourth was a royal sage, famous as Śreṇimāna. The fifth among them was a great demon, known as Mahojā, a great conqueror. The sixth was a wise one, a great demon known as Abhīrura. Abhīrura was known as a royal sage on earth.

Page 263

Samudrasena, the king, was born from them in battle. Renowned, he was knowledgeable about the essence of heat on the earth by the sea. In their time, the great name was Parantapa. He became a king devoted to the welfare of his subjects. A group, known as Krōdhavaśa, was mentioned by the king. Then, the heroes, the kings of the earth, were born. Nandika, Kārṇaveṣṭha, Siddhārtha, Kīṭaka, Suvīra, and Subāhu, and the great hero ASA. Krōdha, the fierce one, the glorious one, and Nīla, the king of the earth. Vīradhāma, Kōravy, and the king of the earth were also known. Dantavakra and Ujaya were also known by name. Rukmī, the king of the Shādūla, and Janamejaya, the king. Āṣāḍha, the wind's speed, and Bhūritejā were also there. Ekalavya, Sumitra, Vāṭadhāna, and Gōmukha. Kārūṣaka, the kings, Kṣemaghūrti, and Śrutāyur, Uddhava, and Vṛhatsena were also there. Kṣemōgrathītha, Kuhara, and the king among Kalingas were renowned. Matimāṁśa, the king of men, and Īśvara were also known. Thus, due to the influence of the group, the royal lineage was established. Born long ago, the great king was Mahākīṭi, the mighty one. He was known as Devakō, the divine king, the lord of the gods.

Page 264

Adivamśāvataraṇapava He was born as the king of men, the chief of the Gandharvas. Know that he was born from the great sage Vṛhaspati, from the lineage of Bhārata, as a part of Droṇa, born from the womb of Bhāradvāja. He, the best among archers, was the most skilled in all weapons, born with great radiance among men. He who is known by the learned as the master of archery and the Vedas, the foremost among them, Droṇa, the slayer of his own kin. From Mahādeva and Antaka, due to desire and anger, the great tormentor was born, who attained unity. Aśvatthāmā, the great hero, the valiant one with lotus-like eyes, was born as a king on earth. The eight Vasus were born in the Ganges as the sons of Śantanu, along with Vasiṣṭha and the sage, by the command of Vāsava. Among them, the foremost was Bhīṣma, the fearless one of the Kurus, wise, a master of the Vedas, and a destroyer of enemies. He who was known as Kṛpa, born from the sage, was a great warrior among the Rudras, born from the assembly of the gods. Know that he was the king of the great charioteers in the Dvāpara age, born as the slayer of enemies.

Page 265

Sātyaki, the one who is united with truth, was born from the Vṛṣṇi lineage. From his side, the enemy of the gods, Maruta, was born. Draupada, the royal sage, also arose from that battle, the best among the human kings in this world, of the lineage of the Sṛbhṛtas. Then know that the one who was made is the king Janādhipa, born from actions that are not to be repeated, the best among the Kṣatriyas. From the group of Marutas, know the one known as the enemy of the gods, the royal sage named Virāṭa, who shines in the other kingdoms. The son of Ariṣṭa, known as Hansa, is renowned; he is the enhancer of the Kuru lineage. Known as Dhṛtarāṣṭra, even from Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana, he is of long arms and great radiance, the king with wise vision. From the anger of his mother, the blind one was born. Here, know the greatly fortunate son, the best among the wise, Vidura, in this world. From the fraction of Kali, the foolish Duryodhana was born, the one who is a great troublemaker among the Kurus. He who is known as the worst among all, the one who caused destruction to the world, is the lowest of men. By whom the great enmity was created, the one who causes destruction to all beings.

Page 266

254 Adi-vaṃśa-avataraṇa All the brothers of Pota were born among men here, the rulers of Duhśāsana and others, all of Kūrava's deeds. Dumukho and Duhsaha, and others who were not born in the proper way, those who are allies of Duryodhana, know them as the sons of Pota, the best of the Bharatas. Know the king as the royal warrior, Yudhiṣṭhira. Bhīma is the seat of Vāta, the king of the gods. The Ashvins are indeed unparalleled in form on earth. Nakula and Sahadeva are charming to behold. He who is known as Suvaca, the illustrious son of Soma, became the son of Abhimanyu, the son of Arjuna. Know the foremost part to be Dhṛṣṭadyumna, the great charioteer, and Shikhaṇḍi, know him as the rakṣasa. The five sons of Draupadī, know them as the best of the Bharatas, are indeed the divine beings. Karna, who was born with a divine armor, know him as the great charioteer, the best part of the sun. He who is known as Narayana, the eternal god of gods, his part among men was the illustrious Aśvattama. The part of the wrathful one is Baladeva, the mighty one, know Sanatkumāra as the great one among kings.

Page 267

Thus, many portions of the divine beings were born from Vasudeva. The group of waters, which I have mentioned, was born from the command of the wine. Sixteen thousand goddesses were born in the human world, under the protection of Nārāyaṇa. The portion of wealth was born for the purpose of pleasure on the earth, from the daughter of Drupada, who was faultless in the middle of the altar. Not too weak, nor very great, with the fragrance of blue lotus, with lotus-like eyes, beautiful, and of dark complexion. Endowed with all auspicious signs, resembling a jewel, she captivated the hearts of five great men. The goddesses of success and determination, the mothers of the five, were Kuntī and Mādri, and the mind was the daughter of Subala. Thus, the descent of portions of the divine and the asuras, as well as the Gandharvas and Apsaras, has been described. Those kings born on earth, who are fierce in battle, great souls of the Yādavas, were born in a noble lineage. Blessed with fame, wealth, longevity, and victory, this descent of portions should be heard without envy. Hearing of the descent of portions of the divine, Gandharvas, and Rākṣasas.

Page 269

No one was a sinner, nor was there fear of hunger or disease in that region. The people, like tigers among men, were engaged in service and enjoyment. The king, free from fear, was prosperous and powerful. The earth was rich and fertile, yielding crops and fruits. A young hero, endowed with extraordinary strength, lifted Mount Mandara. In the battle of bows and maces, he stood firm on the back of an elephant and a horse. He was equal to Vishnu in glory, like the sun in brilliance, and unmatched in strength. The king, being agreeable, was joyful and prosperous. People were aware of the situation and lived there.

Page 270

Vaerāmpāyana said: Once, the mighty-armed one, possessing great strength, went to the deep forest like a horse on the way. With the heroic bearers of the red-hued weapons, the warriors who were like the hands of the great elephant, And the sounds of lions among the warriors, the sounds of the chariots, and the sounds of the great elephants, The mixed sounds of the roaring and the sounds of the trumpets were heard, and the earth trembled. The great palaces, adorned with the beauty of kings, beheld him, the great warrior, who was like the sun. The enemy was like a serpent, and the women there remembered him as a great hero. This man is a tiger among men, possessing extraordinary valor in battle, whose strength in arms does not allow friends to perish. Thus, speaking words of praise, they, with love, showered flowers upon the king. There, being praised by the best of the Brahmins from all sides, he went forth with great joy into the forest, desiring to hunt deer. Thus ends the narration.

Page 271

Sundaramanujagmuṣṭa, the people of the city then, After that, permitted by the king. With a beautiful chariot, the lord of the earth, Filled the earth with a sound that reached the heavens. He, resolute and wise, went to the forest resembling Nandana, Filled with Bilva trees and Kapittha fruits. Difficult to traverse, surrounded by winds, A vast, uninhabited place. Surrounded by a fierce group of animals, Then, the man-tiger, with his retinue, Began to hunt various animals. With arrows reaching far, he struck down the tigers, And pierced them with his arrows. From a distance, the lion among men struck some, And others who approached were cut down. Some with arrows, others with strength, The mighty one, knowledgeable in the art of the mace, Roamed with great prowess. With the sages and the mace in hand, He roamed, striking down the wild animals there. With the king's wondrous valor and the warriors fond of battle, The great beasts of the forest were abandoned.

Page 273

The king entered another forest due to the deer incident. Only one supreme strength, afflicted by hunger and thirst, reached the end of the forest and settled in a great lake. The king, having passed that, reached the excellent hermitage, which generates joy in the mind and is greatly esteemed. Accompanied by the cool breeze, he went to a great lake. With flowering trees, it was indeed filled with sweet sounds raised by the birds. Surrounded by the shade of the flourishing trees, it was adorned with the best of the best. No tree was without flowers, nor was there any fruit or thorns; in that forest, there was no lack of anything. The flowers raised by the birds were indeed very beautiful. Like a garden full of flowers, it was a delightful place. The great bowman entered the excellent forest. There, the flowers were scattered by the wind, and they rained down in a wonderful manner repeatedly. Touched by the sun, the trees there shone with various colored flowers.

Page 274

262 The birds are crying, accompanied by gentle sounds. There, in that region, many adorned with blooming flowers, surrounded by houses, enhancing the joy of the mind. Seeing this, the great radiance became delighted. The forest, adorned with flowering trees from the interwoven branches, shone like the flag of Indra. The fragrant breeze carried the scent of flowers, as if wandering through the forest, yearning. Thus, the king beheld the forest, endowed with qualities, resembling the lofty flag of the beloved, rising from the riverbank. That forest, protected and filled with well-placed birds, was a delightful ashram. Surrounded by various trees, ablaze with fire, it was filled with ascetics and their followers. With many flowers spread out, it shone exceedingly bright, like a great ocean. Around the lovely river, O king, filled with auspiciousness and desires, it was adorned with numerous flocks of birds, a delightful forest for asceticism. There, the gentle deer, having seen the path, attained joy. He too, the illustrious one, approached the ashram.

Page 275

Devalokapratīkāśa, naturally delightful. He saw the river's hermitage, in the sacred waters, like a mother resting there among all living beings. With the banks of the lake filled with the sound of the Chakravāka birds, adorned with the flow of flower foam, And the abode of celestial beings, served by the monkeys. The bank, embellished by the sacred recitations, And the splendid elephant-like trees, served by the great serpents. He then resolved to enter the hermitage associated with the river. At the beautiful shore of the island, adorned by the Narayana place, like the Ganges. He entered the great abode, filled with the sound of intoxicated elephants. Then the lord of men, approaching the renowned Chaitraratha, Spoke exceedingly virtuous and indescribable words. To see the great sage Kaśyapa and the ascetic Kanva. Having arranged the chariots and the assembly of foot soldiers, He spoke to the army at the forest gate. "I will go to see the sage Viraja, the ascetic. Let it be here until my arrival." Then the lord of men, having reached the renowned Nandana.

Page 276

264 Saṃbhavapava The king, suffering from hunger and thirst, abandoned joy and went to Pushkara. The king, accompanied by his minister, went to the best hermitage. Desiring to see the sage, the enemy of austerity. Having seen the hermitage of Brahmaṭoka, filled with various birds and animals. The man-tiger heard the verses of the TETRA, spreading here. The hermitage, adorned with the knowledge of sacrifices and the wisdom of the great souls, shone beautifully. Then, the foremost of the Vedic scholars, approved by the Pugayajnika, recited the verses in order. The illustrious Brahmaṭoka was not heard. The one knowledgeable in the rituals of sacrifices and the science of the Vedas, endowed with wisdom, was present. Skilled in the collection of various statements, adept in the assembly of special bodies, he was engaged in the pursuit of liberation. Having attained the ultimate knowledge of establishment and negation, he was proclaimed by the main speakers of the world.

Page 278

266 She, with dark eyes, looked at the king Duṣyanta and quickly spoke, "Welcome!" while honoring him. After offering a seat and water for washing the feet, she asked the king, "What brings you here, O lord of men?" Having properly seated him, she then smiled and said, "What should be done?" The king then spoke to the sweet-tongued maiden, "I have come to worship the great sage Kaṇva; please tell me where he is." Shakuntalā said: "My father has gone to gather fruits from the hermitage; wait a moment, and you will see him coming here." Vairāmpāyana said: The king, seeing the sage, was told by her, and he beheld her, the beautiful maiden with a charming smile. The king spoke of her, adorned with beauty, youth, and austerity, saying, "She is indeed splendid." "Who are you, O beautiful one, and why have you come to the forest? With such beauty and qualities, from where do you shine?"

Page 279

By the tenth, indeed, O auspicious one, my mind is afflicted by you. I wish to know you; please tell me, O beautiful one. Thus addressed, the maiden spoke this sweetly articulated sentence to the king in that hermitage, laughing. I am considered the daughter of the sage Kṇva, O Duḥṣanta, the daughter of the ascetic, the resolute, and the renowned knower of dharma. Duḥṣanta spoke: O great one, indeed, the blessed one is worshipped with offerings. From conduct, dharma should not waver, O steadfast one. How are you the daughter of that one, born of a noble lineage? My doubt is indeed great; you should dispel it for me. Śakuntalā spoke: As this is the arrival of wine, and as this was not before, Listen, O king, to the truth of how I am the daughter of the sage. A certain sage, having come here, urged me regarding my birth. To him, the Lord spoke; listen to that, O king. Once, indeed, the great ascetic Viśvāmitra was performing great austerities. He greatly tormented Śakra, the lord of the gods. By austerity, this one, of radiant valor, would cast me away from my place; Fearing that, Indra then spoke this to me.

In ancient times, where there was famine, the sage crossed the river, and then the Lord created the name. In that place, the one who was pleased performed the sacrifice; may you protect the moon from fear, O Lord of the gods. Angered by the lineage of the stars, he who possesses the starry wealth, causes the stars to tremble in the direction of Śravaṇa. I am greatly disturbed by the deeds of that one; may you command me, O Lord, so that I am not burned by the angry one. With brilliance, he would shake the earth with his foot; thus, he would shake the great Meru mountain. How can a girl like me, who is endowed with such austerity and is blazing like fire, desire to be like us? How can one like me, with a blazing face like the fire, be desired? Yama, Soma, and the great sages; all the deities and the universal beings, if they are disturbed by him, why should we not be disturbed by him? Thus spoken by you, how can I go near; I, the king of the gods, should think of protection for me; so that I may wander protected for your sake. May the wind grant me my desire to dwell there; may the god reveal the desired one; may my mind be engaged in that work, with your divine grace as a helper.

Page 283

While enjoying as desired, on a certain day, the sage gave birth to Śakuntalā in the forest of Menakā, with the river Malinī flowing nearby in the beautiful Himalayas. Having abandoned her, the daughter of Menakā followed Malinī, then she went to the assembly of the gods. In the lonely forest, the birds surrounded the lion-like tiger, seeing her lying down. The flesh-eating creatures did not harm the young one, the birds protected the daughter of Menakā there. I went and saw her lying in the lonely wilderness, surrounded by birds. After bringing her, I arranged for her to be a daughter. The one who gives life, whose food is provided by the Jharīraka, In due course, the three fathers were mentioned in the righteous decision, and in the forest, she was protected by the birds. The name of this one is Śakuntā, and I have also done this. Thus, know this daughter of mine, my gentle Śakuntalā, and Śakuntalā thinks of me as her father, without blame. This I declare when asked, O great king, know that I am the son of Kaṇva.

Page 285

The self is the friend of the self; the self is the goal of the self. The gift of the self is indeed the highest virtue. In brief, the eight types of marriages are prescribed by dharma: Brahma, divine, Prajāpati, and Asura. Gandharva, Rākṣasa, and Piśāca are considered the eighth. According to them, Manu, the self-born, declared the rules. TARA: Consider the full Brahman. Know the six types of Kṣatriya marriages as virtuous and blameless. For kings, the Rākṣasa marriage is also mentioned. Among the five, the two types of marriages are noted. The Piśāca and Asura marriages should not be performed under any circumstances. By this method, the path of dharma is understood. In Kṣatriya marriages, whether Gandharva or Rākṣasa, there is no doubt about their distinctness or mixture. O beloved, may you fulfill my desires. Śakuntalā said: If this is the path of dharma and if the self is the lord, grant me, O best of the Pauravas, the time for my request, O lord. Truly, let me promise you that I will speak of you in secret.

Page 286

My son, who is to be born to me, will be after you. O Maharaja, this is the truth; please grant me this. If this is indeed so, may there be a union with me. Thus spoke the king, considering her words. Moreover, I will take you to my pure city. As you have said, O well-formed one, this is indeed the truth. Having spoken thus, the king took her, the blameless one, by the hand and dwelled with her. Trusting her, he repeatedly said, "I will send a well-equipped army to you." I will bring you to my pure residence. Thus, having promised her, the king Janamejaya, thinking in his mind, proceeded towards Kanyakubja. What will the blessed one, endowed with austerity, do upon hearing this? Thus contemplating, he entered his own city. After a moment, however, Kanva also arrived at the hermitage, but Shakuntala did not go to her father. Then, knowing her, Kanva, the great ascetic with divine knowledge, spoke to her with joy.

Page 287

You have today, O king, been brought to me by your lineage. The Kṣatriya's marriage with a Gandharva is indeed considered the best. Then the great soul, Duḥṣanta, the best of men, approached the one whom you serve, Śakuntalā. The great soul born in this world is your son, O mighty one, who will enjoy the earth created by the ocean's feet. Moreover, the wheel of that great soul, who is intent on his purpose, will always remain unopposed like a wheel. Then, after washing her feet, she spoke to the resting sage, having placed the burden and fruits nearby. To him, who is my husband, that Duḥṣanta, the best of men, you should grant your favor. I am indeed pleased with him for your sake, O one of excellent qualities; accept the boon that I desire for him. Then, he chose righteousness, as well as the kingdom, for the sake of the welfare of the Pauravas, desiring the good of Duḥṣanta.

Page 289

Sārva-daman, the prince, attained (his goal) with valor and strength. He was indeed a great man, destined for kingship, like Shakuntalā. Knowing this, Kaṇva spoke to Rishyaśṛṅga, "Quickly bring Shakuntalā and her son from the hermitage." Today, he shall be presented with all auspicious signs. A long stay of women does not please relatives; let us not delay. Thus instructed, they all quickly set out, bringing Shakuntalā and her son, accompanied by elephants. Taking the son, the lotus-eyed one, the auspicious one approached Dushyanta. Having approached the king, she entered with her son, like the young sun. After honoring him appropriately, Shakuntalā said, "This son of yours, O king, should be crowned as the future king." Indeed, this son, born of you, O king, is like a divine being. As previously arranged, this is the time you have set.

278 Saṃbhavapava Remember, O great one, the path to the hermitage of Kaṇva. Upon hearing that statement, the king, even while remembering, said, "I do not remember you, O wicked ascetic." I do not recall the connection of desires with you. Go or stay, do as you wish. Freed from service, the noble lady, bashful and thoughtful, stood still, as if struck by sorrow. The fiery-eyed one, with lips quivering, cast a sidelong glance, as if to slay. Taking the form of a house, she was filled with anger, and at that time, she held the gathered radiance of austerity. After contemplating for a moment, afflicted by sorrow, she looked at her husband, angry. Knowing, O great king, why do you speak thus? I do not know, nor am I detached, just like any other mortal. Here, your heart knows the truth of both truth and falsehood. You are indeed a witness to the welfare of the self. He who perceives the self differently, what sin has he not committed, like a thief who steals the self? You think, "I am one," yet you do not know the ancient sage's heart. He who knows the actions of sin, you cause him distress.

Page 291

He thinks that by committing sin, no one will say anything to him. The gods know him, as does the inner man. The sun, moon, wind, fire, earth, water, and Yama. Day and night, and the twilight; they know the conduct of a man. Yama, the son of Vivasvat, drives away the sinful deeds. He is the witness of actions, residing in the heart, and is pleased with the knower of the field. He is not pleased with the wicked man. Yama drives away the sinful deeds of that man. One who belittles himself, thinking otherwise, the gods do not grant him prosperity, even if he is self-sufficient. Do not abandon me, who is devoted to my husband. Why do you not move me, who is present? Why do you treat me like a common person in the assembly? Indeed, I am not a mere shadow; do you not hear me? If you do not fulfill the request of this one who is asking, O Duḥṣanta, you will reap the consequences today. The husband of the wife is born again from him. The poets know that the wife is called 'wife' because of her husband. From the one who has come, a son is born. He saves the ancestors from the lineage of the departed.

Page 292

The son who brings prosperity to his father from hell is called a son, as stated by the self-born. The sister who is skilled in the household, the sister who is fruitful, the sister who is devoted to her husband, the sister who is chaste. The sister is the best friend of a man, the root of the threefold, and a friend of the one who is about to die. Those who are active and engaged in the household are joyful, and those who are endowed with wives are blessed. Friends who speak sweetly arise in solitude, while fathers are engaged in household duties, and mothers are in distress. Even in the forests, a man on his path finds rest; he who is trustworthy finds a higher destination. Even when wandering, the one who is single-minded in devotion to her husband always follows him. The first wife waits for her husband after death, and after the deceased husband, she follows the virtuous. For this reason, O King, the acquisition of a husband is desired, for what one attains in this world and the next is through the husband. A son born of one's own self is called a son; therefore, a man should tread the path of his mother as if it were a thread. The son born of the sister is seen as one's own face.

Page 293

The one who is born is sent to heaven, having performed virtuous deeds. Burning with mental anguish, afflicted by diseases, men are distressed. Even if well-connected, a wise man should not speak unpleasant words, considering love, affection, and righteousness. The Kṣatriya born of oneself is eternal virtue, O Rāma. What power do the sages have over the offspring of Rāma? When the son is tightly bound by the mother's embrace, what more can be expected from the father? You, having attained this son of your desire, why do you belittle him with disdain? Ants carry their own eggs; they do not break them. How can you not carry your own son? Neither the garments, nor Rāma, nor the touch of happiness is the same as the touch of a son. The brāhmaṇa is the best among bipeds, the cow is the best among quadrupeds, and the son is the best among those who are worthy. May this son, dear to you, touch you. There is no touch more pleasurable than that of a son. In three full years, I have become this child, O King, the destroyer of your sorrow.

Page 294

282 Having been struck by the hundred-voiced Vājimedha, Thus spoke the charioteer to me in the sky before. Indeed, having raised the name, they have gone to Rāma's abode out of sorrow; People do not greet the sons of Mūdha. Even in the Vedas, the twice-born speak this mantra; Your son, O king, is also known as the gem of birth. From the heart, you are born from the womb; Your soul is the name of the son, and he lives through the autumn night. For I am indeed dependent on you, and my offspring is also eternal; Therefore, you live, my calf, happily for a hundred autumns. This one is born from your limbs, another man from a man; Like a lotus in the lake, I have a second son. As the sacrificial fire is kindled, so this one born from you is unique; You are the one who has been made. Indeed, having been pursued by the deer, I was approached, O king, by the maiden in my father's hermitage. Uvi, Pūvachitti, and Sahajanyā, along with Menakā; Viśvāchī and Ghṛtācī, the six foremost of the Apsarases. Among them, I, named Menakā, the Apsarā born from the outer womb, Having reached the heavens, gave birth to the world from Viśvāmitra. Born of the pure.

Page 295

She, the celestial nymph Menakā, gave birth to me in the Himalayas. Having been cast away, I now dwell like a forsaken child. What sin did I commit in my previous birth, that I was abandoned by my kin in childhood and now by you? Having been forsaken by you, I shall return to my hermitage. However, I cannot abandon this path, nor my child. Duṣyanta said: I do not recognize you as my son, O Śakuntalā. Who will believe the words of a woman who speaks untruths? Menakā, your mother, is without compassion, having been cast away by the Himalayas like a flower. Your father, a Kṣatriya, is also without compassion, O Śakuntalā. Viśvāmitra, devoted to desire, is outside the fold. Your father is the best among the celestial nymphs, and the great sage. Why do you claim to be of their lineage? This statement is untrustworthy, as it is spoken in anger. Let the wicked ascetic come to me, if he dares. O great sage, you are like a celestial nymph, and Menakā is indeed so. Your son is of great stature, yet he has been brought low by a mere curse. How can he rise again?

Page 297

When one sees a distorted face, he recognizes the other, not the self, as a person. The one endowed with great beauty does not despise anything; the one who speaks sweetly is indeed a charmer here. The foolish, upon hearing the words of men, both auspicious and inauspicious, accepts the inauspicious word like a pig accepts excrement. The wise, upon hearing the words of men, both auspicious and inauspicious, accepts the auspicious word like a swan accepts milk from water. One who speaks ill of others suffers, just as a wicked person is pleased when speaking ill of others. Respectfully greeting the elders, the good ones go to rest; thus, one who is good becomes at peace. Those who live happily are ignorant of faults, while the foolish are burdened by faults. Where the good ones are spoken of by others, they call them thus. Therefore, there is nothing more laughable than a little joke; where the wicked are, the wicked themselves speak. A person who is steadfast in truth and righteousness, even if he is an atheist, fights; what then of the theist? One who belittles his own son, having produced him himself, the gods fear him, and he does not attain the worlds.

Page 298

286 The ancestors indeed said to the son about the establishment of the family lineage: One should not abandon a son who is the best among the worthy. The lord of dreams, having bound five, spoke to Manu about the various letters that arose from the offerings. The sons, who are the bonds of affection for the minds of men, protect the fathers from the hellish births. You, O king, should not abandon your son; while upholding truth and righteousness, O ruler of the earth. O lion among kings, you are not unworthy. Better than this, better than that, better than the four, is the truth, the son of a hundred. A thousand horse sacrifices and truth have been upheld by you; indeed, truth is the most distinguished among the horse sacrifices. The acquisition of all Vedas and the immersion in sacred waters; When speaking truth, O king, it may or may not be equal. There is no dharma greater than truth, nor is there anything superior to truth; Indeed, nothing is more intense than falsehood here. O king, truth is the supreme Brahman, and truth is the ultimate time. Do not abandon the time, O king; may truth be united with you. If you do not believe in falsehood, then you do not believe in yourself. I shall go, for there is no connection with one like you. The pure possibility.

Page 299

In you, O Duṣanta, even without, the son of mine will protect this maiden. Thus spoke the sage Vērāmpāyana. Having said this, Śakuntalā stood firm. Then, in the sky, the disembodied voice spoke to Duṣanta. The priest, the purohita, then covered the sacrificial offering. Well, the son born of the father and mother is indeed he. O Duṣanta, do not abandon Śakuntalā, your son. The son elevates the body, O king, from the death of the man. And you, O Śakuntalā, speak the truth about the bearer of the womb. The wife gives birth to a son, made of her own body in two ways. Therefore, O king, take Śakuntalā as your wife. This is the birth of a son, who is to be abandoned, living as a living being. O Porava, bear the great-souled Śakuntalā, the son of Duṣanta. This is the name you have given, therefore, let your son be named Bharata. Hearing this, King Porava, having been struck by the words of the divine messenger, spoke to the purohita and the ministers. Let those who hear this know the words of the divine messenger. I too know this son of mine.

Page 300

If I were to take this son, there would indeed be doubt in the world; thus, he would not be pure. Then the king, opposing him, joyfully and happily accepted that son from the divine messenger. Embracing him with affection, he fondly held him. Honored and praised by the servants, he was filled with great joy, the king. The righteous one, knowing the dharma, worshipped that mother. The king then spoke to her with comforting words. This relationship has been established in the world through you; therefore, this has been considered by me, O goddess. The world thinks you are connected; this son is indeed established in the kingdom, thus I have considered it. Whatever you have said in anger, dear one, I have taken it as dear to me. Thus, the king, having consoled the beloved queen, worshipped her with clothing and food. Then the king, Dushyanta, named the son Shakuntala and anointed him as the crown prince. Thus, the great soul's wheel began to turn.

Page 301

Shining, divine, unconquered, and the great source of the world's sound. Having defeated the kings, he acted like a speaker. He roamed and attained the supreme fame of the virtuous. The king was a powerful Chakravartin, like the son of the Earth. He performed many great sacrifices like the Marutapati. Kṇva performed a sacrifice for him, like Dakṣa, the great and capable. He obtained the name Śrīmaṅgovitata, having performed the horse sacrifice. In which Kṇva gave a thousand lotuses to Bharata. From Bharata, the Bharati Kīrti, by which this Bharata lineage is known. Others who are also known as Bharata are the Puravas. Indeed, many great kings of divine nature arose from Bharata. Whose countless names are known to all. I will sing of them according to their prominence, O Bharata. The great fortunate ones, divine beings, devoted to truth.

Page 303

The son of Dākṣāyaṇī, who is one of the thirteen, was born to Kaśyapa. The sons of Vivasvat, endowed with strength, include Indra and others. From Vivasvat was born Yama, the lord of the dead. Mātanga, the son of Yama, was also born, O king. From Mātanga was born the wise Manu, the lord. From Manu's lineage, the human race became well-known. From the Brahmins and Kṣatriyas, the human beings emerged. At that time, O king, they were united with external Kṣatriyas. The human beings of the Brahmin class held the Vedas. Vena, Dhṛṣṇu, and others were also born, as well as the eighth Milā. The Pṛṣadhra and others are said to be Kṣatriyas, devoted to their duty. From Nabhāga, the mighty sons of Manu were born. Fifty sons of Manu were born, and others also appeared on earth. Due to mutual distinction, they all perished, as we have heard. From Purūravas, the wise one reached the sky. Thus, she became his mother, and his father, indeed, we have heard.

Page 304

Purūravāha, surrounded by the thirteen seas and islands, is a great hero with a glorious name. In his fury, he engaged in battle with the brāhmaṇas and seized their wealth. Sanatkumāra approached the king from the outer world and then he took him back, but he did not accept him. Then, he was cursed by the great sages and immediately became lost due to the power of greed and intoxication. He, indeed, was a great being residing in the fragrance, along with the earth, and he brought forth actions for the sake of the righteous. Six sons were born to him, including the mighty Ayu. Tṛṭāyu, Vanāyu, and Śrutāyu were among the sons. Nahuṣa, the aged and wise, is said to have sons named Raji and Rambhamana. Nahuṣa, the son of Ayu, is wise and possesses true valor, ruling the earth with great dharma. Nahuṣa struck down the fathers, gods, sages, brāhmaṇas, and the celestial beings, as well as the demons. After killing the band of robbers, he made the sages ride on his back like cattle. He caused the kingship to be established, overpowering the celestial beings. Thus, the pure one was born.

Page 305

In valor, in austerity, and in prowess, Yayāti, the son of Nahusha, generated six sons. Yayāti, the king, was of true valor. He struck the earth with various weapons. With great effort, he always honored his father and the moon. Yayāti, undefeated, had sons of great prowess. From the celestial path, great kings were born, including Jhaminṣṭhā. From the celestial path, Yadu, Tuvansu, and others were born, as well as Purus and Śarmishṭhā. He ruled the subjects with righteousness for many years, and Nahusha, afflicted by old age, became a beautiful woman. The king, overwhelmed by old age, spoke to his sons: "O Yadu, Puru, and others, I wish to enjoy youth with young women." The son, Devayānī, said: "What is your work, O father, that you wish to do in youth?" Yayāti replied: "Let old age take me, and I will enjoy the pleasures of youth."

342 Saṃbhavapava In various forms, many beings exist in the world; under the control of the gods, their efforts are lost. A wise person, having attained that, is not disturbed; considering it is destined, they remain steadfast. 006 If a being experiences happiness or even suffering; it is under divine control, not by their own effort. Therefore, thinking it is destined, a strong person should neither be disturbed nor rejoice at any time. 007 In suffering, they should not be scorched, nor should they rejoice in happiness; a wise person should remain equanimous. Considering it is destined, they should neither be disturbed nor rejoice at any time. 008 In fear, I am not deluded; at any time, there is no mental anguish for me. As the creator arranges for me in the world; thus, I think I will surely be. 009 Born from sweat, born from eggs, and those like serpents, insects, and fish; similarly, the grass and all beings worship their own nature in the destruction of destiny. 010 The transient nature of happiness and suffering; why should I endure suffering? What should I do, and what should I perform to not be scorched? Therefore, I will conquer suffering, being vigilant. 011 HER spoke II Those worlds that are primarily ruled by kings; which you have enjoyed, and how time is; O king, do not hide anything from me, as you are indeed knowledgeable. 012 Yayāti spoke: I am a king here, ruling over the earth; from there, I have attained great victory over the worlds. I remained there for a thousand years; then I approached the supreme world. 013 Then, I reached the beautiful city; extending a hundred yojanas. I remained there for a thousand years; then I approached the supreme world. 014

Page 356

344 Saṃbhavapava What is the reason for the predominance of the Kṛta age; having abandoned the earth, they wander? 001 Yayāti said: The relatives, friends, and kin of a person, when wealth diminishes; abandon the man whose merit has diminished, just as the gods abandon a man of diminished merit. 002 HER said II How do those of diminished merit come to be; my mind is exceedingly disturbed here? What is special about those who go to which abode; that is beyond my understanding. 003 Yayāti said: These men fall into this hell; all the celestial beings are distressed. They, like the Kṛtaghna, are diminished and do not attain growth. 004 Therefore, this action is to be avoided by men; it is wicked and to be condemned. I have told you, O king, if you ask now, what shall I say to you? 005 HER said II When those with sharp teeth are accompanied by age; how do the vultures and the sharp-beaked insects come to be? How do they come to be, how did they become; I do not hear of any other hell. 006 Yayāti said: Having risen from the body, they wander in the earth; these men fall into this hell; they do not see the many insects. 007 Sixty thousand fall in the sky; and eighty in the course of a year.

Page 357

The beings strike the falling one; Bhīma, the mighty, the sharp-toothed demons. The sage spoke: By what means do they strike the falling one; Bhīma, the mighty, the sharp-toothed demons? How do they exist, how do they come to be; how do they become beings in the womb? Yayāti spoke: The essence, the seed, is adorned with flowers and fruits; it is created by that person. That essence, when it falls, is taken by the earth; it approaches the great being there. Plants and herbs enter; water, air, and the earth, as well as the sky. All beings, both four-footed and two-footed, thus become beings in the womb. HER spoke II Another womb is created here; or does it go by its own desire? Being afflicted, the human womb is created; tell me, I seek to understand this. From the body of the being, and the like, who gives the name to the eyes and ears? This truth, tell me all of it, being asked; I consider you, O father, to be the knower of the field. Yayāti spoke: The wind raises the great womb; the dead essence is adorned with the nectar of flowers. There, the essence, having the right to the subtle elements, gradually nourishes the womb here. Born, with limbs taken, a man with six faces; he hears the sound here with his ears. He sees all forms with his eyes.

Page 358

276 Saṃbhavapava By life, one perceives fragrance through the tongue; touch is felt through the skin, and the mind knows the essence. Thus, understand the gathering of the eight; of the great-souled, the life-breath resides in the body. 016 HER spoke II That man who is established, whether he gives or is buried, or is oppressed, having become non-existent, who makes himself aware of the self before that? 017 Yayāti spoke: Having abandoned the body, like a sleeping person, the chief, having performed good and bad deeds, following the wind, he worships the lion among kings. 018 The virtuous go to the virtuous womb; the sinful go to the sinful womb. Insects become sinful; I do not desire greatness. 019 Quadrupeds, bipeds, and six-legged beings; thus, those in the womb are born. This has been declared; why do you ask, O lion among kings? 020 HER spoke II What indeed do the worlds attain by performing austerities, or by knowledge? Tell me, as I have asked, how does one go to the auspicious? 021 Yayāti spoke: Austerity, charity, calmness, and self-control; the essence of compassion for one's own beings. Those who are overwhelmed by darkness are said to be men of the divine. 022 The learned, thinking themselves wise, who with knowledge harm others, they become unworthy; and he does not give that which is not his. 023

Page 360

The possible pure one The one who lives by his own valor, free from sin; a giver, not a cause of distress to others. He who, having attained the ultimate goal, resides in the forest, engaged in regular eating and activities. 004 The one who lives without craft, never in a house; self-controlled, liberated from within. A mendicant who is light, with few words, and wanders in various places. By which, indeed, the worlds that are victorious; desires are conquered, and happiness is attained. Knowing that night, one should strive to be established in the forest. 006 Two Pandavas and others; relatives with their own selves, together twenty-one. The forest-dweller performs good deeds; releasing the elements of his own body in the forest. 007 HER said II How many indeed, O sages, are the silent ones, and how many are there? Please tell us that, for we wish to hear. 008 Yayāti said: For one who dwells in the forest, a village exists behind; or for one who dwells in a village, the forest is behind him. 009 HER said II How indeed, does a village exist behind in the forest? Or for one who dwells in a village, how does the forest exist behind? 010 Yayāti said: One should not use the village, for a forest-dwelling sage will be; thus, when he resides in the forest, a village exists behind. 011

350 Saṃbhavapava Yayāti said: The householder who is unattached, restrained in desires, lives in the village as a beggar, having gone to the better place of those two. One who, having not attained a long life, wanders in a distorted state, if he performs penance, he should wander after that; otherwise, he should perform penance elsewhere. What is cruel is said to be improper; he who serves with a mind of confusion, even a horse is without hope; thus, the king's meditation is the ultimate restraint. HER said II By whom have you been sent today, O king; a young woman adorned with a garland, with sweet words? Where have you come from, in which direction, or is there a place for the king? Yayāti said: This earth, O men, is a hell for one whose merit is diminished; I shall fall into the earth, having spoken thus; the Brahmins and guardians of the world are hurrying me. In the presence of the virtuous, the fall is indeed a descent; all those who are endowed with qualities are gathered. I ask you, do not fall into the fall; if there are sorrows, O king, here with me; if they are filled in the sky or in heaven, I consider you to be the knower of that dharma. Yayāti said:

Page 363

As long as there are cows and horses on the earth; and the animals of the forest and the wind god | These worlds are established in heaven; thus know, O lion among men. I give you those worlds; do not fall into the abyss; those worlds of mine are in heaven, O king. | If they are in the sky or in heaven; quickly overcome the enemies. Yayāti said: There is no one like us, O king; who dwells in the royal assembly. | As the gift is always given to the brāhmaṇas; so I have not given before, O king. TMA: The miser will never live; even if he is a hero, he may also be a coward. | I, who have taken the form of the past, will wander; what is good there, I wonder. Pratadāna said: I ask you, O desirable one; if I have worlds. | If they are in the sky or in heaven; I consider you to be the knower of that dharma. Yayāti said: There are many worlds, O king; even if there is one, there are seven, seven times. | The honey-dripping, ghee-soaked, sorrowless ones; they do not have an end, they declare. Pratadāna said: I give you those worlds; do not fall into the abyss; may those worlds of mine be yours. | If they are in the sky or in heaven; quickly overcome the delusion.

352 Saṃbhavapava Yayāti said: One who desires the same glory as me should not desire the fruits of righteous actions; a king, being a king, should ensure the welfare of his subjects. By the command of the gods, a wise person, having faced calamity, does not blame the king. The righteous path, when followed with awareness, leads to fame; one should not neglect dharma. A mind like mine, which is not foolish, does not desire the wealth of others; what is the use of that which is not done by others? Thus speaking to the king Yayāti, the best of kings, Vasuśmana spoke. Chapter 088 Vasuśmana said: I ask you, O king, about the world beyond; if you are known in the sky, O great soul, I consider you to be the knower of dharma. Yayāti said: Whatever is in the sky, the earth, and the directions; whatever shines with the brilliance of the sun, all beings are established in the heavens; they do not perish, they protect. Vasuśmana said: I give you those who are my subjects; may your sorrows be removed.

Page 365

Yayāti said: I do not remember any false sale; it is futile to hold on to what is not real. What should I do if others are not like me in their actions? Vasumānā said: You should take back what you have given, O king; if the purchase is not pleasing to you. I am not the one to take back, O king; may all beings be yours. Rivira said: I ask you, O Rivira, if I have any possessions here, dear one; If they are filled in the sky or in heaven, I consider you the knower of that dharma. Yayāti said: You do not speak with your heart, O king; desiring what is not yours, you do not stand firm. Thus, the countless worlds in heaven are filled with you; they are resounding with greatness. Rivira said: You should take back those worlds, O king; if the purchase is not pleasing to you. I do not wish to take them back here; wherever you go, you will worship those worlds. Yayāti said: As you are like Indra in your influence, so too are the countless divine beings. Thus today in this world, I do not rejoice in anything else; therefore, I do not delight in the gift.

Page 366

HER said II If you do not honor even one king, we all will go to hell. Yayāti said: If you give, O saints, due to truth and cruelty, I do not desire what was done by me before. HER said II Whose are these five golden chariots that shine like fire? Yayāti said: These five golden chariots will indeed be spoken of by you, O saints, shining like fire. HER said II Get up, O king, take the chariot, and proceed with speed. We too will follow when the time comes. Yayāti said: Now we must go together, we are indeed bound by the sound of the drums. This is our path leading to the abode of the gods. Saṁbhavapava

Page 367

Vaśaṁpāyana said: All the best kings mounted their chariots and, attacking, ascended to the heavens, enveloped by the virtue of Bhāma. I think I am the only one going; my friend Indra is indeed my great soul. Why did this one, who is a friend of the Shibira, go with all speed? Yayāti said: He gave the vehicle until he found the best. This son of Ujjinara is indeed superior to us, Shibi. Charity, penance, truth, and also glory, patience, and endurance; O king, these are not the qualities of a king; they are the qualities of a king who is established in righteousness. Thus, having been deprived of the lowly, Shibi departed with the chariot. Vaśaṁpāyana said: Then the eighth one again asked, "O king, I am curious, tell me about Indra's form; I ask you, O king, tell me the truth; from whom are you, and whose son are you?" What you have done, indeed, is not comparable; in the world, there is no other Kṣatriya or Brāhmaṇa like you. Yayāti said: I am Yayāti, the son of Nahusha; my father is Pururavas, the lord of the heavens. I speak to you about a secret matter; I am the grandson of your grandfather.

Page 371

Bhūmanyu, the best of the Bharatas, was anointed as the prince. Then, from that great king, a son named Vitatha was born. Suhotra, Suhotā, Suhavi, and Suyajus were the sons of Mumanyu of the Mṛcīka lineage. Among them, the eldest, Suhotra, attained the kingdom of the earth. He performed the Rājasūya and Aśvamedha sacrifices with many horses. Suhotra ruled over the entire earth, filled with elephants and horses, abundant and crowded. The earth was indeed burdened by the weight of his greatness, like a heavily laden cart. At that time, Suhotra governed the people justly, and the land was filled with hundreds of thousands of people. The prosperous people shone along with Sahadeva. From Suhotra, the earth's king, were born Eṣvāka, Ajamīda, Sumīda, and Purumīda in Bharata. Ajamiḍha was the best among them, and in that lineage, six others were established. Rikṣa, Dhūmin, and Nīlī were born from the supreme Dhūṣanta, as well as others of the same lineage.

Page 373

The kings of Bharata then offered everything to him. Having presented everything to the sages with proper respect and good words, the king himself chose the auspicious eighth day. Let your priest be devoted to our kingdom. Thus, Vasiṣṭha also spoke to Bharata. Then, it was heard that the entire kingdom of Paurava was under the rule of the great emperor. The land of Bharata was previously ruled by the best of cities, and it was filled with the glory of the earth. Then, having attained the earth, the mighty one rejoiced, like a great sacrifice, with abundant offerings. Then, from the gathering, the Kurus flourished, and the people, knowing dharma, were happy under his rule. By his renowned name, Kurujāṅgala became famous on earth. Kuruśetra became sacred through his great penance. The sage with the colorful chariot, and Janamejaya, renowned, were born to him. The five sons of the mighty one were born. Among them, Parīkṣit was valorous, Abhirāja was glorious, and Shalmalā was mighty.

Page 374

Uccaiḥśravā, Bhadrakāra, and Jitāri are remembered as the eighth. Among these, the renowned ones are: Janamejaya and others, the seven mighty ones. All of them became Parīkṣit’s sons, all knowledgeable in dharma. Kakṣa, Senograsena, and the valiant Citrasenā, Indrasena, Suṣeṇa, and Bhīmasena are also mentioned. Janamejaya’s daughter is known as a great warrior, Dhṛtarāṣṭra is the firstborn, and Pāṇḍavīka as well. Niṣadha, the great one, and Jāmbūnada are strong, Kuṇḍodara, Padāti, and Vasāti are remembered as the eighth. All are devoted to the welfare of all beings. Dhṛtarāṣṭra was a king, and his son was Kuṇḍika, Hastī, Vitaka, Karātha, and Kuṇḍala are the fifth. Haviḥśravā, Indrābha, and Sumanju are also mentioned. Three sons were born to Pratīpa, the best of the Bharatas: Devāpi and Śantanu, both great charioteers. Devāpi, desiring to perform sacrifices, departed, Rāntanu and Bāhīka, both great charioteers. In the lineage of Bharata, many great charioteers were born, Numerous royal ones, endowed with strength, like the gods. Saṃbhavapava.

Page 376

Aditi is the mother of Vivasvat. Vivasvat is the father of Manu. Manu is the father of Ila. Ila is the mother of Purūravas. Purūravas is the father of Ayu. Ayu is the father of Nahusha. Nahusha is the father of Yayati. The daughters of Ushanas are Devayani and the daughter of Vṛṣapavā, named Śarmishṭhā. Here begins the lineage. Devayani and Śarmishṭhā gave birth to children. There, in that lineage, the sons of Purū are Poravas. In the lineage of Purū, there is a name Kausalyā. In her, Janamejaya was born, who conquered the enemies. Viśvajitā entered the forest. Janamejaya indeed performed the sacrifice named Anantā. In her, Prācinvān was born. He conquered the east until the sunrise. Then he became known as Prācinvat. In her, Saṃyāti was born. Saṃyāti indeed gave birth to a daughter named Varāṅgī. In her, Ahaṃpātī was born. Ahaṃpātī indeed gave birth to a daughter named Bhānumatī. In her, Savambhoma was born. The lineage continues.

Page 377

Sāvabhoma indeed defeated and took away Kekeyī and Sunandā. From her was born Jayatsena. Jayatsena indeed gave birth to Arācīna in Vedarbhī. Arācīna also gave birth to Mayādā, like Vedarbhī. Mahābhoma gave birth to Suyajñā through Prāsa. He who is a man of great sacrifice brought forth Ayutanāyi. Thus, he is called Ayutanāyi. Ayutanāyi gave birth to Bhāsā, the daughter of Pṛthuśravas. From her was born Akrodha. Akrodha gave birth to Karṇāḍu in Kālini. From him was born Devātithi. Devātithi gave birth to Mayodā in the Vedehī. From her was born Ṛca. Ṛca indeed gave birth to Sudevā in Aṅge. From her was born a son named Rakṣam. Ṛkṣa gave birth to Jvāla, the daughter of Takṣaka. From her was born a son named Matināra. Matināra indeed performed a sacrifice in Sarasvatī. After the sacrifice, he approached Sarasvatī and married her. From her was born a son named Tansu. Here is the lineage. Tansu gave birth to Matināra's son, and he gave birth to Kalinda, the son of Tansu. Ilin gave birth to many sons through Duhṣanta. Duhṣanta indeed took Shakuntalā, the daughter of Viśvāmitra. From her was born Bharata.

Page 379

In the land of Magadha, Amṛtā was born. From her, Parīkṣit was born. From Suyaśa, Bāhudāma was born. From him, Bhīma was born. Bhīma was born to Kakeiyī, named Sukumārī. From her, Pyāśravā was born. They say he is named Pratīpa. Pratīpa was born to Rovyā, named Sunandā. From her, he produced sons, Devāpi and Śantanu. Devāpi entered the forest as a child. Śantanu became a king. Here is the lineage. Whoever touches the hands of the one who is happy, they say, becomes Śantanu. Thus, he is called Śantanu. Śantanu was born to Bhāgirathī. From him, Devavrata was born. They call him Bhīṣma. Bhīṣma, out of love for his father, married Satyavatī, who is also called Ghandakātī. From her, Kānīna was born. From her, the sons of Śantanu were born. Chitrāṅgada and Vichitravīrya. Chitrāṅgada was killed by a Gandharva at the time of youth. Vichitravīrya became the king. Vichitravīrya married the daughters of Kausalyā and the daughter of Karirāja, named Gambhīrā. Vichitravīrya, without offspring, attained the status of Videha. Then Satyavatī began to think.

368 Possible Purity The lineage of the Duhṣantā is said to be destroyed. | 056 | She contemplated the sage Dvaipāyana. | 057 | He stood before her, asking, "What shall I do?" | 058 | She spoke to him: "Brother, you alone can produce a son with the extraordinary prowess." | 059 | He produced three sons, namely, Pāṇḍu and Vidura. | 060 | There, the son of King Dhṛtarāṣṭra was born in Gāndhāra due to the boon of Apāyana. | 061 | Among the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, four prominent warriors were born: Duhśāsana and Vikarna. | 062 | But Kuntī and Mādrī gave birth to Pāṇḍu's sons. | 063 | Then, while searching for the sage who had gone to the forest, he saw a deer. | 064 | He shot an arrow at it, having been filled with the desire for the taste of the flesh. | 065 | The arrow struck Pāṇḍu. While walking on the path of righteousness, he was struck by the desire for sensual pleasure, and thus, he said, "You too will soon attain this state." | 066 | Pāṇḍu, pale in form, while trying to avoid the curse, did not approach Bhārata. | 067 | He spoke the following words: "I have attained this due to my own fault." | 068 | She spoke to him, "For my sake, produce sons." | 069 | Thus, she produced sons: Dharmarāja Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhīma, and Arjuna. | 070 | The pale Pāṇḍu spoke to her, "This is your duty to produce sons." | 071 | Thus, she was instructed by Kuntī. | 072 | Then, from the horses, Nakula and Sahadeva were produced. | 073 | Mādrī, indeed, gave birth to them, and Pāṇḍu became their father. | 074 |
