Kuntī said: I am the eldest wife, my eldest is the fruit of dharma. O Bhāva, do not abandon me, the daughter of Madrī. I will search for my husband who has gone to the abode of the dead. Arise, and leave this child behind. Māgā said: I will follow my husband who has fled. Indeed, the eldest among desires should not be abandoned. And this one, weakened by desire, is coming to me. How can I cut off his desire? Nor will I remain without distinction among your sons. I will live in this manner, thus I will be. Therefore, O Kuntī, my sons should be treated as your own. This king, desiring me, has gone to the abode of the dead. With the king's body, I too am dead. Make this beloved one well concealed. Let there be no negligence towards the children, and may they be well. Therefore, I will not speak of anything else. Thus spoke the wife of the king.

Page 488

Vardhamānapuradvāramāsasād Yāsvinī Hearing the arrival of thousands of Charanas and sages, a wonder arose among the people of Nāgapura. At the moment the sun rose, all the residents of the city, devoted to dharma, observed the auspicious sight. The assemblies of the Kṣatriyas and the groups of vehicles were gathered, along with the women of the Brāhmaṇas who accompanied them. Thus, there was a great disturbance among the groups of lightning, and none of the wise with dharma consciousness acted. Thus, Bhīṣma, the son of Śantanu, Somadatta, and Bāhika, along with the wise sage Vidura himself. And the goddess Satyavatī, along with the illustrious Kausalyā, surrounded by the royal ladies, and Gāndhārī also. The heirs of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, led by Duryodhana, adorned with a hundred ornaments, took their positions. Having saluted the great sages with their own words of respect, all the Kauravas, along with their priests, entered. Similarly, bowing down to the ground with their heads, they all entered, along with the people of the various janapadas. Understanding the crowd as if it were a great roar, Bhīṣma presented the kingdom and the nation to the great sages.

Page 489

The eldest among them, having risen, with matted hair, knowing the command of the great sage, spoke this. He, the king of the Pāṇḍavas, having abandoned sensual pleasures, went away from here with a hundred peaks. For one who observes celibacy, by divine cause, a son was born to him, Yudhiṣṭhira. Thus, from the powerful one, the great-souled king, Mātariśvā gave birth to a son named Bhīma, the mighty. From Puruhūta, in Kuntī, the one of true valor was born, whose fame will conquer all enemies. But from Mādrī, the best of the Kurus, the two man-lions were born, who stood firm like tigers among horses. While wandering in the forest, always adhering to righteousness, this lineage of Pāṇḍu is renowned. The birth of sons, their growth, and the study of the Vedas, were constantly observed by Pāṇḍu. Having attained the fruit of virtuous conduct, he went to the world of ancestors after seventeen days. Knowing that he had departed, Mādri, having left her life, entered the fire.

Page 490

78 Possible Purity 5. aN She has gone with the body of the son of the Pāṇḍavas. Let whatever work is to be done by him be done thereafter. These bodies of theirs and these sons of theirs are indeed superior. With the same measure, O Parantapa. After the funeral rites are completed, let the great glory of Pāṇḍu, the bearer of the Kuru lineage, be attained. Thus addressed, the Kauravas, the sons of Kuru, were all hidden in an instant with the secret ones. There, hidden again in the form of a Gandharva, the group of sages and siddhas were astonished and went away. 118 Dhṛtarāṣṭra said: Let Vidura perform all the funeral rites of Pāṇḍu. Let the wealth, animals, and various riches be given by Pāṇḍu as desired. And as Kuntī honors the guests, let her not be neglected. Let the sun not see her well-covered.

Page 491

The virtuous king, Pandu, who is worthy of praise, is not to be mourned; he has five heroic sons, comparable to the sons of the gods. Then, Vidura, having spoken thus, along with Bhīṣma, performed the rites for Pandu, who was enveloped in great sorrow. Then, from the city, the offerings of grass and clarified butter were prepared. The fiery Pāṇḍavas were slain by the royal priest. Then, adorned with various ornaments, he was covered with a beautiful canopy. That canopy, adorned like a garland, was surrounded by great wealth, and the ministers, relatives, and friends stood by. The lion-man, adorned with supreme ornaments, was accompanied by the chief of the musicians, well-covered with fine cloth. With white and large umbrellas, and with fans made of peacock feathers, they made a great sound. Taking up many hundreds of weapons, the men fled from the Pāṇḍavas, who were there in their bodies. Then, the auspicious white umbrellas and large ones were raised. The priest, adorned with a beautiful garment, went ahead, shining brightly.

Page 492

Brahmins, Kṣatriyas, courtesans, and Śūdras, countless in number, wept, tormented by grief, and followed the king. This one, having abandoned us, will not go to the eternal abode, having made another his protector, O king of men. All the Pāṇḍavas, along with Bhīṣma and Vidura, cried out in the beautiful forest by the banks of the Ganges. They placed that truthful Shibikā, the wife of Nṛsiṃha, in the chariot of Pāṇḍu. Then, his body, fragrant with all kinds of scents, was bathed with pure water and anointed with the main bath. With the water of the sacred pot, quickly filled with pure water. With sandalwood and the main ingredient, they anointed him with the essence of time and the essence of the lofty. Then, they covered him with a garment, like a lion among men. The man-lion, a great tiger among men, was properly laid to rest. Having been permitted by the officiating priest, he was engaged in the funeral rites, anointed with ghee, along with his own ornaments. With the mixture of the lofty lotus and fragrant sandalwood, and with various other scents, they burned him with a gentle fire. The pure essence.

Page 493

Then, having entered the bodies of both of them, the illusion of SBT took hold. "Alas, my son!" cried Kausalyā, and she fell suddenly to the ground. Seeing his mother fallen, the people of the city and the countryside wept loudly, along with the king's loyal subjects, who were accompanied by Kṛpa. Exhausted by the sound of the wind, all beings, including humans and those in lower forms of life, cried out. Thus, Bhīṣma, the son of Śantanu, and Vidura, the wise one, along with all the Kauravas, were deeply pained. Then, Bhīṣma, Vidura, and the king, along with their relatives, performed the water rites, as did all the women of the Kuru clan. Having taken the ashes of the Pandavas, the hundreds of followers of their command, by nature, began to pour out the water. Just as the Pandavas slept on the ground with their relatives, so did the citizens, the king, and the brāhmaṇas. That city, which was devoid of joy and health, became like a great ocean of sorrow for the Pandavas.

Page 494

The sage said: Then the king and the mighty Bhīṣma, along with their relatives, performed the śrāddha for the Pāṇḍavas, which was filled with the essence of their own nectar. After feeding the prominent Kauravas and many others, they also gave to the village leaders. Then the citizens of Vāraṇāvatī took the Pāṇḍavas, the best of the Bharatas, into the city. The people, always remembering the great Bharata, mourned as if they had lost their own kin. At the end of the śrāddha, seeing that distressed person, Vyāsa spoke to the grieving mother. The times of happiness have passed, and the dreadful times have arrived; each day is worse than the previous one for the world. Overwhelmed by various troubles and filled with many faults, a terrible calamity will arise due to the neglect of righteous actions. Go, taking to renunciation, and stay in the forest of austerities; do not witness the terrible fate of this family. Thus, having received permission, she set out, O Sūshāmā, for the son of Ambikā, who is indeed a great Bharata. The birth of the pure.

Page 496

When the fruits of the trees are being plucked, Bhīma shakes the tree with his foot. Struck by the force of the blow, the branches, filled with fruits, fall down. The young ones, not engaged in battle, did not move at all. Thus, Bhīma, striving against the Dhārtarāṣṭras, stood firmly, without any treacherous intent. Then, the renowned Dhātara, knowing Bhīma's strength, became filled with wicked thoughts. From the sinful thoughts of this wicked one, a desire arose due to greed. This Bhīma, the son of Kuntī, is the strongest among the mighty. Let him be restrained by the eldest, Yudhishthira, among the sons of Pāṇḍu. Thus, having made this wicked determination, Duryodhana then constantly plotted against the great Bhīma. Then, for the purpose of water sports, he arranged various grand and colorful games.

Page 497

The place of the proof is approached by someone, and at the end of the game, all of them, adorned with their own ornaments, slowly enjoyed the abundant food. At the end of the day, exhausted after their play, the heroes took rest in the pleasure houses. However, distressed by excessive exercise, the mighty one, having carried the young ones who had come to play, the one seeking residence in the place of proof fell asleep there. Feeling cold, having gathered his clothes, the exhausted Pandava, intoxicated and bewildered, lay motionless like a dead man. Then, Duryodhana, bound by the desire, caused the deep, swift water of Bhīma to fall. Then, awakened, the son of Kuntī, having cut all bonds, rose from the water and struck Bhīma, the best of warriors. And also, having been asleep, he was bitten by the sharp fangs of the great poison. Angered, they bit him, and all the flesh was struck down. The teeth of the biting ones fell upon him, but they did not pierce his skin due to the thickness of his broad chest. Awakened, Bhīma struck all of them with his fists, and he also struck his charioteer, his beloved, with that blow.

Page 499

As his intellect was awakened, the one who causes pain to enemies | They approached as the Vedic scholars, those who speak of Brahman through austerity | Thus, he, endowed with austerity, obtained all the streams | Due to the greatness of the Vedas and extensive austerity, the sage Gautama caused the king of the gods to suffer | Then, the lord of the gods sent a celestial maiden named Jālapadī, saying, "O ascetic, do this for him" | Approaching the beautiful hermitage by the autumn stream, the girl with the bow frightened Gautama | He, having one garment, became like an apsara in the forest, and in the world, he became unmatched in beauty | Indeed, as his bow and arrows fell from his hands, he trembled, and his body became weak | He, being the most knowledgeable and through the connection of austerity, stood firm, the great sage, with supreme strength | Suddenly, O king, a transformation occurred in him, and thus, his seed was not understood | Leaving that hermitage and the celestial maiden, the sage went, and his seed fell on the arrow-stump | And that which fell on the arrow-stump became twofold, and thus, a pair was born to Gautama.

Page 500

While hunting, a certain warrior of King Śantanu encountered a pair in the forest. The bow and arrows, as well as the black deer skins, were prepared for the archery expert. He then presented the pair to the king, accompanied by Kṛpa. Taking the pair, he came home, saying, "These are my sons." Then he performed the necessary rituals and ceremonies. Gautama, having arrived, also became involved. Due to the bond formed by the two boys, the king established their lineage. Gautama, being there, found the truth through austerity. Upon arriving, I revealed my lineage and other details. Various types of archery were described in four divisions. With this, I revealed all the secrets. Soon after, he attained the status of a great teacher. Then all the great charioteers, including the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra and the mighty Pāṇḍavas, went to learn archery. The Kauravas and other kings from various regions also gathered. The end of the story.

Page 501

Bhīṣma spoke. Then Bhīṣma, with great anger, addressed the sons of Pāṇḍu. The great sage Bharadvāja, while performing a sacrifice, saw the celestial nymphs directly. The wind, having arisen, carried away her garment. Then the sage Droṇa took hold of her essence. In that moment, a great battle arose, and he, the wise one, taught the Vedas and their branches completely. The illustrious Bharadvāja, having been honored, then offered the fire oblation to the best of the strong. That sage, knowing the truth, then honored the best of the Bharatas. There was a king named Pṛṣata, a friend of Bharadvāja. His son was named Drupada.

Page 502

He regularly went to the hermitage and played with Drona, engaging in study as well. Then, after some time, King Drupada emerged, a great warrior among men. Bharadvāja also ascended to heaven at that time, and then, due to attachment to his son, the highly renowned one was appointed by his ancestors. Then Drona found Kripi, the sister of Jarādvatī. He who is always engaged in righteousness and self-control, and is devoted to the good, is indeed Ashvatthama. As soon as he was born, a horse was born as well. Hearing the sound of the horse, he who is in the sky spoke. As the horse went to the place of the river, this boy Ashvatthama will be known by that name. With that son, Bharadvāja was very pleased, and there, he became a master of the Vedas. He heard of the great soul, the son of Jamadagni, who was a great warrior, and he was surrounded by Brahmins. Then Bharadvāja spoke to Rama, who was about to set out into the forest, saying, "Know that Drona is coming, desiring wealth." Rama said, "It is possible."

Page 503

I possess gold and whatever else is not known. Everything has been given by me to the Brahmanas, O ascetic. Thus, this earth is a goddess, bordered by the ocean, and I have given the city of Malinī for the purpose of work. Today, I have only this much to offer. Various excellent weapons, O Drona, what shall I give you? Speak quickly. Drona said: All my weapons are complete, with their collections and secrets, to give to you, O great one. Vyāsa said: Thus spoken, the sage Bhārgava then gave him the weapons, along with the secret vow and the science of archery, in anger. Receiving all of that, the best of the twice-born, pleased with his dear friend, went to Drupada.

Page 504

492 Saṃbhavapava Then, having approached Dṛupada, the illustrious Bhāradvāja spoke to the king, his friend, "Know me." Draupadā said: "Your wisdom is not appropriate for this; you have not made me your friend in a fitting manner." Indeed, among kings, no one becomes friends with a low-minded person who is devoid of glory and wealth. Let them decay over time; may you have previously been bound by strength. In this world, friendship is never seen with the imperishable; desire plays and anger drives it. Thus, let us establish a new friendship; you have been my bond of wealth, O best of the twice-born. Neither the poor nor the wise have a friend; what will remain of friendship if it is not based on prior bonds? Friendship and marriage are not between those who have equal wealth and lineage, but between those who are prosperous and impoverished. Neither the unlearned has a friend in the learned, nor the charioteer in the king; what will remain of friendship in the king's garage? Vairāmpāyana said:

Page 505

Draupadī thus addressed the mighty Bhāradvāja. He contemplated for a moment, filled with anger. Having firmly resolved in his mind, the wise one went to the city of the Kurus, called Nāgasāhya. The princes, having emerged, gathered at the place of the elephant, joyfully playing there. That Vīṭā fell into the well while they were playing, and they did not return to rescue her. Then Drona, having performed the PARKA, spoke slowly with cunning. Alas, how lamentable is the strength of the Kṣatriyas! Woe to you who have failed to rescue Vīṭā, born in the lineage of Bharata! This fist of mine, empowered by the mantra of the weapon, behold its valor, for there is none like it. I shall know Vīṭā through the mantra of the weapon, and through another, I shall unite with her in my grasp. Then the princes beheld her, astonished and with eyes wide open, having discovered the Vīṭā, they approached the Vīṭā-keeper. We salute you, O Brahman; this is not known to others. Who are you? What do we know of you? What shall we do?

Page 506

Drona said: "Please tell me about Bhīṣma's form and qualities. He is indeed very wise and will soon take charge." Vaśampāyana said: "Thus, all of them replied to Bhīṣma, our grandfather, saying that the words of the Brahman are true, and that action is like a curse." Hearing the words of the princes, Bhīṣma recognized Drona, thinking, "He is indeed a capable teacher." Then, having brought him there, Bhīṣma, the best among those wielding bows, respectfully inquired of him. In response to the reason for his arrival, Drona conveyed everything. "From the great sage's neck, I have come here, O infallible one, to learn the science of archery." The celibate, self-controlled, and matted-haired one, remained there for a long time, desiring to learn archery. The son of the king of Pañcāla, Yajñasena, very powerful, was engaged in the service of the teacher, being attentive with me. He was my friend there and was also helpful and dear to me. Thus, I remained happily associated with him for a long time. In childhood, we studied together.

496 Saṃbhavapava Thus addressed by Dronapāla, Bhīṣma, filled with anger, went to the Kurus, endowed with virtues. Bhīṣma received him along with the sons of Pāṇḍu, taking the grandsons and various Vasu deities. He gave the king the name Rīṣya according to the prescribed method, and he, the great archer, received the Koravas. Receiving them, Drona spoke the following words: "One who is truly devoted and has performed the necessary rites." "What is my task? Please tell me, as I am surrounded by many." "Those who have been armed, let them speak the truth." Hearing this, the Koravas, desiring to rule the world, all pledged their allegiance to the great conqueror. Then, embracing the unyielding one repeatedly with joy, he wept with delight. Then Drona took various weapons from the sons of Pāṇḍu, both divine and human, endowed with valor. The royal sons and others, gathering together, approached Drona, the best of the twice-born, with half of the weapons. The Vṛṣṇis and the Andhakas, as well as various other kings, came together. The son of the charioteer, Rādhā, also went to Drona at that time.

Page 510

Drona, having seen Arjuna, once again engaged in battle with chariots and elephants, and even on the ground, displayed his martial skills. In the mace fight, he demonstrated his prowess with the tomara and prāsa weapons, and in the fierce battles, he challenged the Pandavas. The kings and princes, witnessing his skill, gathered in thousands. Then, the son of the Nishada king, with a golden bow, approached the great king Drona. He did not seize him, thinking him to be a Nishada, and observed them with a keen eye. But the son of Drona, having reached the forest, struck Drona on the head. At that time, firmly established in the teacher's conduct, he engaged in archery with great discipline. With utmost faith and through supreme yoga, he attained the lightness of being, leading to liberation. Then, once permitted by Drona, the Kurus and Pandavas, skilled in hunting, set out. There, a man, with some equipment, went alone, taking a dog with him to the Pandavas. As they wandered there, they desired to achieve various things.

Page 511

The dog, foolish, went to Nishada in the forest. Seeing Krishna, whose body was smeared with filth, wearing a black deer skin in the forest, the dog barked and stood near him. Then, the dog, barking, released seven arrows from its mouth, as if it had let go of tenfold lightness. That dog, having approached the Pandavas, the heroes, caused them great astonishment. The lightness and sharpness of sound, then, protected it, and the bashful ones praised it. Then, searching for it, the Pandavas, residing in the forest, saw it, O King, constantly releasing arrows. They did not recognize it then, in that distorted form. Then they asked it, "Who are you, and whose path do you follow?" Ekala vyasa said: "O hero, son of the lord of Nishada, the one with the golden bow, Drona's disciple, I am the one who has been burdened by the weight of wealth." Hiranyapayana said: Knowing him thus, the Pandavas returned and told Drona everything that had happened. O King, remembering the one who was to be remembered, the son of Kunti.

Page 512

Upon meeting Drona, I spoke this affectionate word: "Having embraced you, I shall not be separated from you." Then why should I, who am strong, be less than anyone in the world? There is another disciple of yours, the son of the chief of the Nishadas. After contemplating for a moment, Drona made a decision, Taking Savyasāchin, he went towards the Nishada. Ales, with his body smeared with mud, Was constantly holding a bow, like a dense cloud. However, Ekalaavya, approaching Drona, Humbly bowed down to the ground. After worshipping Drona properly, the son of the Nishada, Having introduced himself, stood with folded hands before him. Then Drona spoke this word to the king: "If you are my disciple, let the fee be grass." Hearing this, Ekalaavya, pleased, spoke: "What shall I give, O Lord? Let my Guru command me." Indeed, I have nothing to give to my Guru, the knower of Brahman. He said, "Let the right hand be given to me, which is covered with dust." Hearing Drona's harsh words, Ekalaavya, always devoted to protecting his promise, remained steadfast in truth.

Page 513

Thus, having taken away the face, the mind became agitated. Cutting through the doubt, he gave it to Droṇa, who was like a lump of flesh. Then, however, Naiṣādī began to pull like a wild animal. He was not as swift as the previous king. Then Arjuna, with a pleased mind, became free from fever. And Droṇa was indeed truthful; no one else became like him. At that time, Droṇa's disciple, especially worthy of the mace, was Duryodhana, and Bhīma approached the Kauravas. Aśvatthāmā was superior in all secrets. Thus, he was like a man among men, both Sārūka and Yamajā. Yudhiṣṭhira was the best of charioteers, and everywhere was Dhanañjaya. In the ocean, the charioteer of the chariot troop was present. In the enthusiasm of wisdom, the Pāṇḍava was in all aspects. In the love of the blood, he became a hero, equal in the teachings of blood and valor. Alone, Arjuna became a great charioteer. More than life, Bhīma was knowledgeable, and Dhanañjaya was wise. The wicked ones, the kings, were envious. They gathered all the warriors, firmly established in all sciences. Droṇa, knowledgeable in weaponry, was curious about the best of men.

Drona, displeased, said, "This cannot be achieved by you, you are merely mocking." Then, Duryodhana and others, with great fame, inquired about the situation. Drona, thus addressed, with a joyful body, released Partha, saying "Let go!" without hesitation. Then, the Pandava, with a sharp sword, struck off the head of that one who was in the city. In that action, having succeeded, he embraced Phalguna, and thought of Ud as defeated with a connection. At some point in time, the best of the Angiras, having gone to the Ganges, Drona, having submerged, was seized by a powerful being, urged by time. Though capable of liberation, he did not urge the Rishis. Having killed the seizer, he hastened, saying "Liberate me!" That statement, however, was met with a piercing arrow from Bhīma. With five seizers, he struck the great being, the one who was like a mother. The others, however, bewildered, fell here and there. Drona, seeing him engaged in action, did not consider the Pandava, and there arose affection for his own disciples. He was like a broken arrow, and the seizer attained the fifth state, having abandoned the great soul. Then, the great chariot warrior, having newly arrived, said "Take this, O mighty-armed one, it is very difficult to bear."

In the presence of Gāṅgeya, Vyāsa, and Vidura, the princes, knowledgeable in the scriptures, arrived, and with your permission, O King, they will impart their teachings. Then the great king spoke, filled with joy in his heart: "O best of the brāhmaṇas, what great work has been done for you by Bhāradvāja?" When you think of the wood in which the body is, as it is, so you should command me for the arrangement. I desire today, out of weariness, to see my sons, who are of great valor. The teacher Dururācārya will be so, O Kuru, for I do not think that such a beloved one will be like a righteous one. Then, following Vidura, the king went outside, and the great sage Bhāradvāja measured the earth. In that place, the earth was established, with the water flowing around it. In that place, the earth performed a sacrifice, worshipped by the moon and stars, and it was also proclaimed in the city for that purpose. The TAT Bags were arranged properly, and the artisans worked there. The king, equipped with all weapons, and the women too. The people of the villages were made to perform the rituals there. The fire of the house of the Jatugṛha was purified.

508 Jatugṛhadāhapavaṁ Then the mighty ones, bound by their bodies, with their arms bound, like the bulls of Bharata, shot arrows with great force. The elder brother Yudhiṣṭhira and the others followed him there, these great heroes, the sons, were truly wonderful. Some, fearing the arrows, looked at the sky with astonishment. They were targets, struck by various arrows, and they released diverse arrows from their bows. There, the group of princes, having taken hold of the bow, saw the city of the Gandharvas and were filled with wonder. Suddenly, there were hundreds of thousands of men there, with eyes wide open in amazement, saying "Well done, well done!" Having taken their bows, they repeatedly struck the elephants and the backs of the horses in battle. Having taken hold of the sword, they struck again and again. The lightness and beauty of the firm grip of the sword was evident in the use of the sword and shield by all. Then, the ever-strong Suyodhana and Vṛkodara, descended, with their maces in hand, like two lions. The mighty one, bound by his arms, stood firm in valor.

The man with the dazzling eyes entered the vast stage, like a conqueror of enemies. He wore a natural armor, his face illuminated by shining earrings, like the wind that moves swiftly. PART: The one with great fame, broad eyes, and sharp rays, is the enemy-slayer of the Kauravas. He is equal to the lion, the elephant, and the king of beasts, shining like the sun and moon combined. The young one, golden in hue, is the slayer of lions, possessing countless virtues, born from the essence of Bhāskara. The mighty-armed one, observing the grand stage, bowed down to Drona and Kripa, as if he were a humble servant. The entire assembly was still, with steady gazes, wondering who had arrived, filled with curiosity. He spoke with a voice like a cloud, addressing his unknown brother, the one seated on the throne of Savitṛ. What you have done, I will do, without anger; behold, the wonder of men is within me. As soon as he finished speaking, the crowd, as if startled, quickly rose in all directions.

The king has attained the kingdom, how can he not become a ruler? Thus, Bhīṣma, the son of Śāntanu, the one who is true to his vows and has great austerity, having renounced the kingdom long ago, will never accept it. We shall anoint the eldest of the Pāṇḍavas, the young one, the wise one, the one who is compassionate and knows the truth. He will indeed worship and unite with his son, the one who knows righteousness, Bhīṣma, and Dhṛtarāṣṭra. Hearing these words, Duryodhana spoke to those who were attached to Yudhiṣṭhira. The wicked-minded one, tormented, could not bear their words and, being envious, approached Dhṛtarāṣṭra. Then, having worshipped his father, who was without a protector, he spoke this in anguish for the people. I have heard the words of the people, O father, during the day; they desire you, Bhīṣma, and wish for the Pāṇḍava as their lord. This is Bhīṣma's opinion; he does not desire the kingdom. However, the people in the city wish to inflict great suffering upon us. Having attained the kingdom from our father, due to the qualities of the Pāṇḍavas, you too, due to the association with the unworthy, will not attain the kingdom. If the Pāṇḍava obtains the inheritance of Pāṇḍu, his son will surely attain it, and another will also be born to him.

Page 537

The king's father, especially with assistance. The strength of the sons and grandsons of Pāṇḍu is always supported. The people of Paurava were honored by Pāṇḍu. How can they not kill Yudhiṣṭhira along with his relatives? Duryodhana said: Thus, O father, I have perceived this fault within myself. The natures of the people are indeed aligned with this. Surely, your allies will primarily be those who are with us. He will soon expel the Pāṇḍavas from the city of Vārāṇavat. Some ministers, summoned by Dhṛtarāṣṭra, spoke of the beautiful city of Vārāṇasī. This society is indeed the most beautiful on earth, present in the city of Vārāṇasī under the lord of poetry. Thus, stories were narrated based on Dhṛtarāṣṭra's words. While speaking in the beautiful city of Vārāṇasī, the Pandava princes were mentioned by the king. When the king expressed curiosity about something else, the son of Ambikā, the Pandava, spoke. These men always tell me repeatedly that Vārāṇasī is the most beautiful city in the world. O father, if you think of celebrating in Vārāṇasī, go with your entourage and enjoy like the gods. To the brāhmaṇas and singers, give as desired, like the gods. Some, having enjoyed the festivities, experienced great joy. This is indeed Hastinapura, where the happy will return. Understanding Dhṛtarāṣṭra's desire, Yudhishthira replied to him about his own helplessness.

Just as my father protects it, so do I wish to protect it. Indeed, there is no one else for me who is more trustworthy than you. You are the ally by whom I can achieve my desires. Protect me, O father, and rescue me. Do what I wish with a clever means. The Pāṇḍavas, sent by Dhṛtarāṣṭra, will enjoy themselves in Vāraṇāvata under the rule of Dhṛtarāṣṭra. You, with the chariot drawn by donkeys, should go to Vāraṇāvata today as you wish. Having gone there, establish the four corners of the house, which is well fortified, and make it a great treasure by taking shelter in the storehouse. Among the various materials, some of which are made of shānasa and other substances, you should offer those that are suitable for the fire. Mixing ghee with the clay and also with the red lac, you should apply the paste in the huts. Ghee from the shānā tree, wood, and various instruments, in that place, you should place all of them around.

There, some Brahmins, trembling, lamented for the Pāṇḍu sons, greatly distressed, O best of the Bharatas. The king, enveloped in darkness, was in a difficult situation; Dhṛtarāṣṭra, of poor intellect, was violating dharma. Indeed, the Pāṇḍava, or Bhīma, the strongest of the strong, or Kuntī's son, would not delight in sin. What indeed will the great sage, the son of Mādrī, do? That kingdom, obtained from the father, is not to be despised by Gṛtarāṣṭrī; Bhīṣma, however, approves of the lowest of the low. While being exiled, the sons of Kuntī, O best of the Bharatas, are in a difficult position.

532 Jatugṛhadāhapavaṁ Indeed, the king is like a father to us, having previously been a peaceful protector. The valorous king, Pāṇḍu, is a joy to the Kuru lineage. In that man, the tiger among men, when the destined time has come, The sons of the king, the young ones, do not suffer from Gṛhatarāṣṭrī. You, our friends, having made a circumambulation of us, Return to your homes with blessings as we depart. But if your work is to be done, may your prosperity and welfare be achieved. Then you shall do what is dear to me and beneficial. They, having made that promise and having circumambulated, With blessings, they indeed went to the city. When the citizens had returned, Vidura, the knower of dharma, Spoke this word to the best of the Pāṇḍavas. The wise one, knowing the essence of wisdom, Understands how to escape from calamity.

He who knows does not harm those who are hostile. He who protects himself does not get burned. One who does not see the path does not find fear. A man who takes what is not given becomes free from the fire. One who moves knows the directions like the stars. By making a decision, Vidura, with the Pandavas' permission, went home. When Vidura returned, Kunti spoke to Bhīṣma and the people. If a statement is made in the assembly, we know it is true. If it can be heard by us without fault, I wish to hear all the conversation between you and him. Yudhiṣṭhira said: Vidura told me that sorrow must be understood, and that no one knows the path.

Purochana provided them with food, drink, auspicious beds, and main seats. There, they were greatly honored, covered with fine garments, and worshipped by the city dwellers. Then, having seen that great wise moon, I was addressed by him in the past regarding the calamity. We, having been enlightened by him, are wise and have entered the house of the wise, supported by the teachers and the good deeds of the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra. Bhīma said: If this house is indeed meant for burning, let us go there where we have previously resided. Yudhishṭhira said: Here, it is pleasing to you to say that it should not be rejected. Like the loss, we are searching for the desired path, surely. If our enemy, the one who is the architect of our destruction, finds it, he will quickly become fierce and burn us. This one does not fear the outcry of the wicked, nor does he fear the wrath of the architect of our destruction. Thus, indeed, the foolish Suyodhana remains steadfast in his opinion. Even if Bhīṣma, our grandfather, is among those who are burned, who will provoke him, or who will anger him? Thus, indeed, the Kauravas may become angry.

Jatugṛhadāhapavaṁ Concealed, Vidura spoke: "You are the best among the Pāṇḍavas. What shall I do for you with confidence?" In the dark of the Krishna Paksha, the fire will consume your house. Moreover, Vidura spoke: "You are indeed a Pāṇḍava, as you have been told this for the sake of trust." As we are, so are you; we are in your protection. This is my opinion: I shall take refuge in you, as directed by the orders of Dhṛtarāṣṭra. That sinful Kaurava and his allies constantly trouble us. May you deliver us from this fire, for indeed, Duryodhana is desirous of our destruction.

This prosperous armory belongs to that wicked one. In the pit, this great deed has been done without any remedy. This indeed is certainly what he desires. Long ago, Vidura knew this and informed us. This calamity has come upon us, which you have seen before. You should release us from the fear of Purōchana. Thus he promised, and the miner, having dug, made a great noise. In the middle of the wheel, the face is not very large, and the door is unknown, equal to the earth, O Bhārata. Out of fear of Purōchana, he covered his face. There he always resides at the house door. There, all the armed ones lived, O King, during the night. By day, they roamed in the forest, the sons of Pāṇḍu. Trustworthy yet untrustworthy, they deceive Purōchana. Dissatisfied, the king, like one who is very unhappy. The townspeople did not understand him. Elsewhere, Vidura's mother came from the best of miners.

The sage spoke. Seeing them, the wise Sumanasa became angry. Purocana, having perceived them as trustworthy, became joyful. The king, devoid of knowledge, slept like a dead man. Then, in the night, when the wind blew, the people slept. The house of Jatu was set ablaze.

The wise one lies where Purochana is. Then a great sound of valor arose from the assembly, and the people became aware of it. By fate, now the sinful one is caught, this extremely foolish one, who did not burn the noble men who were trustworthy. The Nishādī saw the burned one, the five sons of Anāga. With a shovel, he searched the hole, covered with dust, marked by a man. Thus, having approached the trees, the mighty one went. The Pandavas were born from the earth. Again, he was far away, hidden on the path due to the fear of Dhṛtarāṣṭra. You will come to a forest area with little roots, fruits, and water, where a fierce bird-like beast roams in the evening, O Bharata. These creatures cry sweetly, the waterfowl with matted feathers; indeed, this is a great truth. Having received permission, he went, as his elder brother Bharata said, to where the waterfowl cry.

The gods, like the Ashvins, are endowed with beauty; they are like the earth, lying in slumber. The strong, prosperous, and friendly ones live by relying on each other, like the denizens of the forest. Having killed these men, bring them to my presence. There is fear for you from the great lords of our domain. Understanding the words of her brother, the Rakṣasī hurriedly went to the place where the sons of Pāṇḍu, the best of the Bharatas, were. There, having arrived, she saw the Pāṇḍavas with Pṛthā, Bhīma, who was awake and unconquered. She saw Bhīma, like a mountain, and desired him in the form of a beautiful woman. This dark-skinned one, with great arms, with a lion-like body and great radiance, will be mine. I will never heed my brother's cruel words, for he is not as strong as my brother's companion. For a moment, satisfaction and the ability to kill will arise. Having killed him, I will rejoice for eternal years. She, taking the form of a beautiful woman, approached the great-armed Bhīma slowly. As if entwined with a creeper, adorned with divine ornaments, she spoke this smiling sentence to Bhīma.

Page 562

Where have you come from, O best of men? Who are you? These men here are in the form of gods. What is this great dark maiden, O sinless one? She shines like this forest, having reached her own home. This dense forest, served by demons, is not known to anyone. Here lives a sinful demon named Hidiṃba. I have been sent here by my brother, with the wicked intention of the demon, to devour your flesh like that of immortals. I do not wish for any other husband, O divine one, I speak the truth. Knowing this, act accordingly with me, O one whose mind is disturbed by desire, serve me. I am afraid of you, O great-armed one, from the demon and the man. O son, be my protector in this perilous situation. I wander in the sky, as I desire, and attain unmatched joy there with you. Bhīma said: Who would abandon his mother, elder brother, and younger brothers, when born in a demon? Who indeed, when asleep, would eat the food of demons, like a man who is blind to his mother? The killing of Hidiṃba.

Page 564

Hidiṃba said: "Frightened, this is what Bhīma said." "This wicked soul is attacking; I will act as a brother to you, so do as I say." "I am desirous and accompanied by a host of demons; I will take you to my abode, leaving no one behind." "Awaken those who are asleep, O scorcher of foes; I will go with you all, taking you without leaving anyone behind." Bhīma said: "Do not fear, O one with broad hips; no one can stand against me. I will kill this one, O fair one." "This cowardly demon is not a match for me in battle." "Behold my arms, well-formed like an elephant's trunk; my thighs are like iron clubs, and my chest is well-built." "You will see my prowess like that of Indra; do not underestimate me, thinking I am merely a human." Hidiṃba said: "I do not belittle you, O man-lion; I see you as a demon among men." "Verily, I am a demon who has come to kill Hidiṃba."

Page 565

Thus, while Bhīma was speaking, the man-eater Hal heard his words. Observing the human form of Hidiṃba, adorned with a garland and having a face like a full moon, with beautiful eyebrows, gentle nails, and dressed in fine garments, he became angry at the beautiful human form she was bearing. The demon, Hidiṃba, who was strong, spoke to her, "Who is this fool who obstructs my desire?" "Do you not fear Hidiṃba, who is enraged and has been deceived by my anger? I will destroy you, who are a source of trouble for me, and all the powerful demons before me." Thus, Hidiṃba, with fierce eyes, prepared to attack him, baring his teeth. Seeing him rushing forward, Bhīma, the mighty one, prepared to strike, saying, "Stand firm, stand firm!"

Page 567

The elephant's foot crushes the strong. Today, the flesh will be devoured by the vultures and the dogs. Let them rejoice on the ground, having been slain by me in battle. Today, I will clear this thorny forest in an instant. It is always polluted by you who devour men. Today, my sister, you will see me, having been captured by me, like a great serpent. Unhindered by you, having been slain by me, the men who wander in the forest will roam this forest. Hidimba said: What is the use of your boasting, O man, with your empty words? Do not delay in doing this deed. You think of yourself as strong, but I will know your true strength when we meet. I will not let these men sleep as they wish. I will kill you, O foolish one, who speaks unkindly. Having drunk from your sweet limbs, I will then kill this one who acts unfavorably. Vērāmpāyana said: Having spoken thus, the strong man seized the arms of the man.

Page 569

The astonished men, like tigers among men, were with Pṛthā. Then Kuntī, seeing them, astonished by their beauty, spoke this sweet word gently to them. "Who are you, O beautiful one, in the guise of a celestial? By what means, O well-formed one, have you come here?" "If you are a deity of the forest or a celestial, tell me the whole truth, why do you stand here?" Hidimba spoke: "This great forest, resembling a blue cloud, is the abode of the demon, and it belongs to me, Hidimba." "Know me, O beautiful one, as the sister of the lord of demons; I have been sent here by my brother, who wishes to kill you and your son." "Having heard his cruel words, I came here; I did not see your son, who is of great splendor." "Then, while wandering in the presence of all beings, I was compelled by your son, who is under the influence of Manmatha." "Then, surrounded by me, your son, the mighty one, was trying to take me, but I could not." "Knowing me to be long-lived, that man came himself to kill all your kin."

Page 570

Hidimbavadha's glory He, with my beloved and your wise son, was forcefully defeated by the great-souled one. With great speed, he and the elephant-like ones were pulling each other in battle, like man-eating demons. Vyāsa spoke: Upon hearing her words, Yudhishthira arose, along with Arjuna, Nakula, and the mighty Sahadeva. They saw each other, engaged in battle, like lions in a fierce fight. While pulling each other, they created a cloud of dust, like a fire surrounded by smoke. Just as the earth is enveloped by the earth-holder, may it shine like the sun covered by mist. Seeing Bhīma, the man-eater spoke this, laughing: "Bhīma, O mighty one, we do not know you. We are all asleep, like those in the form of Bhīma." "I am here to assist; I will fight the demon. Nakula and Sahadeva will protect our mother." Bhīma spoke.

Page 571

Be indifferent and observe; do not act, for you are confused. If you are reborn, it will be due to my influence. Arjuna said: What is the use of Bhīma living long with the sinful demon? It is not possible to stay here for long. In the past, the east was adorned, and the evening was approaching. The powerful demons are indeed present. Hasten, Bhīma; do not play; kill the demon Vibhīṣaṇa. In the past, he created an illusion with his arms. Vērāmpāyana said: Thus addressed by Arjuna, Bhīma, the son of Bhīma, lifted the body of the demon and threw it into the hermitage with great strength. Bhīma said: You are indeed a fool; you are strong in vain, old in vain, and your thoughts are in vain. You do not deserve to die in vain; you will not be in vain today. Arjuna said: Or do you think that you will bear this burden of the demon in battle? I will assist you; let him be killed quickly. Or I myself will kill him, O Vṛkodara. Having done so, I am indeed exhausted; let us rest for a while.

He recognized the powerful son of the demon, Bhīma. With the deformed eyes, the great demon, Rāṅukaṇa, and Vibhīṣaṇa. The mighty archer, the great hero, the one of great strength, the one with great arms, the one of great speed, the one of great body, the one of great illusion. The inhuman, human-like, Bhīma-like, and very strong. He who overcomes the demons is a human. Even a child, upon reaching youth, among humans, O lord of the world. In all forms, he is the greatest hero, he became prominent. Indeed, the demons in the womb receive and give birth. Those who take on forms of desire also become many-shaped. Bowing down, he grasped the feet of his father. And of his mother, the supreme archer, he also named him Chakratu. He spoke to his mother, "It is Ghaṭa-bhāsa." Thus, he became known as Ghaṭa-udkaca. Ghaṭa-udkaca was devoted to the Pāṇḍavas. He became their beloved, always self-existent. During the time of residence, he spoke to them. Hidiṁbā, having made the time, returned to her own path. Ghaṭa-udkaca, the best of demons, said, "I will approach my father." The slaughter of the crane.

Page 578

This is known to me beforehand, O best of the Bharatas, how those residing in the city of Dhṛtarāṣṭra are situated in unrighteousness. Having understood this, I have come to seek the highest good; there should be no sorrow here; all this is for your happiness. If all of you are indeed with me, the relatives will speak with humility and like children. Therefore, I am currently more than you; I will seek your welfare, so understand this. This city is beautiful and free from ailments; it is hidden for those who long for my arrival. Thus, Vyāsa consoled them, and having reached the one wheel, he comforted Kuntī. This son of yours is the son of Dharma, Yudhishthira; he will rule the earth along with the kings. Having conquered the entire earth through righteousness, Bhīma will surely bring about the destruction of the unrighteous. Your sons and the mighty charioteers will enjoy happiness in their own kingdom. And the tiger among men will conquer this earth, with the Rajasuya and Ashvamedha sacrifices as their duties.

568 Bakavadhapava Then, O lord of the world, they became like the city dwellers with their own qualities. They used to offer food to Kuntī, always without hesitation. She divided the portions, and they ate separately. Thus, the heroes, O Parantapa, were in distress. Bhīma, the great warrior, was in his own abode. At that time, the great time passed over the noble king of the Bharata lineage. Then, at some point, the Bharatas went to the forest, and Bhīma was there with them. Then, Kuntī heard a great sound of lamentation from outside. Seeing them weeping, she was filled with compassion and could not bear it, O king. Pṛthā, in great sorrow, spoke to Bhīma, who was endowed with mercy. "We are living happily in the abode of the Brahmana, unknown to others, and we are honored." She always thought, "What can I do for my son, the Brahmana, to ensure that he lives happily in the house?"

Page 581

Thus, the man has done this, in which ALA. As much as the evil-doers of this, more than that. This is the suffering of the Brāhmaṇa that has befallen. If there is help for him there, may it be a good deed. Bhīma said: Let this suffering be known, from where it has arisen. I will act accordingly, even if it is very difficult. Vērāmpāyana said: Thus, while speaking, they heard the sound of the great sage. The assembly of that Brāhmaṇa was indeed very great. Then, Kuntī quickly entered the inner quarters of that great soul. There, she saw the Brāhmaṇa along with Bhāryā and the son, and the granddaughter also, with a distorted face. The Brāhmaṇa said: Alas, this life is indeed worthless, devoid of essence. The root of suffering is dependence, and it is dear to the foolish. In life, there is the greatest suffering; in life, there is the greatest fever. In the life of the present, SSAA Ya: Even if one is single-minded, he does not enjoy desire.

Page 582

These separations are considered the greatest suffering. Some say that the highest liberation does not exist at all, and hell is indeed attainable. The greater suffering arises from the separation of those who are dear. I do not see a means by which I can be freed from this calamity, whether through sons or wives. I have previously strived, as you know, O Brāhmaṇi, to reach that peace, but I have not heard from you. Here I have grown, and my father is here as well. Indeed, my father is old, and my mother is also here for a long time; what joy is there in living there with relatives who have passed away? This is your desire, O friend, and my words are not heard. The loss of relatives has come, bringing great sorrow. Or perhaps this is my fate; I do not see any way to abandon my relatives while living, cruelly. I have a gentle and devoted companion, like a mother, always. A friend appointed by the gods, always leading to the highest destination. A home is established by mother and father, always a part of domestic life; having chosen rightly, one should live according to dharma. The killing of the duck.

Page 583

I cannot abandon my mother, who is endowed with the wealth of a home and is virtuous and unharmful for the sake of life. I cannot abandon my sister, who is always devoted. How can I abandon my daughter? I am not capable of doing so, having not attained the age of maturity. The great soul has entrusted the deposit of the child. In her, the granddaughters are lamenting along with their fathers. How can I abandon her, whom I have myself produced? Some think that the father is more important than the daughter, but I do not consider either of them equal to my own. In the womb, the birth is established, and thus I cannot abandon the innocent child. Even if I were to abandon myself, I would not be able to live without her. This abandonment of any of them is cruel and blameworthy. If I abandon myself, they will perish without me. I am indeed in a difficult situation and unable to escape this calamity. Alas, I will go to you, accompanied by all my relatives. It is better to die with all than to live alone.

Page 584

The Brahmin said: There should be no distress for you at any time, as this is not the time for distress. Indeed, this time of distress does not exist for you. All humans must eventually face death. The distress that arises from the lower nature does not exist here. A son or daughter will surely be lost to you. Overcome your sorrow, wise one; I will go there myself. This is indeed the supreme duty of a leader in the eternal world. One should act in such a way that even life is forsaken. That which is done there will also bring you happiness here. You will attain imperishable fame in this world. This is the teaching of the guru that I will convey to you. Your wealth and righteousness are indeed visible here. As you desire, so it will be for you; you have received it from me. The daughter and the son have been made by you, and I am indebted to you. You are capable of nurturing and protecting the children. I am not capable of nurturing and protecting the children.

Page 585

My desires are indeed unfulfilled without you. How can I, a child, be? How can you be? How can I live without you, O wise one, with a young son? I will sustain the couple standing on the path of the sage. I cannot protect this daughter of mine, How can I, when your connection is inappropriate? Just as birds spread the flesh they have discarded, So do all people spread the wealth of the cowardly. I cannot stand on the path, being disturbed and sought by wicked ones, O best of the twice-born. How can I, without a father, take this unrefined girl from your household? I do not dare to appoint her. How can I instill the desired qualities in this child? You, O protector of dharma, are without a guardian. Even this girl, who is without a protector, will seek me, As the Vedas and scriptures do. If I do not wish for her, you will take her away, As if from a sacrifice. Seeing your son, who is not like yourself, I have also come to this daughter of yours. The world is disregarded, and the self is not known.

Without doubt, the killing of men is questionable; therefore, you, who know dharma, should establish me. Considering all this, you should abandon the self and the blame; save yourself and this child. Thus addressed by her, he, weeping slowly, released his tears in the assembly.

The city and the land are protected by the forces of demons; for this reason, we have no fear from the beings. The salary for that is fixed, the food of the Shālīvāha; a bull and a man, whoever takes that, goes. Each man takes his share of the food; he who is fortunate becomes blessed with many years. For the liberation of those who strive, the demons devour those who have sons and wives. In the royal house, the king is not established in this way; may there be no affliction for the people, so that today may be eternal. Indeed, we are surely residing in the realm of the weak; always striving for the king, we are dependent. Whose words are to be spoken by the brāhmaṇas, or by the chandas followers? Indeed, they will be fulfilled like the desires of birds. The king should first be found, then fear, then wealth; in the accumulation of three, he should support his relatives and sons. If this trio is opposed by me, we shall endure this calamity. This is the one who has come to us, the destroyer of the hut; the food of a single man should be given, the salary by me. And I do not have wealth for any man.

Page 593

I will not abandon my own self or my son for the sake of others. However, I believe that it is better to know the truth about my own self. There is no sin in killing for the sake of Brahman, nor is there any sin in killing oneself. I do not desire to kill myself, nor do I see any sin in killing for the sake of others. In the case of killing a Brahmin, I do not see any redemption, nor do I see it as cruel or trivial. Abandoning a home is not cruel, nor is killing someone who seeks refuge. The wise do not consider it cruel to kill in times of distress. I do not consider the killing of a Brahmin as something I would ever approve of. Kuntī said: This is my firm resolve, that my son should be protected. My son cannot be a demon, for he is strong, virtuous, and radiant.

Page 595

The one who was seated in the assembly then asked his mother. What does Bhīma intend to do? Is he going to act with strength? Is he indeed willing to do this? Kuntī said: By my word, the one who is a great warrior will act. Yudhishthira said: What is this sharp act done by you, O wicked one? Indeed, the righteous do not praise the abandonment of a son. How can you wish to abandon your own son for the sake of another's son? This act is contrary to the conduct of the world. By whom do we all find comfort, relying on his arms? We shall again seek the kingdom that has been taken away. The one who is thinking of the valor of Duryodhana does not sleep, suffering along with the crow. By the valor of that hero, the one who was killed in the house of the dice is freed from sins. Then, remembering the strength of Bhīma, O Pāṇḍava, I resolved to take action against the brāhmaṇa. This is not determined by greed, ignorance, or delusion; but my decision regarding the strength of Bhīma has been made with wisdom. Then, both of us will be fulfilled, O Yudhishthira, in the future. The killing of the crane.

Page 597

The remedy and the great conduct of virtue. A Kṣatriya should assist a Brāhmaṇa in any matter of importance; thus it is heard. A Kṣatriya, being a Kṣatriya, should not kill another Kṣatriya; he attains great fame in this world and the next. A Kṣatriya who assists a courtesan in battle delights all beings in the world. A king who liberates a Śūdra seeking refuge attains a birth in a noble family due to royal merit. Thus, the Lord Vyāsa, the son of Kuru, spoke this ancient wisdom, which was greatly desired. Yudhiṣṭhira said: This is indeed fitting, O mother, as it has been done for the distressed Brāhmaṇa. This Bhīma will surely return after killing the man-eater. Just as the city-dwellers do not find this, so this Brāhmaṇa should be spoken of.

Bhīma spoke. Then, during the night, the Pāṇḍava took food, and Bhīma went there where the man-eater was. Upon reaching the forest, the powerful Pāṇḍava called out the name of that food. Then, hearing Bhīma's words, the man-eater came, well-prepared, where Bhīma was situated. The giant, with great speed, seemed to tear the earth apart, making a fierce expression with his three peaks and baring his teeth. Seeing Bhīma, the man-eater, with eyes wide open, spoke this word. "Who is this food prepared for my purpose? This is a meal for my foolishness." Hearing this, Bhīma laughed, disregarding the man-eater, and was very eager. He struck the back with both hands while standing behind. Thus, the mighty Bhīma, struck by the hands, did not look at the demon. He seized that tree with great strength. With his left hand, Bhīma, smiling, O Bhārata. Then he again, having lifted many kinds of trees, hurled them at Bhīma, the son of Pāṇḍu. A fierce battle arose, O great king, between Baka and the Pāṇḍava. Having called out loudly, Baka seized the mighty Bhīmasena with his arms. Even Bhīmasena, embracing that demon with his great arms, was seen to be bursting forth with great speed. Being seized by Bhīma and also by the Pāṇḍava, they fought fiercely like two powerful men. With their great speed, the earth trembled, and the great trees were shaken at that time.

Page 601

From that time, the demons were seen there, O Bhārata, by the inhabitants of the city. Then Bhīma, taking the man who had fallen, placed him in the doorway and went away unnoticed. Then Bhīma, having killed him, went to the place and reported to the king as it happened. Then the men, emerging from the city, saw the demon lying dead, drenched in blood. That terrifying place, filled with the dead bodies, was reached by a single wheel. Then a thousand men of the city came there, along with the aged and young women. Then all were astonished, seeing that exceedingly powerful being, like a god. Then they began to count whose turn it was today for the feast, and knowing this, they asked that brāhmaṇa. Thus questioned repeatedly, while protecting the Pāṇḍavas, he spoke to all the citizens. A certain brāhmaṇa, knowing my command, wept with his relatives.

The learned Brahmana, having performed the proper rituals, approached the external Brahman. Then, the Pandavas, along with Kunti, gathered around the Brahmana, listening to his stories. He narrated various tales about different regions and the diverse kings and their ancient histories. There, Janamejaya asked about the extraordinary wedding of Yajñasena, which took place in Panchala. Hearing about the wondrous deeds of Krishna, they inquired in detail about the story of that great soul. How did Krishna come into being from the fire? How did Drona, the great archer, teach all the Kauravas? How did the dear friend become different, and for whose sake? Thus prompted by them, the Brahmana narrated everything about the birth of Draupadi.

Chaitrarathapavana Brāhmaṇa said: At the Ganges' entrance, a great sage named Mahābabhūva appeared, a wise one among the Bharadwajas, always engaged in rituals. He went to anoint the Ganges, who had come there as a pure woman. There, the sage, with a clarified butter offering, spoke by the riverbank. At that time, the wind was blowing, and he was wearing a garment that was pulled down. In that place, the mind of the young men was attached, and the joyful one was engaged in the act of procreation. Then, there arose Drona, the son of that wise one, who studied the Vedas. The friend of Bharadwaja was a king named Pṛṣata, and from him, there was also a son named Drupada. He regularly went to the hermitage and played and studied with Drona, the best of the Kṣatriyas. Then, after Pṛṣata had passed, King Drupada arose.

Page 605

Drona also heard Rama desiring wealth. When Rama had entered the forest, the son of Bharadwaja spoke. Know that Drona has come to me, desiring wealth. Rama said: This has been rejected by me. Choose either weapons or body, O Brahman, not the other. Drona said: If weapons, then all of them, and also the destruction of them. To give the use of all of them is indeed great for you. The Brahman said: Thus spoken, then the son of Bhrigu gave to him. Receiving it, Drona became accomplished then. Having been struck and also approved by Rama, Having reached the great one, he became superior among men. Then, having reached Drupada, the mighty son of Bharadwaja, Know that the man-tiger is my friend. Drupada said: The non-Brahman is not a friend of the Brahman, nor is the charioteer a friend of the charioteer. What will remain of friendship even among kings and landowners?

Page 606

The Brahmana said: Having determined in his mind, he went to the city of the Kurus, aided by the serpent. Taking various riches from there, Bhīṣma gave them to the wise Droṇa upon his arrival. Droṇa then spoke this entire statement to all his disciples. The teacher's salary indeed fluctuates; it should be spoken of by the sinless. When all the Pāṇḍavas were exhausted and had completed their tasks, then Droṇa spoke this word regarding the salary. There is a king named Drupada in the city of Chhatravati; let that kingdom be quickly given to me. Then the sons of Pāṇḍu, having defeated Drupada in battle, were bound to Droṇa along with his minister. Droṇa said: I request you, O king, to befriend me again; for a king is indeed not a friend to a king. Thus, I have exerted myself in the kingdom; I am on the southern side of the Yajñasena, and I am on the northern side of the Bhāgirathi.

Page 607

The Brāhmaṇa said: The great one, though unworthy, did not depart from the heart of the king; he became emaciated due to his troubled mind. King Drupada, who was without anger, sought the Brāhmaṇas for assistance, desiring a son, afflicted in mind by grief. He constantly thought, "There is no one superior to my offspring." Having given birth to sons, he felt detachment, thinking, "Alas, they are bound," and he sighed deeply, wishing to confront Droṇa. He contemplated Droṇa's influence, humility, and deeds, and with strength and valor, he did not yield. The best of kings, striving to respond, was surrounded by the impure. The king arrived at the sacred abode of the Brāhmaṇas, where no one was unclean, and there was no one who was not a Brahmin, nor was there anyone who had taken a vow of renunciation.

Page 609

The one who sees the fruit does not measure the faults of those connected to it; he who distinguishes does not grieve, how can he be elsewhere? The one who studies the scriptures and resides in the guru's abode, always consumes the remnants of others. The one who is skilled in the qualities of food, O Agni, repeatedly. I consider you, O brother, as one who seeks the fruit; go to him, O king, he will offer you a sacrifice. While contemplating this, the king, feeling disgusted, heard the words of the messenger and became agitated. Having worshipped the worthy sage, he spoke to him. O Lord, you should give me eight thousand cows; you are worthy to fulfill the desires of Drona and others. He is indeed the best among the knower of Brahman and the best among cereals; he will defeat me in battle. There is no Kṣatriya equal to him on this earth, the chief among the Kurus, the wise Bharadvāja. Drona's arrows are capable of taking away the lives of beings; his bow is unmatched and great. He, with the speed of an external arrow, protects the Kṣatriya; the great-minded Bharadvāja opposes the mighty warriors.

Page 610

For the destruction of the Kṣatriyas, he is established like Jamadagni. For him, O king, there is a terrible and unconquerable force. The Brāhmaṇa, like a fire that consumes offerings, radiates brilliance. Thus, he firmly holds the Kṣatra, the foremost of the Brāhmaṇas. In the established Brahman-Kṣatriya, the external radiance diminishes. I am dependent on the Kṣatriya's strength, like a bird that has fallen. Having approached the distinguished Droṇa, I will invoke you, O possessor of Brahman wealth. I will strike down Droṇa's son in battle. What should I do for you, O Yāja, to obtain the wealth of cows? Thus instructed, the Yāja prepared for the sacrifice. He also urged the Guru, desiring to perform the sacrifice. The Yāja vowed to destroy Droṇa, and he did so. Then, the great ascetic Yāja, the king, revealed the means for the son to prosper. That son is of great valor, great brilliance, and great strength. O king, you will have a son like him. The king, having surrounded the slayer of Bhāradvāja, performed the action for the success of the task. At the end of the sacrifice, the Yāja invoked the goddess, saying, "O queen, I am here, with you present as a pair."

Page 611

The goddess said: "O Brahman, I bear the auspicious scents; I am obstructed by the son; remain, O my beloved, for the sake of the son." Yāja said: "By the sacrifice, the offering is prepared; by the offering, it is made pure. How can you not be present, O learned one, whether you stand or not?" The Brāhmaṇa said: "Thus addressed by Yāja, the offering was made, and from the fire, a boy resembling a deity emerged." "With a fiery hue, a terrible form, wearing a crown, holding a bow, he roared repeatedly." "He ascended the chariot, and then he proceeded." "Then they proclaimed: 'Be auspicious, be auspicious!'" "The prince, the remover of fear, the bringer of fame to the Pāñcālas, is born to slay Droṇa." Thus spoke the great being, the celestial one, at that time. And the maiden, also a Pāñcālī, arose from the altar, beautiful, charming, and delightful. With a lotus-like face, she appeared as a divine being, taking on a human form.

Page 612

The fragrance of the blue lotus, whose essence is like that, which possesses a supreme form, unmatched in the world. She, born with a beautiful body, speaks with eloquence, the dark one, desiring the destruction of the Kṣatriyas. This beautiful one will accomplish the work of the gods; for this reason, great fear will arise among the Kṣatriyas. Hearing this, all the Pāñcālas roared like a lion's pride. And the earth could not bear the weight of these lions. Then the strong one, the daughter of the sage, approached them, and no one could recognize her as another's mother. Thus spoke the sage to her, wishing to please the king; and their names became renowned, filling the hearts of all. Due to their boldness and extreme desire, this young prince will become the master of the earth. Only Krishna, calling out to Krishna, thus the couple was born at the great sacrifice of Drupada. Then the powerful Bharadvāja brought the Pāñcāla to his own abode. The wise one, considering the divine, performed the protection of Drona.

Page 613

Vyāsa said: Hearing this, the sons of Kuntī became like those pierced by Shalya. All the great charioteers became of disturbed minds. Then Kuntī, seeing her sons bewildered and lost in thought, spoke this word to Yudhishthira, the truth-teller. Having stayed for a long time in the abode of the Brahmana, enjoying in the beautiful city, O Yudhishthira, The beautiful forests and groves that are here, all those have been seen again and again. Again, those very ones please us, not so for us. O hero, the Kurunandana is not so. We shall go there, if you think it right, O father. The delightful sight will be there. The Panchalas are indeed heard to be prosperous. The king Yajñasena is said to be a Brahmana. Staying in one place for a long time is indeed not my opinion. If you think so, O son, we shall go there.

Page 615

The worship of you, O Brāhmaṇas, is not given to those worthy of worship. Then, having spoken thus, the Lord Rishi said: "These stories are indeed wonderful." There was a girl of great virtue, the daughter of the sage Kācira, endowed with excellent qualities. By her own actions, she became unfortunate; the beautiful and chaste girl did not attain a husband. Having undergone austerities, she was unhappy with her husband; through her penance, she pleased Śaṅkara with a sharp thorn. The Lord, being pleased with her, spoke to the ascetic woman: "O beautiful one, I grant you a boon." Then the Lord spoke: "I wish for a husband endowed with all good qualities." He, the Lord, replied to her: "Five husbands will be yours, O auspicious one." "Grant me one husband who will be the best among them," said Śaṅkara. "You have been told to have five husbands; I again say that this will happen as stated." The girl, who has gone to the abode of the gods, is the wife of the divine form; she is designated as the wife of Kṛṣṇa, the unblemished one.

Fee Teal: The stories of the Kurus are told in detail by them. I have heard from Narada and other divine beings about the qualities of your wise ancestors, O hero. I am defeated by you in battle, O son of Kunti, and by that very means, I instruct you, listen to me.

Page 624

Brahmacarya is the highest dharma, and you are also bound by it. Therefore, I, having been defeated by you in this battle, will follow the path. He who is a Kṣatriya and indulges in desires, does not live at all if he fights at night. Even if a king indulges in desires, he will perish in battle if he does not win at night. Therefore, whatever is desired by the king, let it be sought after. The priests should be composed and self-controlled. Those who are devoted to the Vedas, pure and truthful, will be the priests of kings. Victory is assured for the king who is disciplined, and he will have no rival. To gain what is unattainable, and to protect what has been gained, the king should appoint a priest endowed with virtues. The king should stand firm in the opinion of the priest if he wishes to rule the earth. Having attained the best of mountains, the ocean of ambrosia from all sides. A Brahmin cannot conquer a king merely by lineage or by birth. Therefore, understand this: by the lineage of the Kurus, the kingdom can be ruled for a long time with the Brahmin at the forefront.

As the sun shines brightly in the sky, so did the king of the earth, the one who gives light, become radiant. As the Brahmavādins praise the rising sun, so did the people, who were external and unrestrained, praise Partha. Due to the brilliance of the moon, the king, who was dear and radiant like the sun, became a ruler of the friends. Thus, the king, who was of noble conduct, thought to give to him, the one who was self-illuminating. At one time, the king, the illustrious and renowned one, roamed the forest in search of game. While hunting, he was afflicted by hunger and thirst, and the horse of the king, like a mountain, fell. The king, having seen the horse that had died on the path, beheld a maiden with beautiful eyes. He saw a maiden, whose beauty was like that of a lotus. The king, captivated by her form, again became enamored by her radiance. In the mountain abode, she, with her lovely eyes, stood, and she appeared like a golden deer among the trees. And she, with her body full of life, was indeed enchanting.

Page 628

The king perceived the fruit of his eyes. From birth, whatever the king saw, there was nothing like that form. Bound by that gaze, he did not move, neither in the country nor anywhere else. Surely, this form was created by the creator, mixing divine and human qualities. Thus, he began to contemplate the wealth of beauty. In the world, the king was then restrained. The king, seeing that auspicious sight, was troubled in mind, tormented by desire. Burning intensely, he spoke to the illustrious woman. "Who are you, daughter of Rambha, and why do you stand here? How do you wander alone in the forest, with a pure smile?" You are indeed adorned with all ornaments, like the desired ornament among these ornaments. I do not consider you a goddess, nor a demoness, nor a yakṣī, nor a rākṣasī, nor a woman of enjoyment, nor a celestial nymph, nor a human. Among the beautiful maidens I have seen, I do not think any are like you, O you who intoxicates the mind.

Page 630

Thus addressed by the king with sweet words, she stood before him with a broad waist. Then the king, with dark feet, spoke to her with a mind filled with desire and a voice that was uncertain. "Please, O dark-footed one, worship me, for my life is indeed dependent on you." "For your sake, O wide-eyed one, I am pierced by the arrows of desire, and it calms down only when I see you." "Consumed by the great desire, you, with your full and beautiful hips, are the one who brings auspiciousness." "My life is indeed dependent on you; what can I do, O beautiful one, who speaks like a man?" "I cannot live for myself, so, O wide-eyed one, please show me compassion." "Do not abandon me, O dark-footed one, for you can indeed save me through your love." "O timid one, through the Gandharva marriage, unite with me, for among marriages, the Gandharva is considered the best." Tapati spoke: "I am not the one, O king, who can marry without my father's consent."
