620 Chaitrarathapavana The minister, accompanied by his retinue, saw him in the great forest, fallen to the ground like the flag of Indra. Indeed, he was a great archer, fallen to the earth, devoid of horses; he became like a blazing fire to his minister. With urgency, he approached, bewildered by affection, and lifted the king, who was enchanted by desire. Like a father lifting his fallen son from the ground, he, aged in wisdom and years, did so with great effort. The minister, having lifted him, became free from fever and spoke to him with auspicious words, awakened from his stupor. "Do not fear, O son of Bhima; may you be blessed, O sinless one." Exhausted from thirst and fatigue, he lifted the king, who had fallen in battle on the battlefield. With cool water, the lord of the waters bathed him, touching not the crown of the king with the fragrant lotus. Then, having regained his life, he released everything, except for that one minister. Then, by the king's command, he stood in opposition; the king, however, reappeared on the mountain. Then, having become pure in that mountain, with folded hands, he prayed to the sun, standing with the earth beneath his arms.

Page 634

He is indeed the king, endowed with great wisdom and noble qualities; he is capable of restraining the birds of your desires. The Gandharva spoke. Thus addressed, the Sun, having determined to give, replied to the Brahmin. O king, you are the best among the sages; what else can be said about the best of women? The one who is the best among women, the Sun himself, gave to Vasiṣṭha, the great sage. Then the great sage accepted that maiden, Tapati. Vasiṣṭha, having been released, again came back to the place where the renowned Kīṭi became the chief of the Kurus. That king, with his mind absorbed in love, saw the beautiful smiling daughter of the gods, Tapati. With Vasiṣṭha as his companion, he was exceedingly joyful. After twelve nights of difficulty, the pure-souled sage Vasiṣṭha, the divine sage, arrived. The one who is worshipped through austerities, the bestower of boons, the lord of the cows, is indeed Vasiṣṭha, shining with brilliance.

Page 635

Thus, the noble man, having been duly authorized by Vasiṣṭha, desired to conquer the city along with his wife. Then, in that city, the king enjoyed with his wife for twelve years in forests and waters. In that city of the king, there were twelve sons, and the thousand-eyed one did not prevail over them. Then, afflicted by hunger, the man became like a corpse, and the city of that king was enveloped as if by death. Then, having seen such a sight, the great sage Vasiṣṭha, with a warm heart, approached the best of kings. He brought him to that city, where he had lived with his wife for many years. Then, when the rain came, the king's messenger entered the city again. Then, the entire kingdom rejoiced, and the city was filled with great joy.

Page 637

There was a sage, O Lord, who spoke this. The Gandharva said: Conquered by austerity, always victorious over the immortal. Whose feet are borne by both desire and anger. He who does not cut off the noble-minded of the Kausikas, Holding the great wrath due to the offense against Viśvāmitra. The powerful lord, afflicted by the misfortune of his son, Did not consider anything dreadful without Viśvāmitra. He who, having been struck down by death, Did not move like the ocean at high tide. Having attained him, the great-souled, the kings of Ikṣvāku, Ruled over this earth. Having obtained the foremost of priests, the great sage Vasiṣṭha, The kings, O son of Kuru, worshipped him with various offerings. He indeed defeated all those noble kings, Like Brahma, the best of the Pandavas, like Brihaspati among the immortals. Therefore, the one whose soul is devoted to dharma, desiring knowledge of the Vedic dharma, Let some virtuous Brahmana be sought. For a Kshatriya born to desire to rule the earth, The former priest is to be engaged for the increase of the kingdom.

Page 639

Vasiṣṭha, the foremost sage, having regarded the arrival, honored Viśvāmitra, the best among men, with worship. With water for washing the feet and a welcoming gesture, he also honored him with wild offerings. Then, the great-souled Vasiṣṭha, who is like a wish-fulfilling cow, spoke, "May you grant your desires," and thus she began to milk the desires. From the village, the herbs and the milk flowed, and the nectar-like essence, the supreme elixir, was produced. Various edible items and drinks, as well as delightful ambrosial preparations, were offered. The king, honored by these desires, was greatly pleased, along with his ministers and strong allies. With six spacious sides, surrounded by five, having the eyes of a frog, and with a form that is not to be criticized, He beheld the beautiful Shankhakaṇā, with a robust neck and a charming appearance, and was astonished. Vasiṣṭha's milk-giving Nandī was praised by him, and Viśvāmitra, being very pleased, then spoke to the sage. "By the power of my kingdom or by the grace of the cows, grant me Nandini, O great sage." Vasiṣṭha spoke.

Page 640

Devatātithi, Pitr, and the milk-giving Nandini, I do not wish to give you, O sinless one, even with my kingdom. Viśvāmitra said: I am a Kṣatriya, O Bhavānī, a practitioner of austerities and study. What valor is there among the Brāhmaṇas, who are peaceful and self-controlled? O Abudena, you do not give me what I desire. I will not abandon my duty; I will lead your cow by force. Vasiṣṭha said: You are indeed a king, and a Kṣatriya of great strength. Do as you wish quickly; do not deliberate. The Gandharva said: Thus addressed, Viśvāmitra forcibly took the cow Nandini, which shone like the moon among swans. Struck by the staff, she was being driven away, and the beautiful Nandini, trembling, was then with Vasiṣṭha. Approaching, she stood facing the path, her divine form turned towards the Lord, and even though she was being driven away, she did not leave the hermitage. Vasiṣṭha said: I hear your cries, O auspicious one; they are repeated by the one who is crying.

Page 641

By force, I am indeed Nandi, capable of enduring. The Gandharva spoke: But she, from their strength, Nandi, the best of Bharata, Fearing Viśvāmitra, approached Vasiṣṭha. The cow spoke: Struck by the stone staff, crying out, I, without a protector, O Viśvāmitra, why do you ignore me? The Gandharva spoke: Thus, at that time, on the path, the great sage, Did not stir, nor did he move, steadfast in his vow. Vasiṣṭha spoke: Among Kṣatriyas, strength is the best, among Brāhmaṇas, endurance is the strength. If endurance pleases me, then let it be delightful. The cow spoke: What, O Lord, do you say, that I have abandoned you? I have been abandoned by you, and cannot be led away by force. Vasiṣṭha spoke: I do not abandon you, O auspicious one, stay if possible. Thus, bound, my dear one, you are.

Page 642

Gandharva spoke. Hearing this, the daughter of Vasiṣṭha, the one with the flowing waters, Kr̥odhara, with red eyes, made the army of Viśvāmitra flee completely. Then, Kālī, being angry, like the sun, with a blazing form, released a great weapon. Asujat, the powerful one, with a tail like a lion, also released a stream of anger towards the Yavanas. The Pundarikas, Kirātas, Dramiḍas, and Singhalas, and also the Dardas, were thus affected. Then, the great army was released, with various divisions and many armed warriors. Surrounded by the enraged Viśvāmitra, one warrior stood alone, surrounded by five or seven. With a great weapon, the mighty Kālī then released a banyan tree. Seeing this, all were terrified of Viśvāmitra.

Page 645

He approached them. While they were discussing thus, the sage Viśvāmitra, the powerful one, came near. Then the best of kings, having understood the sage, recognized him as the son of Vasiṣṭha, like Vasiṣṭha himself in brilliance. Then Viśvāmitra, having understood his own self, also desired to approach them, wishing for his own dear one. But then, cursed by him, the best of kings, went to the powerful one for refuge, wishing to please him. Understanding his mood, the son of the Kurus, Viśvāmitra then ordered protection for the king. By the curse of that one and by the command of Viśvāmitra, a demon named Kiṅkara then entered the king. Then, knowing that he was seized by the demon, the sage Viśvāmitra also withdrew from that place. Then the king, knowing himself, though being oppressed by the powerful demon, remained firm. A certain brāhmaṇa saw him, the king, who was again set out, and asked him for food, being hungry. Then the king spoke to the brāhmaṇa, his friend, "Be at ease, O brāhmaṇa, I will give you food as you desire."

Page 646

Thus speaking, the king stood there, the best of the twice-born. Then, the king, having heard the words of the Brahmana, entered the inner quarters. Then, rising at night, the king quickly spoke to the Sūta, remembering the promise made to the Brahmana. "Go to that land where the Brahmanas are waiting for me." "Since you are in need of food, provide him with food." Thus addressed, the Sūta, having obtained some flesh, then reported to the king, afflicted with anxiety. But the king, possessed by a demon, said to the Sūta, "Go and feed him with human flesh." Thus instructed, the Sūta, going to the place of execution, quickly seized human flesh, trembling. Having prepared it properly, he placed it before the Brahmana. To him, the Brahmana, the ascetic, gave food. The best of the twice-born, with keen sight, said, "This food is not to be eaten." "That king who gives me inedible food will be a fool." He who is attached to human flesh, as previously stated, will wander, causing disturbance to this earth.

Page 647

The curse of the powerful one was pronounced upon the king who had killed the double. Possessed by the strength of the demons, he then fell unconscious. Then, the best of kings, deprived of his senses by the demon, spoke to that Shakti, looking like a tiger, O Bharata, without delay. Since this curse, which is not good, has been cast upon you by me, I shall begin to devour humans. Thus speaking, he immediately seized him as if he were a life, and the tiger began to consume Shakti, as if he were a desired prey. But the red one was killed, and then Viśvāmitra again commanded that demon, just like the son of Vasiṣṭha. He began to devour the sons of the great Vasiṣṭha, just like a lion devours small animals. Hearing that his sons had been killed by Viśvāmitra, Vasiṣṭha bore that grief like a great mountain bears the earth. He devised a plan for self-destruction, that best of sages. The best among the wise did not think that the destruction of the Kurus was good. The Lord Rishi released himself from the peak of Meru, and his head fell upon the stone like a heap of cotton. He did not die from the fall when the Pandava was struck; then the Lord entered the great forest.

Page 649

The great sage, released from the noose, then rose up. He named the river as Mahānṛṣi. Then, in grief, he reflected and did not remain in one place. He went to the rivers and lakes. Then he again searched for the river Hemavatī, which was under the influence of the fierce demon. Considering him equal to fire, the river bank thought of him and thus became known as Didirūtā. Then, having settled on the ground, he could not bear to be without himself and thus went back to the hermitage. Following the invisible woman, he approached the hermitage and then heard the sound of Vedic recitation. On the back, there were six decorated pillars. "Who is following me?" he said. "I am known by the name of the unseen," replied the sage. Mahābhāga, endowed with austerity, spoke. "O daughter, whose is the sound of Vedic recitation? Long ago, I heard it as if from the power of the Vedas." They spoke.

Page 650

This Fel has arisen from your son, O powerful one. He is equal to twelve here, having studied the Vedas, O sage. The Gandharva spoke: Thus addressed, Vasiṣṭha, the foremost sage, became joyful. He said that there is a lineage, and then he turned away from death. Then, having returned, he, along with that wife, approached Kalmaṣapāda, who was seated in the solitary forest. The king, having seen him, rose up, O Bhārata, and was filled with great fear from the fierce Rakṣasa. That unseen one, fierce like death, spoke to Vasiṣṭha with a voice filled with fear. Death, fierce like a punishment, approached with a terrifying Rakṣasa, having seized him. To ward him off, there is no one else in the world, O great one, among all the knower of the Vedas. Therefore, O Lord, save me from this sin, from the red-eyed one. The Rakṣasa surely intends to devour us here.

Page 651

Vasiṣṭha said: "Do not be afraid, O daughter, you should not fear the demon at all. This is not the fear of a demon that you see before you." King Kalmaṣapāda, renowned for his valor on earth, resides in this forest, exceedingly terrifying. The Gandharva said: Seeing him approaching, the great sage Vasiṣṭha, the Lord, prevented him with his brilliance. With the water, he again drenched him, liberating him from the terrible demon, the best of kings. For twelve years, he was indeed consumed by the brilliance of Vasiṣṭha, like the sun in the sky during the time of the wind. Freed from the demon, the king then shone with great brilliance, like the sun emerging from the evening mist. Then, having regained consciousness, he saluted with folded hands and spoke to the great sage Vasiṣṭha. "O great one, I am the son of Daśaratha, the best of the twice-born. In this matter, whatever you desire, tell me what I should do for you." Vasiṣṭha said:

Page 652

Chaitraratha's wind This is the way to the kingdom, govern it accordingly. O best of men, Brahmins, do not be unsteady at any time. The king said: I shall not be unsteady, O Brahman, at any time. I shall not be unsteady, O twice-born, in the presence of the worthy. O best of the twice-born, I wish to obtain the boon by which I may not be indebted. I wish to go to the desired drink, the great one, for the increase of Ikṣvāku's lineage. The Gandharva spoke: Thus he spoke to the king there. Vasiṣṭha, the best of the twice-born, who is true to his word. Then, at the right time, the sinless one, along with Vasiṣṭha, went to the renowned city of Ayodhya, O lord of men. The people rejoiced at him, as if he were the divine lord of the heavens. Soon, the king of men, along with the great-souled Vasiṣṭha, saw the city of the virtuous, adorned with jewels. The people of Ayodhya saw him, like the sun rising at the right time. He indeed filled that city, the best of the prosperous.

Page 653

In Ayodhya, the cool moon rose like in autumn, adorned with a flag and surrounded by a path of mixed rain. The mind was delighted by that excellent city, filled with prosperous and happy people, O Kurunandana. At that time, it shone like Amarāvatī, graced by Indra. Then, upon the arrival of the king, that city was born from his friend. By the command of the king, the goddess approached Vasiṣṭha. The great sage Ṛtavā, along with her, was born. By the divine arrangement of the goddess, Vasiṣṭha, the foremost sage, was present. Then, in that city, a womb arose, and the best of sages, honored by the king, went back to his hermitage. The long-held womb did not break when the divine being was in it. Then, after a long time, a son was born, named Aramaka, who was the king and the one who established the lineage.

Page 655

For the destruction of all worlds, the great-minded one deliberated. Then, the great-souled and great-austerity Vasiṣṭha restrained him for a reason; listen to that. Vasiṣṭha said: The king, known as Kṛtavīrya, became renowned; he was a performer of sacrifices and a scholar of the Vedas, a ruler among the Bhṛgus. He, the one who seized the earth, performed austerities with wealth and grains. At that time, the king, while engaged in his own affairs, found that the wealth of his lineage was present. Knowing the abundance of the Bhṛgus, the kings, all together, approached that great being. Some, knowing the earth, gave wealth without loss; some, fearing the Kṣatriyas, gave to their own kin. But some of the Bhṛgus gave wealth as desired to the Kṣatriyas, due to the sight of other causes. Then, by chance, the Kṣatriya dug up wealth from the earth. That wealth was seen by all. Out of anger, he then insulted those who had come.

Page 656

The Maheshwas fasted, except for the arrows. They cut through the earth like the Gabhodana. When the Bhṛgus were being destroyed, they fled in fear. The Bhṛgupalyas reached the snowy mountain, Himavanta. Among them, the most powerful was the one with the radiant womb, who, with one thigh, increased the lineage. They saw her shining externally with her own radiance. Then Gabhah, like the sun at noon, emerged from the external form of Prats, burning the Kshatriyas. Then they, separated, wandered in confusion. Then, in fear, the Kshatriya bulls, with futile resolve, sought refuge with the Brahmani, that unblemished one. The Kshatriyas, with their minds agitated, saw the great fortunate one, like flames of fire, bringing peace from suffering. By the grace of the goddess, let the Kshatri be seen with clear vision. Let us go, having rested, along with those who have committed sins. O mother, you are our refuge, how can you abandon us all? By giving back again, you should protect the king.

Page 657

Brahma said: "I do not grasp the object; my vision is filled with anger. This indeed is the great one, surely angry with you today." "With this eye, surely the great soul, remembering his relatives, does not doubt to take them." "When you, the sons of Bhrigu, strike the cow, then this great one is indeed held by me." "The six limbs of the Vedas indeed entered this great one, desiring the welfare of the Bhrigu lineage." "This one, surely desiring to kill, is not without anger due to the paternal vow, whose divine eye has blinded you." "O father, you should request this great son of mine; he will surely be pleased by your prostration and will release you from your affliction." The Gandharva said: "Thus addressed, all the kings then spoke to that great one, 'Be pleased,' and he granted them his grace." "By this very name, he became renowned as the best among the sorrowful; thus, the sage, breaking the bonds, revealed himself." "Having obtained the eye, the kings then returned."

Page 658

Rye Chaitrarathapavana Bhārgava, the sage, perceived the downfall of the world. He, with a great mind, created for the people. = He = With the essence of all, the mind of the self is inclined. Desiring to destroy the Bhṛgu, the best of the Bhṛgus, For the destruction of all worlds, through great austerity. He heated the beings, both divine and human, With great austerity, he would please his grandfather. Then, knowing the best of the Bhṛgus, the fathers approached him, Saying, "O son of HARE, You have been affected by the power of your great austerity; Grant the worlds grace and control your anger." At that time, the Bhṛgus, with their minds disturbed, Were disregarded by the Kṣatriyas, who were violent. When the superior life entered us, Then we were struck, desiring the Kṣatriya's own. That wealth indeed was hidden, by someone’s influence. It was cast aside by the Kṣatriyas, who were about to be angry. What indeed can be done with wealth for the heavenly beings? When death does not take away our own, Then this method is accepted by us.

Page 660

When the anger of the lowly Kṣatriyas of the Bhṛgu clan arose in the world, then I was filled with anger. The full treasury of my parents was not accessible due to fear in all worlds. At that time, a certain one, having taken the form of a woman, held me with a great embrace. When there is a prohibition against sin in the world, then sin does not arise in all worlds. When, however, the sinner does not obtain the one who prohibits sin, many remain in the world, then engaged in sinful acts. Even knowing, if one does not restrain sin, the Lord also is engaged in action by that very sin. If the kings and the Lord are my fathers, then they are capable of saving me with blood, considering this life to be dear. Therefore, I am angry with these beings today, and I cannot bear to act according to your words. And may this great Lord of mine also be indifferent to the sins of the worlds. And whoever desires to take away my anger, let him burn me with his own energy.

Page 661

I know your desire to protect all beings. Therefore, let the lords of the worlds bestow what is beneficial. The fathers said: He who wishes to take away the sorrow of the worlds, let him release you, for you are established in the waters. The waters are the essence of all that exists. Therefore, let us release this anger in the waters, O best of the twice-born. Let this remain with you, O sage, if you wish for the great ocean. The anger of the man who wishes to take away the worlds is indeed the waters. Thus, this promise is true; it will surely come to pass. The worlds will not fall into destruction. Vasiṣṭha said: Then, O son of the ocean, he who is angry released the great ocean. Having become great, as the wise know, he who drinks the waters of the great ocean. Therefore, you too should not harm the worlds, O best among the knowledgeable.

Page 664

Arjuna said: By the king Kalmaṣapāda, the best of the teachers among the knowers of Brahman, what is the reason for the fear that has been set in motion? Knowing the supreme dharma, why did that great soul not come? All was done by him earlier when asked. Gandhavva said: O Dhananjaya, understand this which you are asking me about the difficult king Vasiṣṭha and the enemy. I have told you before how that king was obtained by the great soul Vasiṣṭha. He, having obtained the kingdom, went to the city of the king Parantapa. Going to the desolate forest, he wandered about with his wife, amidst various groups of animals and diverse beings. Covered with various bushes and trees, the forest was filled with terrible sounds, and he wandered about there.

Page 665

He, once disturbed, hunted for his own sustenance. The Brāhmaṇī and the Brāhmaṇa were united for procreation. Then, having observed, he was frightened and fled. He seized the brāhmaṇa who was running away. The wife took hold of her husband and spoke to him, "Listen, O king, I shall tell you what you should do." You are indeed renowned in the world as the son of the sun. You should not commit a sin, for you have reached the invincible. In the season of mating, they have arrived. I, having been unproductive, am indeed great in my delivery. Be pleased, O best of kings, let my husband be released. Thus, while she was crying out, the cruel one devoured her husband like a tiger would a desired prey. Overcome by her rage, she fell to the ground. That fire became a blaze and illuminated the land. Then, she, tormented by grief, was angry with the king and the Brāhmaṇa. Because of you, who are cruel and insignificant, I have been devoured today by the one of great fame.

Page 666

Therefore, you too, being surrounded by my fish, will give up your life with compassion after reaching the land of the dead. By whom the sons of the best of the sages have been saved by you, he will unite with your brother and will give birth to a son. He will be the progenitor of the lowly king. Thus, the auspicious one spoke to the king, and he, being present, shone brightly like a fire. Vasiṣṭha, the great sage, knew all this through great knowledge and yoga, and through penance, O scorcher of enemies. The king, freed from the curse, was then greatly troubled by the intoxication that had fallen upon him. Indeed, the king, being enchanted by the curse, did not remember it. Hearing the words of the goddess, he, the best of kings, remembering that curse, was greatly distressed at that time. For this reason, the king appointed Vasiṣṭha, afflicted by the curse, in his own house, O best of Bharata.

Page 669

Yudhiṣṭhira said: "Those who have come from the many chariots, brothers of the divine beings, should indeed know that they are accompanied by maternal relatives." "Today, let us go to the residence of the Pāñcālas, where the great Swayamvara will take place, and it will be a grand event." "We have come here for a single purpose; there will indeed be a wonderful festival there." "The daughter of Yajñasena, the great-souled Drupada, born from the altar, with a gaze like a lotus petal." "A young maiden, flawless and delicate, the sister of the mighty Drona, who is renowned for her prowess." "Born with a golden arm, with a strong body, shining like fire." "Her sister, Draupadī, of medium stature, whose fragrance is like that of a blue lotus." "Let us go to the Swayamvara of the daughter of Yajñasena, to that great divine festival." "Kings and royal sons, generous in offerings, devoted to study, pure-hearted, and with great vows."

Page 670

The young women from various regions will come together. The great charioteers will arrive, having prepared the ground. There, the kings will offer various gifts and food for the purpose of victory. Having received all of that, we will go to the self-choice ceremony, having experienced the festival as desired. The dancers, the spirits, and the bards will also come from different regions, the mighty ones. Thus, having made arrangements, we will receive everything, and we, the great souls, will return again. O you, who are in the form of swans, looking at Krishna, let him choose the best among the gathered. This brother of yours, O illustrious one, is a great warrior, striving for victory with great wealth. Yudhishthira said: "Let us go to see the great festival of the gods, along with everyone, to the self-choice ceremony."

Thus spoke (the sage) Uvāca. Thus addressed, the Pāṇḍavas departed, protected by King Dakhinapāśchāla and Drupada. Then they, the great-souled, pure-hearted, and sinless Pāṇḍavas, honored the sage Vyāsa appropriately and were consoled by him. After that, they were permitted to proceed towards the land of Drupada. They enjoyed beautiful forests and lakes along the way, and there, the charioteers also arrived. Being engaged in self-study, pure, and sweet-tongued, they reached the Pāñcālas, the descendants of Kuru. The Pāṇḍavas then settled in the city of Skandhāvāra, in the house of the potter. There, they took to a livelihood of collecting alms, and they received what was offered to them. The desire of Yajñasena was for the Pāṇḍava with the crown; he always sought Krishna, and if he did not, he would not be fulfilled. While searching for him, the Kuntīyas, the Pāṇḍavas, made the earth tremble.

Page 672

The machine and the bow were also artificially created. With that machine, King Get obtained gold. Darapad said: This bow is strung; with this arrow, whoever pierces the target is my son. Then King Drupada himself proclaimed it. Hearing that, all the kings gathered there, O Bharata. The great sages, with the intention of the svayamvara, Duryodhana and the Kurus, along with their followers, And the blessed Brahmins from various regions came. They were honored by the great-souled Drupada with royal gifts. Then all the townspeople, with the sound of the ocean, reached the city of Ridyumāra and settled there, along with the kings. In the auspicious part of the city, in the northern part, The assembly courtyard shone, surrounded by houses on all sides. Enclosed by walls and a fence, adorned with gates and arches, Covered all around with a beautiful canopy. Decorated with sandalwood water and garlands, it was splendid. The svayamvara of Draupadī.

Kailāsarikhara, adorned with the best of the best, Surrounded by a splendid palace, elevated by virtuous deeds. With golden nets and jeweled decorations, The grand staircase leads to a magnificent seat. In the midst of a gathering of great sages, With many fragrant offerings, the swan-like beauty shines. On the bed adorned with various decorations, Like a mountain peak, it is filled with many elements. There, in various aerial vehicles, The kings of the earth sat, adorned with their own splendor. Draupadī's Swayamvara Achutājī, adorned with all ornaments. Taking the valorous Kānsa, adorned with gold. The priest of the Somakas, pure and knowledgeable, then performed the ritual with clarified butter. Thus addressed, the son of Drupada then spoke to Draupadī, "With lineage and action, let us unite with these kings."

Dhṛṣṭadyumna said: Duryodhana, difficult to conquer, Duhśasana, and Vivinśati, along with Vikṇa, are present. A hundred of the great-souled, renowned Kṣatriyas. Rākuni and Bala, as well as Vṛṣaka and Bṛhadvaṭa, all the sons of the king of Gāndhāra have gathered. Abhibhū, with his son, and Sudāma, with good speech.

Draupadī's Svayaṃvara Struck by the arrows of Kandu, the hearts of the kings were filled with sorrow at Krishna's arrival. The chariot of Drupada's daughter was adorned with gems and friends were also present there. Then, the celestial beings arrived in their aircraft; the Rudras, the Ādityas, the Vasus, and the Aśvins. All the Sādhya beings and the Maruts, along with Yama and the Lord of Wealth, were honored. The great serpents and the Gama, along with the Guhyakas and the celestial musicians, and the foremost Gandharvas accompanied by Apsaras. There were also the mighty Kauravas and the Vrishnis, as well as the prominent ones among them. They stood there, as per Krishna's wish. Those resembling the king of elephants, with five lotus-like faces, were like the fire-covered ones, and the Yadava heroes were present. Rāma, Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhīma, Jīṣṇu, and the hero Yama were there. Observing them, Rāma felt pleased with Janārdana. Others, however, were various sons and grandsons of kings; with Krishna's arrival, their minds were not at ease. They were troubled, with their teeth bared and their faces turned red. The mighty Yama was not a great personality, and so were the broad-armed Pāṇḍavas; Yama and the mighty ones were there. At that time, all were struck by the arrows of Kandu, and Draupadī was lost. The divine fragrance of the Gandharvas was present, adorned with divine garlands and surrounded by the Nagas and Siddhas. The great sound of the dundubhi echoed; that assembly filled the sky. The aircraft were bound together, and the sound of the veena resonated throughout.

Page 680

The strength of the bow is indeed great. How can one, with a weak bow, achieve success in archery? Brahmins will be mocked in this kingdom, if the work is not accomplished and is done carelessly. If one is weak in archery or due to Brahminical carelessness, let them not proceed to shoot the bow. We will not be mocked, nor will we be established in weakness, nor will we attain knowledge in the world of kings. Some say that the illustrious one is like a serpent king, with broad shoulders and strong arms, like the mighty Himāvat. This work seems possible due to enthusiasm. The power of great enthusiasm does not allow the incapable to proceed. And nothing is known in the world that is impossible for Brahmins in the three realms of existence. The wind-eaters and fruit-eaters are chanting. The weak are indeed stronger, for the learned ones possess the brilliance of Brahman. A Brahmin should not be disregarded, acting with faith and devotion. Happiness and sorrow, great or small, whatever comes, Thus, various voices of the Brahmins lament. The archer stood firm like a mountain. The contest of Draupadī's swayamvara.

Page 681

He, having circled that bow, performed the circumambulation; bowing his head, the joyful Parantapa seized it. With a twinkling eye, he swiftly grasped the ten arrows; he shot at the target, piercing it with a sudden strike through the ground. Then, in the sky, there arose a great sound among the assembly; divine flowers rained down, as the god aimed to slay the enemies of Partha. The sounds of the arrows and the cries of the wounded fell all around; there rained down a shower of flowers from the sky. The sounds of the conch and the drums resounded; the groups of charioteers and bards sang praises there with sweet voices. Seeing him, the mighty Nishūdana was pleased; he quickly approached Partha for assistance. When that great sound arose, Yudhiṣṭhira, the foremost of the righteous, swiftly went to the abode, accompanied by the two supreme beings. Observing the target, Kṛṣṇa, looking at the image of Pāṭha, took the dry garland of flowers and went, smiling at the son of Kṛntī. He, having taken her and defeating the sound, was worshipped by the Brahmins; he then released the arrow, which was inconceivable, and she too followed him.

Page 682

The sage said: To that great soul Brahmana, desiring a daughter, There was anger among the kings, seeing each other closely. He wishes to give the daughter, Draupadī, to the Brahmana, having disregarded us and treating us like grass. Let him not think that he can kill this wicked one; For killing does not bring honor, nor does it enhance the qualities of the aged. Let him be killed along with his son, the wicked one; For he has indeed called us and honored us, the kings. Having gained qualities, he then later speaks ill of us. In the assembly of enemies, like the gods in a gathering. What is this similarity? I have not seen such a king. Nor is there any right among the Brahmanas regarding the choice. The self-choice is indeed well-known among the Kshatriyas. Or if this girl is not here... Let her be cast into the fire, as the kings of the realms. If a Brahmana or a child has done this, It is not to be killed in any way by the king. O Brahmana, indeed we do not desire the kingdom, life, or wealth. Draupadī's self-choice is pure.

Page 683

The son and grandson are asking for something that is dense. Due to fear of humiliation and to protect one's own flesh, let there be no fate like that of others in the svayaṃvara. Thus spoke the king's courtiers, delighted, with their arms like clubs, ready to strike. Seeing the angry kings who had taken up their arrows, the frightened Trapa fled. The son of Pāṇḍu, mighty and swift, was perceived as a great enemy. Then, the armed ones rose up; the kings, with their fingers bound, aimed to kill the sons of Kuru, attacking Bhīma. Bhīma, of great strength, like a thunderbolt, uprooted a single hero, as an elephant would. Taking that tree, the enemy's destroyer, the fierce one, stood near the lion-like man, the son of Pritha. This statement was made by Dāmodara, who is like a brother to Jṣhṭa, with a mind beyond human comprehension. This one, who is equal to the mighty bow, causes great pain; this is Arjuna, there is no doubt about it, for I am Vasudeva. This tree, suddenly stopped in the king's transformation, is not another; how can the wolf be capable in this world?

Page 685

The elephant, frightened, approached like a wild elephant. The kings, desiring to kill, spoke harsh words; indeed, the killing of a twice-born is seen in battle. Then, Kṛṣṇa swiftly went to Arjuna, as an elephant goes to another elephant for battle. The mighty Shalya, lord of the Madras, who is like Bhīma, along with others, gathered with Duryodhana. In the battle, the one who was not fighting, shot arrows at the one who was falling with three arrows. Kārṇa, with his intellect, pulled the bowstring with great strength. With the speed of their sharp arrows, the radiant ones, Rādhā's son, bewildered, fled. Both of them, desiring victory, fought fiercely against each other. In response to the challenge, I shall show my strength and prowess. Thus, they spoke to each other. Then, knowing the unmatched strength of the arms of the one who was not fighting, he prepared for battle. With arrows released by Arjuna, the army was struck down, and they honored him.

Page 686

Karna said: "I am pleased with you, O foremost of the brāhmaṇas, in the battle of strength. And also with the lack of despair and the conduct of the conch-blowing." "Are you indeed the great sage Dhanurveda, or Rāma, the best of brāhmaṇas? Or are you directly the great horseman, or directly Viṣṇu, the infallible?" "For the purpose of concealing my self, I rely on the strength of my arms. Having assumed the form of a brāhmaṇa, you then fight against me." "For in battle, no man can directly challenge me, the son of the Sun, whether he is a warrior or the one with the crown, the son of Pāṇḍu." Vaśampāyana said: Thus addressed, Phalguna replied: "I am not a particle of the science of archery, nor am I Rāma, the glorious." "I am a brāhmaṇa, the best among warriors, a great boon." "Standing firmly under the instruction of my teacher, I am here to conquer you in battle, O hero, be unwavering." Thus addressed, the son of Rādhā, Karṇa, fell into battle, thinking that his splendor would conquer the great chariot-warrior. The battle raged there, the king's army like a wolf, with warriors madly charging, and with the trees and the wind. They were mutually challenging each other like great elephants in madness.

Page 687

Then Bhīma, lifting Shalya with his arms in battle, struck him down, the best of the weak. There Bhīma, the son of Kuntī, performed a great deed. What Shalya, the mighty, fell to the ground, was not struck down by the strong Bhīma. When Bhīma had struck down Shalya, Karṇa was also in doubt. All the kings were frightened. They said there, "Who can fight against the son of Rādhā?" Who indeed can fight against Duryodhana in battle? Similarly, against the Madra king Shalya, the best of the strong. Let a vow be taken by us, united with the Brahmins in this war. Then, having considered Bhīma's action, Krishna, the son of Kuntī, was in doubt. He thought, "The king should not be bound by dharma."

Page 688

Thus, the warriors, skilled in battle, returned, astonished, as they went to their respective places. The superior Brahman, adorned with the western attire, departed, and those who were present there also left. However, the Brahman, concealed, wept with the horsemen, and with difficulty, Bhīma and Dhanañjaya went there. Freed from the crowd of people, accompanied by Kṛṣṇa, the noble ones shone there. Their mother contemplated various forms of destruction, fearing for her sons, who were in danger. The Kurus, unaware of the plot, would not be the cause of the downfall of the Dhārtarāṣṭras. Accompanied by fierce demons, they were like a mirage. Thinking contrary to the great sage Vyāsa, Pṛthā, accompanied by her sons, reflected thus. Then, enveloped by dense clouds, the great one appeared there, preceded by Jṣhṇu.

Page 689

Having gone to that Bhāgavata assembly; the great one reached the earth. He then informed Yājñaseṇī, greatly pleased, to beg. Having gone to the hut, the sons did not see; they all said, "Eat." Later, Kuntī, seeing the maiden, said, "I have spoken harshly." Being afraid of the same dharma, lamenting; Yājñaseṇī, greatly pleased. Kuntī, taking her hand, approached Yudhishthira and spoke this word. "This maiden is the daughter of King Drupada; she has been given to you two. As appropriate, my son, I have also spoken; together, do not neglect the king." "How could I have spoken untruth today; O best of the Kurus, listen. The son of the King of Pāñcāla, dharma would not approach." After a moment's thought, King Yudhishthira; comforting his mother, the best of women. To Kuntī, the hero of the Kurus, Dhananjaya spoke this word. "By you, the daughter of Yājñaseṇī is conquered; by you, the princess will be pleased. Let her be ignited, let her be called, take the hand of this one according to the ritual." Ajana said: "Do not, O king, make me a sinner; do not let the dharma be desired by others." "Then you will be placed first; Bhīma, the great-armed, is inconceivable." "I am then Nakula; after me, the son of Mādrī, Sahadeva, is lowly. I am Vṛkodara, and Yama, and the king, all the maidens are yours." Thus, whatever is to be done here; consider what is dharmic, which is dear to the King of Pāñcāla, all are under your control.

Page 691

There sat the broad-armed one; Krishna, along with Rāuhineya, saw Ajaṭaśatru and those seated, shining like moving lights. Then, the divine Vāsudeva approached Kuntī's son, the foremost among the bearers of dharma; pressing the foot of Yudhishthira, the king, he said, "I am Krishna." Thus, Rāuhineya also praised him, and the Kurus too rejoiced; the sons of the fathers, the Yadavas, took hold of the feet of the chief of the Bhārata. Ajaṭaśatru, the hero of the Kurus, asked Krishna, "How is it that we, O Vāsudeva, who are hidden, are known to all?" Vāsudeva spoke secretly, "Even if hidden, O king, this valor of the Pāṇḍavas is known; who else among men possesses such?" By good fortune, you all, the Pāṇḍavas, are freed from the fire; by good fortune, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, along with his maternal uncle, would not have been desirous. May you be well; it is established that you should strive like a blazing fire; let not the earthly ones among you be aware of this until the time of the camp. Thus permitted by the Pāṇḍava, the imperishable glory was quickly granted by Baladeva.

Page 692

Draupadī's Svayaṃvara Dhṛṣṭadyumna, the son of the Pāṇḍavas, followed behind the Kuru princes as they went to the abode of Bhāgavata. Not knowing the men around him, he was not able to kill them; he was seated in the abode of Bhāgavata. Then Bhīma, the slayer of enemies, and Jaya, the great one, having performed their deeds, informed Yudhiṣṭhira, the one of humble nature. Then Kuntī, the noble lady, spoke at the right time: "O blessed one, give alms to the Brahmins and take the lead among the Kurus." "Those who desire food, give to them; those who are determined, let them be around you." Then quickly dividing the rest, they gave honey to the four and to my own self. "Give to Bhīma, O blessed one; he who is equal in form to me, this young man, strong and capable, is indeed a hero who enjoys much." She, like a beautiful maiden, spoke her words; and as it was rightly said, they all accepted the food. The son of Madri, Sahadeva, made a bed for the Kauravas; all the heroes, having performed their own duties, slept soundly. Agastya's command was indeed in that direction; the Kauravas, the best of men, were there. Kuntī was in front of them; and Kṛṣṇa was also there. The earth was made a bed for the Pāṇḍu sons; there was no sorrow there, nor did it seem to be a burden.

Page 693

They were there, the heroes, speaking; the tales were strange, of the Pṛtanādhikara. Divine weapons and chariots, and also the serpents, the conch shells, and the axes. The tales of them were being recounted; at that time, the son of the Pāścāla king. He heard about Kṛṣṇa and also about those seated; and all of them looked at the humans. The prince, however, was troubled; the entire story of them was told at night. He quickly went to inform King Drupada; The king of Pāñcāla, in a sorrowful form, not finding the Pāṇḍavas, asked the great soul; where has Kṛṣṇa gone, and who has taken him? Not by a lowly person; nor by a courtesan, nor by a servant. Is there not some place where my son is not found; is there not a garland fallen in this place? Is there not a distinguished man; even if of a different color? Is there not a left foot of mine; today, my son has been made dear to Kṛṣṇa. Is there not a great being, who is supremely revered; united with Partha, the best of men? Is there not a great victor, who has taken my daughter? Is there not a son of the Kaurava hero, who bears the strange name; or is it that Partha, the more powerful, has struck down the target with an arrow?

Page 695

The kings were asleep, and Krishna was their support; they lay on the earth, resting on the beds of the Dabhājināga. They were like clouds of time, and their stories were wondrous. Those tales are not of courtesans or lowborn; nor do the heroes speak of the twice-born. Without doubt, those Kṣatriya chiefs tell of war, O king. Indeed, this hope is manifestly prosperous; for we hear of the pearls from the burning of the path. As they speak to each other, the paths are concealed and they wander. Then King Trapada sent for the priest and spoke thus: "May you have knowledge." The minister, having received the king's words, went and praised them, speaking the king's words as they were. The lord of the earth wishes to know you; the king of the Pāñcālas, Drupada, is a boar. He is determined to strike the target; he does not see the end of the joy. Therefore, knowing the lineage, you should strike at the heads of the enemies. My heart is troubled by the king of the Pāñcālas and his followers. Pāṇḍu was a dear friend of King Drupada, equal to himself. This desire of King Drupada is always present; if Arjuna, with his long arms, were to find my daughter.

Draupadī's Svayaṃvara The best seats and various thrones were there. Kuntī, having taken hold of Kṛṣṇa, entered the inner quarters of Drupada. The women, along with the sons of the Kauravas, were filled with compassion. Those with lion-like movements, adorned with the best garments, were there; the great heroes with long arms and powerful bodies. The king, all the ministers, and the sons of the king, as well as friends, all gathered there. The heroes sat on the highest seats, without any doubt, and entered the assembly of the princess, not astonished by the great honors. Delicious food was served in golden vessels, and the servants and maidservants were well-dressed; even the Bhojas provided food. There, the great heroes, having freed themselves, were pleased according to their desires. They enjoyed all the wealth there, entering the assembly of the brave. Thus, calling the Pāñcāla prince Yudhiṣṭhira, with the acceptance of the Brahman, the great luminary was pleased. The soul, afflicted by thirst, asked the son of Kuntī, "How shall I know your noble lineage?" Whether endowed with qualities or born of a Śūdra lineage, or having taken refuge in illusion, they who are accomplished are indeed self-illuminated. For the sake of Kṛṣṇa, those who have not arrived, let the lord speak to us; indeed, the doubt here is not great. At the end of our doubts, may our minds find satisfaction here; may auspicious things be ours, O scorcher of foes. Desiring, speak the truth; you shine in truth, O king; thus, it should be said with offerings, not falsehood. "By whom your sons, O king, have been conquered in the royal assembly."

Upon rising, the great soul Krishna was worshipped with great devotion. He then sat on a golden seat, his mind serene. All were permitted by Krishna, the one of immense radiance, to sit on the grand seats. Then, after a moment, the sweet voice of the sage was heard. The great soul Drona, the husband of the princess, asked him, "How can one be among many, and not be a part of the whole? May the Lord reveal this to us as it is." Vyasa spoke: "In this matter of separation, which contradicts the Vedas, I wish to hear whatever each one thinks." Rapa spoke: "This is my opinion: this unrighteousness contradicts the world and the Vedas. There is no doubt that many of the twice-born are not aware of this. Nor have the great souls acted in accordance with this before. Nor is righteousness to be practiced in many ways, it is eternal." "Therefore, I do not engage in such actions, for doubt in righteousness indeed arises in me." Gṛṣṭyusna spoke:

Page 704

How can the younger brother Bhāya be superior? Brahman is indeed exalted. Brahman is indeed exalted; the good are indeed established. We do not know how the essence of dharma is established; adharma cannot be considered as dharma. I will not engage in that which is not established; may the five queens be Krishna, in some way. Yudhiṣṭhira said: My speech is not false; my mind does not dwell on adharma. Indeed, my mind here does not engage in adharma at all. It is heard in the Purāṇas that there is a sage named Gotamī, who is the best among the seven bearers of dharma. The words of the guru are said to be the best among those who know dharma. Among all gurus, the mother is the supreme guru. She also spoke the words, "Let the beggar be fed." Therefore, I consider this to be dharma, O best of the brāhmaṇas. Kuntī said: Thus, Yudhiṣṭhira, the follower of dharma, speaks; how can I be freed from the intense fear of falsehood? Vyāsa said: The self-choice of Draupadī...

Draupadī's Svayaṃvara Then the people became numerous; they were afflicted by the passage of time and death. Then the Shakas, Varuṇa, Kubera; the Sādhyas, Rudras, and Vasus, along with others, gathered there, the lord of the world, Prajāpati. Then they spoke to the teacher of the Tattvas; there is no fear for humans, therefore, desiring happiness, all should proceed to the abode. Brahmā said: What fear do you have, O immortals, from humans? Let there be no fear from the assembly of the wise at any time. The gods said: There is no particular fear; those who have come here are not distinguished. Brahmā said: The son of Vivasvat is engaged for the sake of the sacrifice; therefore, these humans do not die. In that focused state of action, that is their destined end. The body of Vivasvat is also endowed; by your strength, it is used. The end of them will be in the final moment; the strength will be in men. Vyāsa said: Then, having heard the words of the gods, the gods who are worshipped, they sat together, the mighty ones; in the Bhāgirathī, they are like lotuses.

Page 707

The seers were astonished; Indra, the hero, went there. He saw the woman with the brilliance of fire, where the Ganges flows continuously. There, the woman, weeping and flowing with water, stood holding the goddess Ganges. From her tears, a moon fell; then there was a golden lotus. Seeing that wonder, he asked the woman nearby, "Who are you, why do you weep, and for whose sake? I desire to know the truth." She spoke: "You will know me here, O king; for this reason, I weep, unfortunate one. I will go ahead; you will see me weeping for what I have done." Vyasa said: "As she was going, he saw a young man with a beautiful form, seated on a throne, accompanied by a maiden, like a mountain king. The king of the gods then said, 'You should know this; the whole world is established in me. I am indeed the one who is angry and very much intoxicated.'" Seeing Shakra angry, the god laughed and looked at Shakra with a slight smile. The king of the gods became still, like a pillar, gazing at him. When the water was filled with the creature, then he spoke to the weeping goddess, "Bring her here, for I have not yet known her." Then Shakra, touched by her, fell into the earth. There, the divine one, with his grace, said, "Thus, O Shakra, do as you wish."

Draupadī's Svayaṃvara The great king, with unmatched strength and valor, is revealed; where those like you, shining like the sun, reside. Seeing that great mountain peak, he observed others equal to him. Seeing them, he became sorrowful; "Will I not be like these?" Then the god Girīśa, with a thunderbolt in hand, angrily spoke with open eyes. "Enter this, O hundred-eyed one; for I was abandoned in my youth." Thus addressed by the mighty king of the gods, he was greatly agitated by the wind. With folded hands and a humble face, he spoke suddenly, "O Bhagavān, you are the seer of the remaining." He, with a fierce bow, laughing, said, "Such beings do not express anger here." "These too will become like this; therefore, let this Draupadī be revealed." There is no doubt that anger will also arise in you; all beings will be born human. There, what will you do to endure; having caused many others to perish? The arrivals are honored by their own deeds; I have spoken this thus; other duties must be done. The former gods said: "We shall go to the human realm, where liberation is prescribed. Let us not be abandoned; righteousness, the wind, and the great one are to be considered." Vyāsa said:

Draupadī's Svayaṃvara Then, adorned with a divine diadem, like Indra, he who is the fire, the sun, and the forest; the young ones bound by the form of the great ones, the sky was covered by the sound of the conch. Paresh: Shining with golden garlands, endowed with great fortune; directly the Vasus or the divine ones, endowed with all virtues. Seeing them, the king Drupada, delighted and astonished. Having attained that divine illusion, immeasurable; and that form resembling prosperity, endowed with beauty; the king of the earth, with their beauty, splendor, and fame. He, having grasped the great form of the son of Satyavatī; with a cheerful mind, spoke to him. Vyāsa said: There was a maiden of great virtue in the forest of the sage; she, being beautiful and chaste, did not approach a husband. She pleased the lord with her penance, and the pleased lord himself said, "Choose your desire." Freed from service, the maiden desired a husband endowed with virtues, again and again. Then the lord of gods granted her that boon, saying, "Five excellent husbands will be yours." She, having pleased the god, spoke again: "I desire one husband endowed with virtues from you."

The sons of this one should be tempted, as well as the entire lineage of ministers. Abandon the king, the son of Kuntī, Yudhishthira. Let them enjoy their residence there. Let them describe the faults of these individuals separately. The Pandavas, being divided, should focus their minds there. Or some skilled men, adept in means, may separate them from one another due to attachment. Let them either awaken Krishna, for it is indeed easy due to the multitude, or let them frighten the Pandavas in that situation. Let death be arranged for Bhīmasena or for the king, for he is indeed stronger than them. But when the king is slain, the valiant one will be killed, for they will not strive for the kingdom; he is indeed their refuge. Unconquerable is Arjuna in battle, like a wolf guarding the rear. Without Bhīmasena, Falguna will not have a share in the battle against Rādheya. They, knowing of Bhīmasena's death, will surely destroy us, knowing us to be weaker. In the presence of the sons of Pārtha, we shall act, O king, in accordance with the commands, not in disbelief. Or let them be tempted by the pleasures, let each of the sons of Kunti be separated, and then let Krishna be abandoned.

Page 722

Vidura's Arrival You are not able to express your misfortune; it is indeed destined for you. The paths of the ancestors are marked, and the desires are also under control. There is a division among them, and it is possible to separate them. Those who are attached to one do not break apart from each other. Nor can Kṛṣṇa separate them from others. What can be said about those who are surrounded by the dust? The desired quality of women is to have many husbands. Kṛṣṇa, who has attained that, does not find it easy to separate her. The king of the Pāñcālas is not dear to wealth. Surely, no one will give up the kingdom, O Kaunteya. Thus, his son is virtuous and attached to the Pāṇḍavas. Therefore, I think it is impossible to achieve them in any way. This is indeed within your capacity, O best of men, as long as the Pāṇḍavas are not rooted. Let your valor be pleasing to us, as long as they are not taken away. Our side is indeed great; as long as the Pāñcāla is light, let the attack be made upon them; do not deliberate. There are many vehicles and numerous friends. As long as they are not in Gandhāra, let your valor be swift. As long as the king of the Pāñcālas does not turn his mind towards the Kurus.

Page 725

Thus, by righteousness, you attain the kingdom; they too have attained the kingdom, this is the opinion. Let the kingdom be given to them as sweet; this is indeed for the benefit of the tiger among men and all people. If otherwise done, it will not be beneficial; your fame will also be completely lost. Let fame be protected; this is the greatest strength. The life of a man is remembered as lost when fame is lost. As long as a man does not lose his fame, he lives; when fame is lost, he perishes. Establish this righteous act, O mighty-armed one, suitable for the Kurus; it is appropriate for the self of the former ones. By destiny, those heroes bear; by destiny, Pṛthā lives; by destiny, the sinful one, the evil-doer, has gone away. From that time onward, in Gandhāra, no one will be able to look upon you. In the world of living beings, someone has heard of Kuntī, who has come. Nor does the world go to the evil-doer because of a fault; just as, O tiger among men, the world goes to you because of a fault. Therefore, this life of theirs is your cleansing of sins; it should be considered, O great king, as the destruction of the Pandavas' sins. Nor do the heroes among them live, O descendant of the Kurus.

Page 727

The appropriateness and affection of yoga should also be described. Again and again, the son of Kuntī comforts Mādri's son. May the auspicious golden ornaments be given to you, O king, as per your words. Thus, for all the sons of Drupada, for all the sons of the Bharata lineage, and for all the Pāṇḍavas, those associated with Kuntī. Thus, the Pāṇḍava, united with the comforting Drupada, should be heard after this is said regarding their arrival. Let the valiant Duhśāsana and Vikarna take the Pāṇḍavas to the beautiful place. Then, those who are always honored by you, O king, will remain in their positions with the consent of the nature of the people. Thus, O great king, I think that among your sons, the conduct is suitable with Bhīṣma. Karna said: Having been united with those two, in all matters, what is more wonderful than this, if they do not consult you? The one who, with a wicked mind, speaks secretly from within, how can he say what is the highest good for the virtuous? Friends, in difficulties, or for the sake of welfare, whether it is happiness or suffering, indeed, for everyone, it is according to the rules.

Page 728

716 Vidur's Arrival The wise and the unwise, the child and the elder, all beings find their own everywhere. Indeed, it is heard that there was once a king in the royal house of the Magadhas. That king, devoid of strength, was the supreme ruler, and he was established in all affairs. His minister, the great Kaniṣṭha, was previously known as a powerful lord. The king took possession of the enjoyable and valuable wealth, and he himself became foolishly wealthy. Taking away the gains of the greedy, he desired the kingdom for himself. Even the one who is weak and the one who is the supreme ruler, it is said, could not achieve that kingdom. What else is hidden in that manliness? If the kingdom is destined for you, O lord of the wise, it will remain in you. Otherwise, if it is otherwise destined, striving for it will not succeed. Thus, the wise should be cautious in their dealings, and the words of the wicked should be understood.

Page 730

Vidur's Arrival And you, O King, are equal to the sons of Pāṇḍu. In righteousness, O King, and in truthfulness, O Bhārata, Neither have they spoken anything superior, nor has anything unfavorable been done by you. These two, O King, are like tigers among men; how can your greatness be diminished? The wise ones among men, O best of men, do not speak anything unworthy due to you. Thus, my thoughts, O descendant of Kuru, are directed towards you. And they will not speak anything against the purpose, for this is indeed the highest good for you, O Bhārata. The sons of Duryodhana and others, O King, are like that. Similarly, the sons of the Pāṇḍavas, O King, are also in doubt. If they wish for anything good among them, They do not see any good due to their anger. But, O King, in their hearts, there is a drought; The good is certainly within them, revealing itself. Thus, O King, these great souls with great splendor, Do not reveal anything, nor is there any certainty from you.

Page 731

The one who is like a lion among men, how can the Pandava, the illustrious Savyasāchin, be defeated even by the mighty king? How can Bhīma, the great-armed one with the strength of a thousand serpents, be defeated in battle, even by the immortal? Similarly, how can Yama, the son of Yama, be defeated in battle while wishing to live? In whom there is restraint, patience, truth, and valor, how can the best of the Pandavas be defeated in battle? What can be said of those whose supporter is Rāma, whose minister is Janārdana? What can be said of their victory in battle, where Sātyaki is on their side? Those who have their father-in-law and brothers-in-law as warriors, how can they be defeated? Knowing their strength, O Bharata, act in accordance with the rightful path. This is a great act of valor, done with the favor of them, O king, for the purification of the self. Even the one who is weak, if he acts rightly, can gather strength, O king, against his own side. The strong ones and many others fall.

Page 733

Go to the six charioteers, well-prepared with their mothers, along with the divine form of Krishna, O Bhārata. Those who live by destiny, Pṛthā lives by destiny; the great charioteers have obtained the daughter of the three-footed one by destiny. All are peaceful, O Purōchana, regarding the great distress that has come upon me by destiny. Then Vidura went, under the command of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, to the Yajñasena and the Pāṇḍavas, O Bhārata. Having gone there, he, well-versed in the lineage, approached the king Dṛupada justly. He also took hold of Vidura by dharma, and he acted according to justice, as per the agreement with the Kurus. He embraced the Pāṇḍavas and Vāsudeva there, and then he asked them about their well-being. With great intelligence, he was purified in due course, by the words of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, again and again. He asked the king about the Pāṇḍavas, and then he also gave various gifts and wealth. As for the Pāṇḍavas, Kuntī, and Draupadī, and the sons of Dṛupada, as given to the Kauravas.

Dhṛtarāṣṭra, along with the minister and relatives." "King, repeatedly pleased, with unadorned hair, you too, O king, are dear to me by relation." Thus, Bhīṣma, the son of Śantanu, along with his companions, inquires about your well-being, O great sage." As they were going, Krishna spoke to Yudhishthira, who was leading the Pandavas. The sage Bhagavān said to them in a secluded place. O Dharma's wife, Yarasvini, you are the only wife of the five, so let there be no distinction among you; thus, let the rules be established. Sundopasundau, the two brothers, are renowned in all three worlds for being invincible. In a single kingdom, in a single home, with a single seat and food, let the best of sesame seeds be the reason for their mutual conflict. Let the brother be protected, as there is mutual recognition; therefore, let there be no conflict here, so do this, Yudhishthira. Yudhishthira said: Whose son is Sundopasundau, O great sage? How did the distinction arise, and how did they come to fight each other? Is Tilottama an Apsara or a daughter of the gods? By whose desire were they both killed by each other? I wish to hear all this in detail, as it happened, O ascetic; for we are indeed curious.

Due to the power of their austerities, the Vindhya released smoke for a long time, as if it had become something extraordinary. Then the gods, frightened by Bhīma's austerities, caused obstacles. They were terrified and repeatedly cried out, but they could not break the great vow of those two. Then the divine beings, having great power, created an illusion. The sisters, mothers, and relatives of those two were also frightened. Terrified and pursued by the demon with a trident, they took refuge in the forest, clad in the garments of the forest. Then they cried out for help, but they could not break the great vow of those two. When neither of them caused any disturbance, the women and all beings became invisible. Then the grandfather, directly approaching the great demon, granted a boon, the grandfather of all worlds. Then the beautiful Sundopasunda, the brothers with great prowess, saw the grandfather, the god, and stood there, wise at that time. O Lord, if the grandfather is pleased with our austerities, then let him be with us. The illusionists, those skilled in the art of deception, are powerful and take on forms of desire.

Page 746

If both are pleased, then the lord will be favorable; otherwise, not. Grandfather said: Except for immortality, nothing else will be. The decree of death is the same for the immortals. This great penance, which you will perform, is for the purpose of naming your immortality. By this reason, I will not fulfill any desire. Sundopasundāvr̥catuḥ: In the three worlds, whatever exists, whether stationary or moving, There should be no fear from each other, O grandfather. Grandfather said: As it has been stated, I now give this desire to you. This decree of death will be as it is for you. Nārada said: Then, after giving this boon, the grandfather returned to the realm of Brahmā, And both of them, having received all boons, went to their own abode, invincible in all worlds.

With the mace held by the one who supports the earth, accompanied by the great demon army, he set out with his righteous companion. Praised by hymns, they departed with great joy. The demon, having risen into the sky, went to the abode of the gods, ready for battle. Knowing of their arrival and the boon granted to them, the gods left Triviṣṭapa and went to the world of mortals. Having conquered Indra's realm, they defeated the groups of Yakṣas and Rākṣasas, as well as the beings that fly in the sky. After defeating the serpents that had reached the earth, those two great demons conquered the ocean-dwelling beings. Then, all the earth began to tremble as they prepared for battle, summoning their troops and speaking sharp words. The great sacrifices of the kings increase the strength and power of the gods. Thus, with all the demons united, the task of killing them was to be accomplished by all of us together. Having gathered together, they set out towards the cruel shore of the great ocean.

She, who is adorned with the form of a goddess, was created from the assembly of the Rāḍha. By the great effort of Viśvakarman, she became unparalleled in beauty among women in the three worlds. There is no GAARA for her, where the wealth of beauty is not suitable for the one who gazes upon her. She, endowed with the form of beauty, captivated the eyes and minds of all beings. The grandfather, having gathered the gems, named her Tittottama. The grandfather spoke: "Go, with the beautiful and the very beautiful, and create a charming allure with your exceptional beauty." Indra and the Lord Śiva, having taken their positions. The face that emerged from the southern side was adorned with feathers. When there is no obstruction from the guardians, some here become inactive, like the immortal ones. With friends, Bhīma, and the fragrant ones, they enjoyed various drinks and food with great delight. In the inner palace, in the garden, and in the desired places, they became like the immortal ones. The beings born on the riverbank, searching for Kārṇikāra, went to that land where those two great demons were. Then, having drunk the boon of wine, intoxicated by the red drink, they beheld that boon-giving woman, and being disturbed, they became agitated. Leaving the seat of their birth, they went where she was situated, both being desirous and both seeking her. In the right hand, the beautiful one seized her heavy arm, while the handsome Upasunda also grasped the excellent Tilottamā with his left hand. Intoxicated by the boon of wine, both of them, with strength and the intoxication of wine, were filled with desire for each other. The beautiful one spoke, "My sister is your teacher," while the handsome Upasunda said, "My sister is your wife." "She is not yours, nor is she mine," thus anger entered there. Due to the cause of that woman, Bhīma and both of them did not seize her. Then, seizing Bhīma's mace, he was enchanted by her desire. "I am the elder, I am the younger," they struck each other. Then, struck by the mace, Bhīma fell to the ground. Covered in blood, the two were like a blazing fire in the sky. Then, the demon host was not there.

744 Ajunavanavāsapava Then the grandfather, along with the great sages, arrived there, pure in spirit, to worship the excellent Tilottamā. The one who was adorned with boons, pleased by Brahmā, was addressed by the grandfather with affection. "O Bhāminī, you will wander in the path of the sun; no one will harm you with their brilliance." Thus, having granted her the boon, the grandfather, having taken Indra to the realm of Brahmā, departed. Thus, having become united, they both struck each other for the sake of Tilottamā. Therefore, I will go to you, O friends, for the sake of the Bharata's greatness. As there should be no division among you, let it be for the sake of Draupadī. Thus, may good come to you all. Having been addressed in this way by the great sage Nārada, the great souls decided to meet each other. In the presence of that divine sage Nārada, of immense energy. If anyone among you speaks ill of Draupadī, let him live as a celibate in the forest for twelve years.

The cow is killed by the lowly and deceitful, who are devoid of self-control; the Pāṇḍavas rush from this place. The offering of a heedless brāhmaṇa is lost. The lowly one attacks the empty cave of the Jādūla. When the brāhmaṇa is taken away, the thief is lost in wealth. When I am weeping, let the blood be held. The Pāṇḍava heard those words, the son of Kuntī, Dhananjaya. Upon hearing, the great-armed one spoke to that brāhmaṇa. Where the weapons of the great-souled Pāṇḍavas were. There, King Yudhiṣṭhira was with Kṛṣṇa. The Pāṇḍava was eager to enter and to go; he was repeatedly urged by them. There, Kuntī's son, distressed, began to think. In that wealth, the ascetic's tears were to be shed. If I do not protect the great king while he weeps, what protection will I provide? In the protection of all, may there be no lack of faith; let there be no lowly one in this world. Ajuna's forest dwelling is pure.

Page 759

There is no doubt that if I leave without asking the king, it will be unpleasant for me. The king's entry will lead to my exile, or death will be great for me in the forest. Even for the body, dharma is superior. Thus determined, the son of Kuntī, Dhanañjaya, entered and asked the king, the lord of the world. Taking up his bow, he joyfully spoke to the brāhmaṇa, "As long as the thief seeks your wealth." We will not be far from you, O lowly ones, until I go today and take your wealth from the thief's hand. Following him, the mighty-armed archer, having vanquished the thieves, took that wealth. Having taken the glory of the brāhmaṇa and having drunk it, the hero Savyasāchi, the scorcher of foes, arrived in the city. Thus spoken, Dharmarāja suddenly said an unpleasant word.

Page 763

Ajana said: O auspicious one, this vow of celibacy has been given to me by Dharmarāja; I am not a suitor. I wish to know how dear you are, O water-dweller. I have never spoken a lie, nor have I ever said anything at any time. How can there be a lie, and how can you be dear? My dharma would not be disturbed, nor would it be like a serpent. Ulūpī said: I know, O son of Pāṇḍu, how you traverse the earth, and how your guru has instructed you in this vow of celibacy. Regarding the daughter of Drupada, who is present, if one does not enter into delusion, he is not twelve years old. In the forest, one should observe celibacy; this is the time for you. This is for the sake of Draupadī, the mutual departure has been arranged; dharma does not fail here for the sake of warmth. And how can there be a failure of dharma for the sake of the broad-eyed ones? By ensuring my protection, your dharma does not perish. If there is even a slight transgression of this vow, then your dharma would indeed be the one to give up my life.

Page 765

From the sacred fig tree of Agastya and the wind of Vasiṣṭha, the son of Kunti, having fulfilled his desires, gave a thousand cows to the twice-born at the holy places and shelters. Having bathed in the holy place of Hiranyabindu, the best of men, he praised the holy places of Pavatashṛṣṭha. The best of men, having traveled to the eastern direction, went to the sacred place of Alāta. Having seen the holy places in succession, the best of the Kurus, went to the beautiful river Utpalanī and the forest of Nemiṣa. He also visited Nandā, Aparanandā, Kaurikī, and the great river Gayā, and even the Ganges in Bhārata. Thus, having visited all the holy places, he gave wealth for the purification of his soul to the Brahmins. He went to all the holy places and shelters where some meritorious deeds were done. He also gave wealth according to the rules. In the houses of the Kaliyuga, the Brahmins, followers of the Pāṇḍavas, having been permitted, approached the son of Kunti, O Bhārata. Then, having been permitted by them, the son of Kuntī, Dhanañjaya, with a few brave companions, set out towards the ocean.

Page 766

In the age of Kali, the Lord, observing the beautiful places, went to the abode of the sages. He went to Mahendraparvata, adorned by the ascetics, by the seashore. There, having visited all the holy places and sacred abodes, the mighty-armed Bhīma approached the lord of the intoxicated. The king of Maṇalūre, Dharmarāja, with the beautiful chariot. His daughter named Citrāṅgadā, with a lovely face, was seen wandering in the city by him. He approached her, the excellent one, and informed the king of his purpose. The king then spoke to him, pacifying him with these words. The king named Prabhāṅkara was born in this lineage. Without a son, he performed great austerities. By his severe penance, Lord Śiva, the Lord, was pleased with him, Mahādeva, the consort of Umā. To him, the Lord granted a single boon: that in this abode, there would always be one birth. From each birth, sons were born to all of them, my previous ones. But a daughter was born to me, surely the one who produces offspring of this abode.

Page 768

The ascetic then asked the Kurunandana, "Why are these sacred places mentioned by the Brahma-advocates?" The ascetics replied, "The five Grahas reside here and take away the ascetics' power. Therefore, these sacred places are mentioned, O Kurunandana." Hearing this, the mighty-armed one, the great ascetic, went to see those sacred places, the best of men. Then, the son of Subhadra, the great sage, having bathed, performed the ritual with great speed. Then, that great man, the tiger among men, caught the son of Kunti, Dhananjaya, in the water. Taking him, the son of Kunti, who was shining with matted hair, the mighty-armed one, the best among the strong, stood up. Indeed, that Graha was excellent, and it was adorned by the glorious woman, beautifully decorated with all ornaments. Shining with splendor, the king, divine in form, was enchanting. Then, the wonderful Dhananjaya, the son of Kunti, spoke this word, filled with great joy.

Page 769

What are you, O auspicious one? Where do you reside, O ascetic? Why did you commit this great sin in the past? I am an Apsara, O mighty-armed one, wandering in the celestial forest. I am always desired by the lord of wealth, known as Vaga, the great strong one. I have four other friends, all auspicious, who are also desirous of pleasure. With them, I will properly establish the guardians of the worlds. Then we shall proceed to the Brahmana, who is endowed with great strength, beautiful, and who wanders alone in solitude. By his austerity, the king's radiance was covered, shining like the sun in that land. By his austerity, he attained a wonderful vision. We descended to that land, wishing to disturb his penance. I, along with Sorabhayī, went to that sage through the path of yoga, O Bharata. Singing and laughing, they enchanted that Brahmana, yet he did not act against us, O hero, in any way. The great radiant one does not tremble, standing in penance. That Brahmana, angry with us, said: "You Kshatriyas, who have been caught in the water, you shall wander for a hundred years."

Page 770

The sage spoke: Then we, the best of the Bharatas, were distressed. We seek refuge in that sinless sage, the ascetic. By form and age, we are indeed proud like Cupid. It is inappropriate for us to act thus; we should not offend the great twice-born. This is indeed our destruction, O sinless ascetic, since we have come here to tempt you, who are of resolute mind. Those who are created are considered inviolable by the thinkers. Therefore, O knower of dharma, you should not harm us. Among all beings, the knower of dharma is called a friend. Let truth prevail; this is the opinion of the wise. The righteous protect those who have surrendered. Therefore, O protector, you should forgive us. Veraṁpāyana spoke: Thus addressed, the Brahmana, who performed auspicious deeds, granted his grace, shining like the sun and moon. The Brahmana spoke: O Arjuna, the one who resides in the forest...

Page 771

A hundred and the universe are indicators of one's own destruction. A hundred is the measure, but this is not an indicator of destruction. When you are grasped by the FOIA, a certain place from water, O best of men. Then you all will again attain your forms. I have never spoken untruth, even in jest. All those sacred places from here onwards, the places of women will gain fame. And the virtuous will become pure in the future. Then, having saluted that Brahmin and circumambulated him, I thought of approaching him, being very distressed. What name do we have, all of us, to approach that man with little effort, who can restore our form again? Thus we thought, having seen the great sage, the divine Narada. All of us, delighted, saw that sage, and having saluted him, we stood with troubled faces. So we asked him about the root of our sorrow, and having heard that, this statement was made here.

760 Ajunavanavāsapava In the south, there are five sacred places near the ocean, O king. Those holy and beautiful places, let us go there soon. 017 There, the tiger among men, the Pandava Dhananjaya, will liberate us, pure-souled, from this suffering. 018 Hearing this, we, the heroes, have come here. Indeed, today I will be liberated by you, O sinless one. 019 These are my friends, four others situated in the water. O hero, perform a good deed and liberate all of us. 020 Vairāmpāyana said: Then, the best of the Pandavas, knowing everything, with his bow, liberated the souls of those afflicted. 021 Rising from the water, he assumed his own form, and those Apsarases, O king, were seen as before. 022 After purifying the sacred places, the lord granted permission, and he went to the city of Maṇalūrapura, where Chitrāṅgada resides. 023 In that place, he gave the thread to the king Babhravāhana. Then, the two Pandavas approached the king from all sides. 024
