Vērāmpāyana said: In the far regions, there are sacred places and shrines. Amitavikrama went there, following his own path. In the western sea, there are sacred places and shrines. Having visited all of them, he reached Prabhāsa. Upon reaching the land of Prabhāsa, Bhīmasena, the unconquered, Heard about the sacred places and their followers from Madhusūdana. Then, the son of Kunti, known as Mādhava, approached, And saw the two of them, the Kṛṣṇa-Pāṇḍavas, in Prabhāsa. They embraced each other, and in the forest, They were accompanied by their dear friend, Nara-Nārāyaṇa. Then, Vāsudeva, the unborn, asked them, "Why do the Pāṇḍavas follow the sacred places and their followers?" Then, the unborn, as it happened, narrated everything, Hearing this, the Lord said, "Thus it is, O Vārṣhṇeya." Then, having wandered as they pleased, the Kṛṣṇa-Pāṇḍavas, Went to the mountain Revataka, as if to stay there. By the word of Kṛṣṇa, they indeed made that mountain, And the men prepared food and offered it.

Page 774

The Pandavas, having received permission, along with Vāsudeva, saw the dancers. Having taken them to their own place, the great luminary went to the divine bed that was honored. He narrated the stories of the holy places and the rivers, as well as the forests of the earth. While narrating these stories, Janamejaya, being tired, was in a state of sleep, and the son of Kunti was engaged in his own meditation. With a sweet song and the sound of the lute, he was awakened and understood the praises of the bards. Having spoken words of praise, he was welcomed by the son of Vasudeva and went to the city of Dvārakā in a golden chariot. The city of Dvārakā was adorned, and Janamejaya was honored even in the small huts of the son of Kunti. The people of Dvārakā, eager to see him, came in thousands, like grass. In the sight of women, there were hundreds of thousands, and a great assembly of Bhojas, Vṛṣṇis, and Andhakas was present. Thus honored, he was greeted by the sons of Bhoja, Vṛṣṇi, and Andhaka. Having greeted and reciprocated with everyone, he was honored with respect. The hero was honored with respect and greetings. Ajuna, the son of Kunti, was in the forest.

764 Subhadrā-haraṇa With their retinues and followers, the kings arrived in thousands. Then Haladhara, along with Revati, the lord, was followed by the Gandharvas in that place, O Bhārata. Similarly, the powerful king Ugrasena of the Vṛṣṇis, was being sung about by the Gandharvas, accompanied by the sound of their instruments. Rokṇi's son and Samba, along with Kṣīva, the two great warriors, were adorned with divine garlands and garments, like the immortals. Akrūra and Sārana, along with Gada, Bhānuvindratha, Niṣṭha, Śvārudeśṇa, and the mighty Prithu, were also present. Satyaka and Sātyaki, along with Bhāṅgakāra, were there as well. These were surrounded by women, each of them distinct, and they made the festival at Revati's place splendid. At that time, amidst the great auspiciousness, Vāsudeva and Pāśa arrived together. There, while moving about, Vāsudeva's son, the auspicious one, was adorned among the friends, making Bhadrā shine. At that moment, the god of love, seeing the beauty of Arjuna, was born in his heart. Then, laughing, the lotus-eyed one said, "What is this, O Bhārata? Is the mind of the forest dweller being stirred by desire?"

Page 777

My sister is the sister of Pāṭhaṃ Sāraṇa. If you wish, I will inform our father myself. Ajana said: The daughter of Vasudeva and the sister of Vāsudeva, endowed with beauty, should not enchant like a creeper. Indeed, all that is auspicious is mine, if my daughter-in-law is your sister. If a means is obtained, I will rely on that, if it can be done by a man. Vasudeva said: The svayaṃvara of kṣatriyas is a marriage of men, and it is uncertain due to the nature of Pārtha. Forceful abduction is also praised among kṣatriyas. The wise know that it is for the purpose of marriage that the brave act. You, O Ajuna, have a beautiful sister. Who knows what is desired in her svayaṃvara? Then, Ajana and Kṛṣṇa, having determined this, quickly sent men to the city.

Page 779

Then, that man-tiger, taking the pure-smiling one, set out towards his own city in a chariot like a bird in the sky. Seeing Subhadrā being dragged, the army of men rushed towards the city, all around her. They, having reached together, surrounded the assembly hall and spoke of the prowess of Pāthava. Hearing this, the assembly hall sounded the great conch, beautifully polished like the Jāmbūnada. With that sound, the Bhoja, Vṛṣṇi, and Andhaka clans, having given up food and drink, then left the assembly. Then, the Jāmbūnada's limbs shone like the bright jewels of the sky. The great charioteers of the Vṛṣṇi and Andhaka clans, like blazing fires, sat on hundreds of thrones. Among those seated, like the gods in assembly, the assembly guardian, along with his followers, narrated the deeds of Jīṣṇu. Hearing that, the Vṛṣṇi heroes, with eyes like red lotuses, unable to contain themselves, rose up in pride. "Quickly bring the chariots, and also the weapons and great bows," they said, "and let the large arrows be prepared."

Page 780

Some shouted to the charioteers, and some adorned the horses themselves. When the chariots were being brought, there was a great distress among the heroic men due to the armors and flags. Then the one with a garland, resembling a lion, clad in blue and intoxicated with pride, spoke this word. What is this, O Kurus, that you are desiring, unaware of the essence of this matter? This is the opinion of the wise one; let it be known that whatever is pleasing to him, you should act accordingly. Then, upon hearing those words, the beings, having taken the form of the plow, were all satisfied, saying, "It is good." The wise Baladeva said, "Let the words be equal," and all sat down again in the assembly. Then, the one who protects desires spoke to Vāsudeva, the great scorcher, "Why are you sitting quietly, observing the people?" You are honored for our sake, and we are not ungrateful for that worship. Who indeed has eaten the food that is to be broken? Thinking thus, the man was born. Desiring the relationship that was previously established, Subhadra was taken away.

Page 782

The Pandava thinks that the swayamvara is not to be seen. Who would dare to take the bride's hand? Who would want to buy the son? Kunti's son has seen these faults, this is my opinion. Thus, the Pandava, having been pleased, took the maiden by virtue. The relationship is appropriate, and Subhadra is auspicious. Who would desire the son of Kuntibhoja, born in the lineage of Bharata and the great soul of Śantanu? I do not see anyone who could defeat Partha in valor. Who among all the warriors is equal to him? By pacifying him with the highest wisdom, let us turn back, my opinion is supreme. If Partha, by force, were to take his own city, he would immediately bring glory, not defeat. Hearing this, Vasudeva and the king of the Chakrus, Arjuna, then performed the marriage there. After staying there for a year, Kunti's son, the lord, spent time in the lotuses.

Page 783

In the twelfth year, at Khāṇḍavaprastha, the king approached with humility. Having approached the king, Partha went to Draupadī. Draupadī replied to the Kurunandana with affection, "Go there, Kaunteya, where she, the daughter of the Satvata, is." Even the well-bound Bhāra was previously bound. Thus, Dhananjaya, who was of many kinds, consoled her and repeatedly showed patience. In haste, he sent Subhadrā, who was red-eyed and had a beautiful form, to the cowherd. With that form, she was adorned and became the best among the beautiful maidens. Pṛthutāmra, the glorious and renowned, worshipped her. Then, quickly approaching, the one with a full moon-like face worshipped Draupadī, saying, "I am here." Rising, Kṛṣṇa addressed his sister, "May your husband be without a rival." Thus, the delighted Bhadrā spoke to her, "Let it be so." Then, the great charioteers, the sons of Pāṇḍu, were filled with joy.

Page 784

Kuntī, very pleased, became joyful upon hearing of Janamejaya. Hearing that Pundarīka-eyed Kṛṣṇa had arrived at his best city, Indraprastha, where Arjuna, the foremost of the Pāṇḍavas, was. Kṛṣṇa, the pure-souled one, arrived with Rāma, along with the great heroes of the Vṛṣṇi and Andhaka clans. Surrounded by brothers and sons, and hundreds of warriors, the great warrior Parantapa came with a mighty army. There, the great and renowned Akrūra, the commander of the Vṛṣṇi heroes, arrived. Uddhava, of great glory and unmatched brilliance, also came, a direct disciple of the Lord. Sātyaki and Sātyaki, along with Kṛtavarmā, and the sons of Śrī, such as Pradyumna and Sāmba. Chārudeṣṇa, the valiant one, and Jhilī, and the great-armed Gada, the best among the wise. These and many others from the Vṛṣṇi, Bhoja, and Andhaka clans arrived, bringing much wealth to Khandavaprastha. Then King Yudhishthira, upon hearing of Madhava's arrival, prepared to receive Kṛṣṇa. That which was received from them, the great wheel of fortune, entered Khandavaprastha, adorned with flags and banners.

Page 785

The path, adorned with flowers, is moistened and beautiful. It is served by the cool essence of sandalwood, fragrant and pure. In every region, it burns with a strong scent, Adorned by well-gathered people and merchants. Mahābāhu, along with Rāma, approached, The supreme person, surrounded by the great Bhoja of the Vṛṣṇis and Andhakas. Worshipped by countless beings, He entered the palace of the king, resembling the abode of Indra. Yudhiṣṭhira, too, approached Rāma as was fitting. Embracing him with his arm, Kṛṣṇa, pleased, honored him with humility. He duly worshipped Bhīma, the tiger among men. Yudhiṣṭhira, the king of dharma, received them with respect, As was appropriate according to the ritual. He worshipped like a guru, as if desiring something. Some spoke with great reverence, while others greeted him. Then Vāsudeva gave the best wealth for the sake of the people, The great fame of Subhadrā's kin was to be taken. Of the chariots, adorned with golden limbs, Entangled in the net of skillful arrangements, They were endowed with four yokes.

Page 786

Kṛṣṇa gave a thousand cows and the best of them. The noble ones of Mathurā, the two-horned ones, of the virtuous words, and the white ones of the Vṛṣṇi clan, like the moon. With joy, he gave a thousand golden ornaments. Similarly, for the horses, the gentle ones, the wind-like ones, and the five white ones of Rātā and Aṅjanakeśī. In the expulsion of curses, well-suited and endowed with virtues, he gave a thousand beautiful women with good qualities. To the golden-necked, healthy ones, and those skilled in service, the one with lotus-like eyes gave. To the chief of the accomplished, HARA: He gave ten men to the burdened ones, the Dāśārha. Among the elephants, those that were broken, the intoxication of the three kinds flowed. To the skilled ones of the golden garlands, those who mounted on elephants, he gave a thousand, dear to valor. Rāma gave the foot-grabbing one to Pāṭhā and Lāṅgalī, being pleased, he brought forth the joy of relationships. He, the great wealthy one, light in body, with cloth and blankets like foam, the great elephant, the great grasping one, with a falling form and a tumultuous appearance. The one who steals the garland of the beloved.

Page 787

The great river entered the ocean of the Pāṇḍavas. A great sorrow arose for them, filled with grief. Dharmarāja Yudhishthira accepted all of that and worshipped the great chariot warriors of the Vṛṣṇis and the Andhakas. The great souls, the best of the Kurus, the Vṛṣṇis, and the Andhakas gathered together, as virtuous men do in the abode of the gods. In various places, amidst the great sound of the excellent waters, the Kurus and Vṛṣṇis rejoiced according to their desires and preferences. Thus, having defeated many days, the purified Kurus returned to the city of Pundarikā. The great chariot warriors of the Vṛṣṇis, led by Rāma, took auspicious gifts given by the best of the Kurus. Vasudeva, along with Partha, resided in that beautiful city of Shakraprastha, with great joy. Partha wandered with him at the banks of the Yamunā. Then Subhadrā, the beloved sister of Keshava, gave birth to a son, resembling Jayanta, the son of Pālakā. Subhadrā gave birth to the mighty Abhimanyu, a hero with long arms and great strength. They say that he was named Abhimanyu, the lion among men, and he was filled with valor and anger.

Haraṇahārikapava In the Satvata lineage, the mighty one arose from Dhananjaya. Whether being sacrificed or not, he bore the fire of Rāmīga. In that lineage, the great-armed Yudhiṣṭhira, son of Kuntī, was born. He gave a hundred cows to the twice-born and also a gold coin. From childhood, he was dear to Vāsudeva, Like the moon to all fathers and beings. From birth, Kṛṣṇa performed auspicious deeds for him. He also grew in strength and prowess. The four-footed one, the learned one, Like Arjuna, the knower of the Vedas, was divine and human. Even in the science of weapons and in valor, He taught the great warrior in all actions. In arrival and application, he was like his own self. Seeing his son, Dhananjaya was pleased. Endowed with all auspicious marks, With a neck like a bull, and a face like a serpent. With the pride of a lion, the great archer, With the might of an intoxicated elephant, The sound of clouds roared, With a face like the moon, he was glorious. Kṛṣṇa's valor and form were thus manifested. Like the son of Vībhatsu, he was indeed formidable.

Page 789

Pāñcālya also received five sons with auspicious marks from the five husbands, like the five mountains. Yudhiṣṭhira's son, the one with the mind of the Vindhya, was born from Vṛkodara, and from Arjuna, Śutakāma, and Śatānīka, and Nakula. From Sahadeva, Śutasena, these five great charioteers were born, like the sun and the moon. The learned ones spoke to Yudhiṣṭhira about the one born from the Vindhya, saying he should be known as such due to his knowledge of external weapons. From the thousand sons of Soma, the great archer, Bhīmasena gave birth to the mighty son, Śutāsoma. Having performed a great deed, the one with the crown was born, and thus it was said, "This is your son." From the kingly sage of Śatānīka, Nakula, the son of Kuru, created a letter of fame. Then, Kṛṣṇa gave birth to a son under the star of the deity Vahi, and they knew him as Śutasena from Sahadeva. In a single year, the sons of Draupadī, being devoted to each other, were born, O king. Having performed the birth ceremonies and the sacred thread initiation, he made them according to the prescribed rites, O best of the Bharatas. Having completed the study of the Vedas, they then took up the vow of good conduct.

Page 791

The entire earth was under the influence of Dharma, as if it were bound by kinship. Accompanied by his brothers, the king shone with great brilliance, like a grand sacrifice being performed. The learned ones, like the great sage Bṛhaspati, surrounded him, resembling the immortal Prajāpati. With great affection for Dharma, he delighted the people, like the moon delighting the pure-hearted. The people did not merely rejoice in the presence of the king, but he performed actions that captivated their hearts and minds. What was spoken was neither falsehood nor deceit, but the eloquent words of the wise were born from the depths of understanding. Indeed, the most excellent Bharata delighted in the welfare of all beings and himself. Thus, the Pandavas, free from fever, joyfully settled on the earth, shining with their own brilliance. Then, after a short while, the fierce Bhīma spoke to Kṛṣṇa: "The heat is intense; let us go towards the Yamunā." There, surrounded by good friends, let us enjoy, O Madhusūdana; in the evening, let us return to our abode, may it please you, O people. Vasudeva spoke.

Page 792

Kuntī, having obtained this, is pleased with the water. Surrounded by friends, let us enjoy as we wish. The sage Vedavyāsa spoke. Having received permission from the king of heat, they went to the path of Govinda. Upon reaching the place of enjoyment, adorned with various trees, it resembled the abode of Indra. With food and drink, and various garlands, it was well-suited for them. They entered, full of joy, with auspicious sounds, and all the people played joyfully. In the forest, some in water, some in the air, as they pleased, they played with Kṛṣṇa. Draupadī and Subhadrā, with their garments and ornaments, should give great joy to you, O mighty one. The women danced and sang, and others also joined in. They wept and struck each other, and the Asuras engaged in secret activities. The sounds of flutes and drums filled the forest with great prosperity.

782 Khāṇḍavadāhapavaṁ While standing near Khāṇḍava | 001 | I am a Brahmin with many desires, always consuming unlimited food; let the path of Vārṣṇeya provide me with one satisfaction | 002 | Thus addressed, the Kṛṣṇa-Pāṇḍavas, wondering how they would be satisfied with that food, | 003 | Thus spoken, the Lord, both newly arrived, then asked the hero, "What is the food and how much?" | 004 | I do not desire food; O Pāvaka, listen to me; whatever food is suitable for me, you both should provide | 005 | Indra always protects the fire of Khāṇḍava; I cannot burn it, being protected by the great soul | 006 | There lives his friend, the serpent Tākṣaka, who, with his retinue, always protects the fire for that reason | 007 | There, many beings were being protected due to the situation | I cannot burn that which is being protected by Indra with his power | 008 | For I am ablaze, and the clouds rain upon me | Therefore, I cannot burn the fire that is desired to be burned | 009 | You both, having come together as allies, this fire of Khāṇḍava, which is surrounded by food, should be burned by me | 010 | You both, being the bearers of water, and the beings all around, the best archers, will surely protect | 011 |

Page 795

Thus spoken, Vībhatsu addressed the one born of the fire, desiring to burn the Khāṇḍava forest for the sake of Śatakratu. I possess excellent divine weapons and many as well. O Lord, there is no one equal to your mighty arms in battle; grant me the strength to withstand the speed of my enemies. With many protections, I shall quickly defend this. Indeed, my chariot cannot be moved by arrows as desired. And I wish for divine horses, swift and white as the wind. My chariot is like the roar of clouds, shining like the sun. Thus, by the valor of Kṛṣṇa, there is no weapon equal; by which Madhava can slay the serpents in battle. O Lord, you are capable of speaking of the means to success in action; I shall prevent Indra from being driven away from the great forest. Whatever work is to be done by strength, that should be done by you, O Lord, as you are worthy to grant the means.

Page 796

The sage Vērāmpāyana said: Thus addressed, the Lord Dhūmaketu, the fire of sacrifice, contemplated Varuṇa, the protector of the worlds. The deity residing in the waters, the sun. Knowing that which was contemplated, the fire was honored by Dhūmaketu. The protector of the fourth, the great Lord. By the king Soma, that which was given, and the great bow, quickly grant the chariot with the beautiful features. Indeed, the great Partha will act with the Gandīva, and let it be given for the purpose of Vāsudeva. Varuṇa replied, "I shall give the fire." Then, the great glory, the fame that increases, was raised. The great commander with all weapons, the one who is the leader of the army. One hundred thousand were counted for the destruction of the nation, adorned with various colors, shining without blemish. Purified by the gods, demons, and celestial beings, eternal years. The burning of the Khandava.

Page 797

The divine chariot of the mighty TCA, adorned with the best of monkeys, shines with golden ornaments of the horse of King Rajat. It is resplendent like the white clouds, filled with the essence of the wind. Equipped with all necessary instruments, it is invincible against the gods and demons, glorious like the sun, captivating to all beings. The one who is the lord of the worlds, shining with his own radiance, is like the form of the sun, unchanging. He who, like the moon, rises to conquer the demons, is blazing like a fire in the sky, countering the clouds. He is supported by Tamas, equal to the weapon of Indra, auspicious and adorned with beautiful banners. In that place, a divine monkey, resembling a lion, stands there, roaring, stationed as if it were. There, various great beings are seated, responding to the sound of the enemy's call. He, adorned with various flags, approaches the excellent chariot, circumambulating and bowing to the deities. Equipped with armor and a sword, he ascended the chariot like a virtuous Partha.

Page 798

That divine bow, the best of bows, was previously ordained by Brahman. Taking up the Gandīva, Arjuna became joyful. Having honored the fire god, he then seized the strength and engaged the bow with victory. In the battle, the mighty Pandava, while being drawn, heard the cries of those who were distressed. The chariot and the bow of the great warrior became auspicious; Kunti's son was delighted in the act of assistance. Then the fire god gave the thunderbolt-like discus to Krishna; the beloved Agneya weapon also became auspicious at that time. The fire god spoke: "With this, you will surely conquer even the non-human beings in battle, there is no doubt." With this, you will be superior to humans, and even to the gods, as well as to the demons, ghosts, and serpents. There is no doubt you will be the foremost in the destruction of enemies. In battle, O Madhava, you will again strike down the enemy who has been struck down. Varuna also gave him a mace named Komodaki, which is terrible to the enemies. Then, the Krishna-Pandava, delighted, spoke to the fire god: "We have been equipped with weapons and chariots."

Page 799

Kalyos, O Bhagavan, even when the gods and demons are present. What then of the one who is eager to fight for the sake of the serpent? Ajana said: The weapon of the wheel, O Vārṣṇeya, when released in battle, is powerful; there is nothing in the three worlds that can withstand it, except for the one who is not afraid of death. Taking up the Gandīva bow, the great archer, I too am capable of conquering the worlds in battle, O Pāvaka. From all sides, O Lord, with great fire, may Kalyos, the jewel of the earth, be ignited. Thus addressed, the Lord, with the sage Dāśarha and others, assumed a form of radiance and prepared to burn the fire. Surrounded by the seven flames, it burned the Khandava forest as if it were the end of an age. Embracing the surroundings, the Bharata lion roared with thunderous sound, causing all beings to tremble. As the form of the great fire burned, O Bharata, it shone like the golden mountain Meru.

The sage Vērāmpāyana said: Then the man-tiger stood between the chariots of Dāva, positioned on both sides. In all directions, a great destruction of beings occurred. Wherever the creatures of Khāṇḍava were fleeing, there the hero rushed forth. They do not see the gap due to the swift movement of the chariots; the charioteers appear as if struck. While Khāṇḍava was burning, beings arose in thousands, making sounds like the roar of drums, filling all directions. Many were steadfast, and others were scattered, their eyes wide open, and they were bewildered. The sons of the mothers, having abandoned their fathers, went to death. In the midst of the chaos, many arose suddenly. There, there, the bodies of the burnt ones were seen moving about. In all the water bodies, the creatures were seen, the tortoises and fish, in thousands. The burning of Khāṇḍava.

Ashvaseṇa was there, the son of Takṣaka, a servant of AGAR for the purpose of liberation from the sacrificial offering. Kunti, afflicted by the arrows, could not drive away the one who was like a rabbit. She liberated him, singing, the mother of the serpent. Previously, he was seized by the tail, and she, desiring her son, ascended. With a sharp spear, the Pandava severed the head of the great deity. He was released by the wind, and the Pandava was bewildered; immediately Ashvaseṇa was freed. Then, deceived by the terrible illusion, the serpent cut him into two or three parts, like a bird. The great fire, along with Vāsudeva, would not be established. Then Jṣhṇu, with a thousand eyes, expanded the sky with sharp arrows, remembering that deception. Even the king of the gods, seeing him, was like Phalguna, as if he had created his own path, which was then obstructed in the sky. Then the great wind, causing a great uproar, disturbed the ocean, and from the sky, it produced clouds with torrents of rain. Then, the great destruction was created, even though it was the best.

Page 804

Winds alone, Abhimanyu, the one who is determined. By that, the strength of the clouds of Indra was stirred. The torrents of water, they went to dry, and the lightning struck. In an instant, the sky became filled with dense darkness. The one who is pleased with the offerings, the one of various forms, burned with a flame that was like a great fire, filling the world with sound. Protected by Krishna, the one who is firm, and by him, the one who is like a blazing fire, the birds of auspicious omens took flight into the sky. Garuda, resembling a thunderbolt, with wings, beak, and claws, desiring to strike, flew towards Krishna and the Pandavas. Similarly, the serpents gathered near the Pandavas, casting forth terrible poison, their faces blazing. Those who were angry, the celestial beings, the Yakṣas, Rākṣasas, and serpents, rose up, producing an unparalleled sound, releasing the battle cry. The iron particles, like a whirlwind, Krishna and Arjuna, desiring to kill, their anger overwhelming their strength. They proclaimed their presence, and let go of the fearsome. The best of the limbs, the supreme ones. The pure of the burning of Khandava.

Page 805

Krishna, with great radiance, then struck down the enemies. He caused a great destruction of the demon hordes. Then, the powerful ones, like the wind, gathered together, standing like waves. Then, Indra, the great lord, mounted the pale elephant and both charged at each other. Seizing the thunderbolt swiftly, the lord of the gods spoke, "Let it be so!" Then, the gods gathered their own weapons, ready for battle against the great eater. Yama, the king, took a wooden staff, and Varuna also prepared for battle. The medicinal herbs, glowing, were seized, and they also took the heavy mace and victory. Gad also seized the great bow, while the powerful Death seized the sword. Grasping the terrible club, he roamed about. The friend seized the razor-edged wheel and stood ready. Pushan, Bhaga, Savita, and the lord of the worlds, having taken their weapons, rushed towards Krishna.

Page 806

Kāṇḍavādāhapavaṁ Rudra, the Vasus, and the mighty Maruts, the Viśvedevas and the Sādhyas, shining with their own radiance. These and many other gods, the supreme person, Krishna, desiring to kill, armed with weapons. There, the offerings were being consumed, the causes of the great battle, to manifest the beings, O Bhārata. Thus, the gods, having been seen, took their positions, fearful in battle, ready to fight. And many other gods came, each with their own weapons, to prevent the arrows from falling. The gods, having been frightened, abandoned the battle, like Indra himself. The sages, astonished, were there, in the heavens, established. Indra, too, having perceived them, became exceedingly pleased and began to fight. Then, the great commander, the lord of the feast, began to release arrows, desiring to know. That arrow, like a rain, struck down the unyielding. The fruitless actions being performed, the great king, observing, began to act again.

Page 808

While observing the fire, Krishna, armed and ready, appeared as if frightened by the sound of the explosion. The radiant wheel was wielded by Janārdana, causing the beings to tremble like clouds in a storm. Hundreds of beings were cut down and fell instantly. There, where the demons were, the wheel of Krishna was unleashed, drenched in the blood of the slain, like clouds in the rainy season. Demons, birds, and serpents fell in thousands. The wheel of Krishna, the slayer of enemies, was hurled, killing many beings repeatedly. Thus, the entirety of the beings of Bharata was overwhelmed by him. Among the assembled gods and demons, the victorious one was not Krishna or the Pandavas. When the gods sought to rescue him from the fire, they turned their faces away. Shatakratu, seeing the gods retreating, was pleased and praised Krishna and the Pandavas. When the gods had withdrawn, the voice of the body spoke, considering the great and profound silence. Khandavadaaha.

Page 809

The great serpent Taksaka, your friend, is not present. He has gone to Kurukṣetra, the place of the burning of Khandava. In this battle, you cannot be defeated by anyone, even by Vasudeva or Indra. Know this word of mine. The god Narānārāyaṇa is well-known in heaven. You too know what is your strength. It is not possible to conquer the unconquerable in battle. In all worlds, he is the best among the ancient sages. Even the most worshipped ones, along with the gods and demons, the Yakṣas, Rākṣasas, Gandharvas, Kinnaras, and serpents, are present. Therefore, along with the gods, you should go to the great Vasava. It is also destined that the destruction of Khandava will not be delayed. Thus speaking, the lord of the gods, having given up his anger, then ascended to heaven. The great soul, having set out, was observed by the denizens of heaven. Quickly, the king's companions followed him, the hundred sacrifices. At that time, they went to the king of the gods, along with the gods, to perform the initiation. The hero Arjuna, son of Vasudeva, roared like a lion. When the king of the gods departed, the Kṛṣṇa-Pāṇḍavas burned the unburnt again.

Page 810

As the wind, Arjuna struck down the gods with arrows, like a storm dispersing the clouds. Not a single being could move, as the arrows were cutting through them, even the mighty ones in battle. What could they do against Arjuna, who was invincible? In the face of such a powerful force, how could they engage in battle? He pierced through hundreds with a single arrow, as if they were directly struck by death. There was no peace for them, nor could they find solace in the abode of their fathers or gods, as they were filled with anguish. The multitude of beings cried out in despair, along with the animals and birds. By that sound, the ocean of fear was stirred. Neither Arjuna, the mighty-armed, nor Krishna, the great strength, could be seen; who could dare to face them? Even those who had come to the battlefield were being devoured by the arrows of Hari. They fell, their bodies struck down by the force of the arrows, like great mountains collapsing. The wind carried away the blood and flesh, as the smoke rose up. The fire of the Khandava was blazing.

Page 811

The radiant one, with a blazing tongue, and the great-faced one, having attained the nectar created by Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna, became joyful and satisfied, having reached the supreme bliss. Then, from the abode of the serpent named Takṣaka, the demon Maya, suddenly rushing, was bitten by Madhusūdana. The charioteer of the fire, desiring to burn, prayed to him, becoming a body with matted hair. Vasudeva, having raised the discus, stood firm, and with the discus raised, he aimed to burn the enemies. Hearing the fearful sound, Dhananjaya spoke, "Do not fear," thus reviving the fallen. To him, Partha, I gave the fearlessness that was given to Namuci; the fire did not wish to burn, nor did it burn. In that forest, I also, being clever, did not burn the horse.

Page 813

What is the reason for these children being covered by austerity? What have I not done there, of which this is the result of action? There I shall do what is covered in this manner. The result of this austerity is for the divine beings. The gods said: The indebted humans are known externally; listen to that. By the practice of Brahmacharya and through progeny, it is not gained. All this is accomplished through sacrifice and austerity with sons. The ascetic who performs sacrifices does not have progeny. Thus, these worlds are covered for the sake of progeny. May you beget offspring; then you will enjoy the eternal fruits. The son of Punname, being saved from hell, protects his father, O sage. Therefore, O best of the twice-born, strive for the continuation of offspring. Vairāmpāyana said: Hearing this, the slow-witted one pondered the words of the divine beings: What indeed will be the offspring, if it is not quickly produced? While pondering, he did not approach the birds of the offspring. Becoming a bird, he approached the one who gives birth. In that, he generated sons, the four Brahma-voiced ones. He then went there, having spoken to them.

Page 814

In the forest of the sages, with the joy of the sons of the young ones, when the great one arrived, the wise one thought deeply. Having abandoned the ascetics in the forest, the king did not abandon the threads. The mind was engaged in its own activities. Then the sage saw this Khandava forest burning. Understanding this resolve and knowing the sons and the young ones, the Brahmin, the knower of the Vedas, was very pleased. The great guardian of the world, having given the leaves, spoke. "O Agni, you are the mouth of all the gods, you dwell within all beings, hidden and moving, O fire. The poets call you one, and again they call you diverse. You, having created this world, they say, if the entire universe were not created by you, it would not exist immediately." To you, the Brahmins bow, having achieved their own goals.

Page 815

They go with the palisades, even the eternal ones. You are called the giver of water, O sky, with the lightning. All beings are burned, having departed from you, O horses. O Agni, you are this world’s creator, the one who gives life. Your work has been established for all beings, both mobile and immobile. The waters are established by you; this entire world is in you. The offerings and the sacred food are properly established in you. You alone are the flame, you are the killer, you are Brihaspati. You are Yama, the friend, and you are the moon, and you are also the wind. Vairāmpāyana said: Thus praised, the fire, with a gentle breeze, was pleased. He was satisfied with the king, the sage of immeasurable splendor. The pleased one spoke: "What do you wish for me to do?" Then the gentle one, having heard the words of the sage, burned the Khandava forest, desiring my sons. Thus, having heard that promise, the Lord, the carrier of offerings, set ablaze the Khandava forest with that fire, desiring to consume.

Page 816

The sage spoke. Then, when the bright Venus was ignited, those who were greatly distressed and agitated did not go to the refuge. Hearing the cries of her sons, the ascetic mother, tormented by sorrow, wept, O king of men. This terrible one, who burns like fire, is coming, Bhīma, who torments me with my suffering. This one frightens and approaches, like a fierce lion. Due to my inability, my sons are not able to take refuge with me; I am unable to save them from another. I am not able to abandon my heart, which is greatly distressed. Who, indeed, will I take with me if I go, having taken my son? What will happen to me after doing this? How will you, my sons, think? While contemplating liberation, I do not attain anything. Covering you with my limbs, I will face death together. The elder one is established in this, as a result of the elder's birth, may he be born for the fathers.

Page 817

Stambamitra, the best among Brahmavids, spoke thus: "O father, you have gone to the abode of the dead." Who can take away the best of destinies? What should be done to achieve the task, they wondered. Not seeing the liberation of their sons, the Shāṅgas spoke to their mother. "O mother, you are the one where there is no sacrificial altar; indeed, your sons will be born among us who are destroyed." "O mother, in your womb, our lineage will not exist." One should consider both aspects; it may be that our lineage is not capable. "Do not be distressed, O mother; do not let our sons be lost. This is not a futile endeavor for our father who desires worldly pleasures." Jarita spoke: "This is the place of the tree near the earth; quickly enter here, there is no fear for you." "Then I will create an opening with dust, O sons. Thus, I believe it is done on the burning path of Krishna." "Thus, I will take away the accumulation of dust; may this be a means for your liberation from the fire of sacrifice."

Page 819

Out of doubt, the one who doubts will meet death. You will surely follow your sons as is just. Jarito said: I saw that eagle coming to the hole. Taking the gate, it seized the mouse from the hole. I swiftly followed that falling eagle from behind. Using its claws, it caught the mouse. Whoever takes the mouse, O king, you run away with it. May you ascend to heaven, free from enemies, golden. When it was eaten by that hungry bird, I, having obtained permission, returned home with a countermeasure. Enter the hole, sons, there is no fear for you. By this, my sight is upon you. MHA said: We do not know, O mother, how we can enter the hole, not knowing, we cannot enter. Jarito said: I indeed know that the mouse was killed by that eagle. Therefore, there is no fear, let my words be acted upon.

Sārṅgakopākhyānam They go upwards and downwards, and they also flow to the sides. O great power, may you protect us! Sārisṛkka said: The mother is surrendered, the father is not known; the wings do not know the progenitor. There is no one else before you; therefore, O hero, protect us! May your auspicious form and the seven causes protect us! You alone are the one who performs austerities; there is no other ascetic among the gods. May the sages, who are like the wind, protect us; send us the offerings! Stambamitra said: You alone are the one who sustains the world. You support all beings; you are the one who manifests the universe. You are the one who carries the offerings; you alone are the supreme offering. The wise worship you in many ways. When the offerings are received, you cook them again in the pot. You are the source of all this universe, and you are again established. You are the food of all beings, the essence of the world, O lord of the universe. You are always growing; everything is established in you. Droṇa said:

Page 823

The sun, having become radiant, knows the earth's essence; the earth's essence, born of the earth, is also the essence of the trees. Taking all, at the time of resurgence, you are the one who brings forth the essence. These are the green coverings of the mighty sea, which arise again from you, O hero. This is the supreme refuge of Varuṇa, the one who protects us from the heavens. O red-necked one, Krishna, the fire of sacrifice, go forth to our homes by the sea. Thus spoke Vāyasa. Having been addressed, the knower of the sacred fire, Drona, with a firm resolve, spoke to Drona, who was of steadfast mind. O sage Drona, you are indeed that which is external; this has been brought forth by you. I shall fulfill your desires, and there is no fear for you. By the gentle recitation, you have indeed been previously informed by me; do not reject my sons, for I remember this. And whatever you have spoken here, I hold both as significant; what shall I do for you? I am pleased, may you be blessed; I offer you praise, O Lord. Drona spoke: In restraint, the mighty ones always elevate us.

Page 825

Lalapyamāna, the sage Mandapāla, thus spoke in the forest, addressing this with envy, O Bhārata. You do not regard the sons; you have spoken to those sages. The radiant and valiant ones do not burn from their glow. Thus, you are indeed surrounded by those who are devoted to me; this is what was promised, and so it is, by the great-souled one. The protector of the world does not speak untruths, nor is there any speaker among them who has any esteem. Thinking of my friend, you are tormented; do not think of me as that which was in the past. Indeed, it is not just for a friend to be pained without reason; the self cannot bear to observe being tormented. Go, you are indeed tormented for that reason; I too shall wander alone, as in the case of a coward. Mandapāla spoke: I do not act in the world as you think; it is difficult for me to wander for the sake of offspring. Leaving the past and relying on the future, the foolish one who is dependent will be despised by the world as you wish. This indeed is the blazing fire, consuming the earth and the trees.

Page 826

The pain indeed arises in my heart, O enemy. Then, at that moment, the burning fire of grief took hold, and she hurried away like a mother seeking her son. She, with her skilled sons, weeping and distressed, wandered in the forest. The most untrustworthy of them, she repeatedly sought each of her sons, who were hidden away. Then, suddenly, even the slow-witted Mandapala approached, O Bhārata. And they, the sons, did not rejoice at all in this. They were again and again lamenting the one who was burning. They did not speak a word, whether it was true or false. Mandapala said: Which of your sons is the eldest, and who is next? Which is the middle son, and who is the youngest? Thus speaking, in distress, why do you not respond to me? I have performed the sacrifice, yet I do not attain peace from here. What work is there for the eldest son, or for the next, or for the middle one, or for the youngest, O ascetic?

Page 827

You, who previously abandoned me, go to that charming young woman with a lovely smile. Manda Pāra said: No woman is ever found elsewhere than in men. In this world, it must be so, except for the one with a husband. Even the virtuous Kalyāṇī, who is renowned in her own realm, is like Arundhatī, the best of sages. The hero, who is always engaged in the welfare of others with pure intentions, is known as the sage among the seven stars. With her reddish hue like smoke, she is neither the target nor the aim, appearing as if she is the cause. For the sake of offspring, you have come here, and so have I, leaving behind what is desired. There is no trust in a wife; the duty belongs to men in any case. A virtuous woman does not think of her husband when she has children. Then, those sons worshipped him properly, and he began to comfort them, the sons of his own.

Page 828

Mandapala said: "I have been instructed by you for your protection, and thus I have promised to set fire. Knowing the words of Agni and your understanding, I have not come here to fulfill your desires. Indeed, you should not be troubled, sons, regarding death; even the sage Hutaśa knows this. Then, having reassured the sons and taking Bhaya, Mandapala went to another land. The Lord, with the sharp sun, set ablaze the Khandava forest, granting fearlessness to the world along with Krishna. The waters of the lakes there, having been drunk by the fire, went away, fully satisfied. Then, from the sky, the Lord, the king of the gods, surrounded by the Maruts, spoke this to Partha and Madhava. To you both, I have done what is difficult; I am pleased, so choose your boons. Partha, however, chose the weapons from Shakra."

To capture that, he did HTS. When the pleased Lord Mahādeva will be, then I will give you all the sons of Pāṇḍu. I alone will know that time, O descendant of Kuru. With great penance, I will also give you those. You will capture all the Agneya and Vāyavya ones, as well as all my own, O Dhananjaya. Vasudeva also took eternal delight with Partha, and then Indra granted him that boon with joy. Having eaten the flesh and drunk the fat and blood, he spoke to the lord of the worlds with great joy. I am satisfied by you two, as you wish. I allow you, O hero, to go wherever you desire. Thus, being permitted by the great-souled fire, Vasudeva and the demon also did so. Then, having circumambulated, all three, O best of Bharata, sat together on the beautiful riverbank.
